Rule 46(b) expressly allows one **or multiple** series, and every ₹5–50 Cr manufacturer with a plant plus a depot, or separate export and domestic books, or a manual challan book at the gate, runs several. `series_code` is a first-class tracked field on the document, chosen at entry, never inferred.

*Mid-year cut-over was impossible.* A client going live on 1 October having issued invoices 1–842 in Tally had two options and both failed: set `current = 842` and the continuity auditor — a **blocking** precondition on IRN and GSTR-1 — fails forever on 842 documents that will never exist in the database; set `current = 0` and you restart the series mid-year, which is the audit finding the auditor exists to prevent. Stage 4's own "Tally backup Monday, live Friday" demo could not pass its own Stage 1 gate. Corrected: `start_ordinal` is the first ordinal this system issues, `migrated_from`/`migrated_to` **declare** the externally-issued range with a provenance note, and **the auditor asserts continuity only from `start_ordinal` forward**. GSTR-1 Table 13 reports the declared range and the issued range as separate rows for the cut-over period.

**Table 13 — Documents Issued.** PHASE4 §4.1 lists it and revision 1 never restated it, while designing a series table that could not feed it. Reporting is per `series_nature` per series: issued, cancelled, net, from-number, to-number. That is why `series_nature` is a column and why the four voucher types and Bill of Supply (§3.2) need their own series rather than borrowing the invoice series. Neither reference treats per-GSTIN isolation as first-class, and the **India FY reset token exists in none of the scanned repos** (PHASE6 §1.1) — net-new design, small, living once in `packages/domain/fiscal.ts` and consumed by the series, the period gates and the GST returns alike.

Postgres `SEQUENCE` is **rejected** — rollback burns the number and produces an unexplained gap in a GST series. Odoo's optimistic-retry-against-UNIQUE `sequence_mixin` is rejected as primary, along with its trick of inferring the format from the previous document's name (there is nothing to infer from: the FY token does not exist in either reference).

**Numbers are assigned at submit, never at draft creation.** Drafts display `DRAFT-<short uuid>`. Deleting a draft therefore cannot create a gap, and Frappe's entire `revert_series_if_last` mechanism and its bug surface need not exist. This is our deliberate improvement over both references.

**The lock is held until COMMIT — not for microseconds — and the honest fix is to shorten what follows it, not to misdescribe it.** Revision 2 claimed "~100µs" and it is wrong about Postgres: `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` holds the row lock until the transaction ends, so throughput per series is bounded by **commit latency**, including the audit write, the deferred balance triggers and the commit fsync — not by the duration of the UPDATE. *This still resolves the real conflict:* PHASE6 §1.1 recommends the counter + row lock; PHASE5 §2.6 measures that Frappe holds that lock from the *start* of the insert ladder through all validation, all child inserts and every `on_submit` GL posting, serialising every concurrent invoice behind the slowest one. Keeping the pessimistic counter but allocating **last** removes the validation and posting time from under the lock, which is the whole win:

```
BEGIN
  validate(doc)               -- no series lock held
  post(gl, sle)               -- no series lock held
  loop attempt in 1..6:
    SAVEPOINT sp_series       -- REQUIRED: NOWAIT raises lock_not_available,
    try:                      -- which aborts the transaction, not the statement
      SELECT … FOR UPDATE NOWAIT; UPDATE counter; break
    except lock_not_available:
      ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT sp_series; sleep(jitter 25/50/100/200/400ms)
  writeAudit()                -- chain-head lock taken here, same slot
COMMIT                        -- both locks released here
```

The `SAVEPOINT` is not a detail: revision 2's pseudocode retried inside a transaction that the first `lock_not_available` had already poisoned, so every retry would have failed with `25P02`. After six attempts the user gets a clean "please retry". **Throughput budget, stated so it can be tested:** one series sustains roughly `1 / commit_latency` submissions per second — at a 5 ms commit that is ~200/s, comfortable for 200 invoices a day and **not** comfortable for L11's opening-invoice import, which is why imports allocate through the migrator's own bulk path (a single locked range reservation per batch, recorded as `migrated_from/to`) rather than one round trip per row. Rollback un-consumes the number because the counter UPDATE rolls back with the transaction. Cancellation never returns one. The mechanism is **never reused for non-statutory documents**.

**Format validated at series-design time** (PHASE4 G7 / V8): `^[^\W_][A-Za-z0-9\-\/]{0,15}$` — ≤16 chars, alphanumeric first character, only `-` and `/` thereafter. Numeric-token width validated against projected annual volume so the series cannot overflow mid-year. Retrofitting this after customers have live series means renumbering live books, which is not a thing you can do.

**Continuity auditor — blocking on an *unacknowledged* gap, with a documented way out.** Locks prevent future gaps and never detect existing ones — restored backups, migrations and manual DB edits break sequences no lock protects (Odoo added `made_sequence_gap` for exactly this; Frappe has no equivalent). A nightly job asserts an unbroken run from `start_ordinal` to `current` per (registration, doc_type, series_code, FY).

Revision 1 made any detected gap a hard block on IRN and GSTR-1 with **no remediation path** — so a support-team restore silently converted into a tenant that could not invoice, and a factory that cannot dispatch is a worse outcome than an explained gap. Corrected: a gap raises a `series_gap` record; a **supervisor-role "gap acknowledged with reason"** action, audited and permanent, unblocks IRN and GSTR-1 while **permanently disclosing the gap in the GSTR-1 workbench and in the Table 13 output**. The blocker is the *unacknowledged* gap. Acknowledgement never deletes the record and never removes the disclosure.

Series is a **first-class tracked field on the document**, never inferred from the name by string arithmetic. Delivery Challan gets its own series, separate from internal stock-document naming — conflating an internal document name with a statutory serial makes amendment and cancellation semantics murky, and the reference does exactly that.

### 3.7 Tenancy — the one place the decision agents split

**Decision: schema-per-tenant on a shared PostgreSQL cluster, shard ceiling derived below. [BET]**

PHASE5 §6, PHASE6 §6 and two of the six decision agents recommend **database-per-tenant**. This is the least-settled decision in the plan and it is stated as a bet.

Shape: one **control-plane database** (tenants, users, subscriptions, licences, shard map, migration state), never joined to tenant data. One schema per tenant, `t_<slug>`, identical DDL everywhere. Tenant resolution is subdomain or JWT claim → shard + schema via the control plane; the request transaction sets `SET LOCAL search_path = t_x, public`. **Never from a client-supplied header** — PHASE5 §6.3 flags `X-Frappe-Site-Name` as a cross-tenant auth bypass one proxy misconfiguration away; any inbound `X-Tenant`-shaped header is stripped at the edge, with a test asserting it. On-prem is a one-tenant cluster with schema `t_main`, same code path, no branch.

*Why not row-level/RLS:* per-customer **export** (`pg_dump -n t_x` is one command and an Indian SME procurement asset — PHASE5 §6.2; **it is the database half only, and the export we actually promise is the dump plus the tenant's object-store prefix — §3.11, D18**); raw-SQL report blast radius (one tenant vs all); index and plan quality with a leading `tenant_id`; per-tenant VACUUM/ANALYZE and maintenance. *Honest correction:* revision 1 claimed per-customer **restore** as part of that asset. It is not. Restoring one schema into a live shared cluster is not one command, and PITR is cluster-wide — single-tenant point-in-time recovery is a genuine capability of database-per-tenant that we are giving up. The recovery story under this bet is: export is cheap and fast; restore-in-place is an engineered procedure (restore to a scratch cluster, `pg_dump -n`, load into a quarantined schema, swap) that must be **written and rehearsed before the first customer**, not improvised during an incident.

*Why schema- rather than database-per-tenant, overriding PHASE5 §6.4:* **the bet now rests on connection pooling alone.** PgBouncer pools per (database, user): 500 databases is 500 pool definitions, and while `min_pool_size = 0` means idle tenants hold no server connections, `max_db_connections` and `default_pool_size` are per-database, so the *bursty* case — 40 tenants active at 09:30 on the 10th — allocates 40 separate pools that cannot lend each other capacity, against one shard pool that can. Schema-per-tenant is one pool per shard with `search_path` swapped per transaction. Numbers to set and monitor from day one: `default_pool_size` 20–25 per shard, `max_db_connections` 100, `server_idle_timeout` 60s, transaction pooling mode, alarm on `cl_waiting`. If those numbers do not hold under a load test at shard capacity, the bet is wrong and we pay the orchestrator bill.

*Two arguments revision 1 made and this revision withdraws.* (i) **The SeaRM citation is struck.** SeaRM appears in `docs/erp-scout/` only in `CRM-HANDOFF.md`, a CRM consolidation memo; **no scout report examined its tenancy model**, and its schema-per-workspace design exists to support runtime per-workspace DDL — the metadata engine this plan explicitly rejects. Citing it was exactly the evidence drift this document's own §2 forbids. (ii) **Migration fan-out is no longer offered as evidence.** PHASE5 §6.3 measures Frappe's *implementation* — `for site in context.sites` under a bench-wide file lock, serial, forward-only, no canary — which is an argument against Frappe's migrator, not against the storage model. And we need the orchestrator either way (§7 Stage 1). Cross-tenant metering as one `UNION ALL` remains true and remains a minor convenience, not a reason.

*What we lose and how we bound it:* a `search_path` bug could theoretically address another schema. Mitigations — the tenant role is granted `USAGE` on **only its own schema** so a wrong `search_path` errors rather than leaks; `SET LOCAL` is applied inside the request transaction by middleware with no other caller; a test suite asserts cross-schema denial. That is a smaller and more testable risk surface than RLS's, and materially larger than DB-per-tenant's.

**Shard ceiling, derived rather than asserted.** Revision 1 asserted ≤100 tenants per cluster with no arithmetic; PHASE5 §6.3 recommends **shards of 25–50** on a table-count argument (~300 framework tables per Frappe site before any ERP layer; 500 tenants × ~800 tables = 400,000 tables degrades `table_open_cache`, file descriptors, catalogue memory and `information_schema`). Our per-tenant table count is **not Frappe's** — we have ~40 entities plus child tables plus partitions, so the honest figure is ~90–130 tables per tenant *plus one `audit_log` partition per month per tenant*, which is the term that actually grows. At 120 tables + 96 audit partitions after eight years, a tenant costs ~220 relations; 100 tenants is ~22,000 relations, which is comfortable, and the binding constraint is the audit partitions, not the entities. **Ceiling: 100 tenants per shard, re-derived from the real table count at the end of Stage 1 and again before tenant #50**, with cold-partition detachment (§3.5 property 7) as the release valve.

**Reversal condition:** if either of the first two design partners makes a dedicated database a contractual isolation term, switch to database-per-tenant and pay the orchestrator bill. Nothing else in the architecture changes. **This is a Stage 0 gate (§10 Q8), not a question to raise during sales conversations that happen later** — revision 1 said "ask in the first sales calls", which schedules the answer after Stage 1 has already written migrations against the chosen model.

`tenant_id`/`company_id` stays on every table's spine regardless of storage model — it is free, and the audit log and fleet reconciliation require it (PHASE4 §7.5 names the missing company stamp as permanently breaking multi-company reporting). Also from PHASE5 §6.5: copy the Redis client-side-invalidation design with its placeholder race guard; reject ambient mutable globals (`frappe.local`) in favour of `AsyncLocalStorage`; do not inherit the `scheduled_job_type.py` maintenance-offset bug.

### 3.8 Cross-cutting

- **Permissions:** role × entity × right, `if_owner`, plus a small fixed set of **NOT NULL** scoping columns (`company_id`, `registration_id`, `branch_id`, `warehouse_id`, `cost_center_id`) — NOT NULL by construction so the blank-means-allow footgun cannot exist. Compiled to **typed parameterised predicates**; no string SQL in the permission path ever (PHASE5 §7.7: Frappe splices hook-returned SQL verbatim — "any bug is a cross-tenant data leak, not a 500"). One predicate path used by API, list, report and export — Frappe has two and PHASE5 §2.7 verified they are non-identical. Odoo Bet 6 semantics adopted: AND-global / OR-group, enforced at the data layer, field ACLs applied *inside* query compilation so a hidden field cannot be binary-searched; the ACCESS-vs-CONSISTENCY split kept. Odoo's `safe_eval` domains rejected. Default inverted from Frappe: the permissioned call gets the short name; the bypass is `unsafeQuery({ reason })` — long, greppable, counted in CI.
- **Jobs:** BullMQ + Redis fed by a **transactional outbox in the tenant schema** (enqueue-after-commit that survives a Redis flush), dedup by namespaced job id, deadlock retry on serialization failure with backoff. Batches of ~1000 with a **savepoint per batch** and per-row savepoints on imports — on Postgres one bad row otherwise poisons the whole transaction (PHASE2 §5 and PHASE3 §4.7 agree exactly). Long replays are resumable and checkpointed from day one.
- **Migrations run only in the migrator process**, never sharing a transaction with business writes. PHASE5 §8.1 records Frappe's `MAX_WRITES_PER_TRANSACTION = 200_000` with `auto_commit_on_many_writes` **silently committing mid-bulk-operation** — destroying atomicity exactly during imports and year-end postings.
- **Webhooks:** config row + template + HMAC + DB-persisted retry log on `[5m, 30m, 2h, 5h, 10h, 10h]` with a sweeper, replayable by support.
- **Idempotency:** `submit` accepts an idempotency key stored in `request_idempotency`; a retried submit returns the original result rather than a second invoice.
- **Rule-version stamp** on every computed document and ledger row, so any historical number can be reproduced and defended. This is one mechanism serving two risks (statutory rule store; settings-that-change-posting-math).
- **Report access is not an exception to the permission path.** §3.8's "one predicate path used by API, list, report and export" and "no string SQL in the permission path ever" cannot both be true if reports are arbitrary TS writing arbitrary recursive CTEs. Resolution: a report's only ledger access is `ctx.ledgerQuery()` / `ctx.entityQuery(entity)`, builders that **pre-apply the compiled scoping predicate** and compose further; `unsafeQuery({ reason })` is the sole escape and is counted in CI exactly as promised. Full contract in §3.10.
- **Not built:** generic form renderer for core screens (hand-built React; the generic renderer is a fallback for extension fields and long-tail masters only), own child-table grid (buy AG Grid — Frappe's has **zero paste handlers**, grep-verified, so no range-select and no fill-down), visual print builder (11,021 LOC of Vue, still beta, and "nobody lays out their own GST invoice"), server-script sandbox, stored-SQL query reports, multi-DB support, EAV settings, dynamic-link polymorphism, own realtime layer (polling for year one), own APM (OpenTelemetry, `trace_id` on every error record).

### 3.9 The statutory rule store

W1 is the plan's most defensible wedge, D9 an unretrofittable day-one decision, and L10 a Wave 0 invariant — and revision 1 specified the *policy* and never the *thing*: no table, no key structure, no resolution function, no provenance columns despite R9 declaring "a rule with no provenance is a defect", and a `gl_entry.rule_version_id` comment pointing at a section that does not exist. Stage 1 could not have started. The schema:

```sql
create table statutory_rule (
  id uuid primary key,
  domain text not null,          -- gst | tds | tcs | pf | esi | pt | income_tax | ewb | codes
  key text not null,             -- 'gst.rate.hsn.7318' | 'pt.slab.MH' | 'tds.194Q'
  jurisdiction text not null,    -- 'IN' | 'IN-27' | 'IN-DD'
  effective_from date not null, effective_to date,     -- null = open
  payload jsonb not null,        -- the rule's own shape, validated per domain
  notification_ref text not null,      -- 'N-78/2020-CT' — provenance is NOT NULL
  source_url text, verified_by text not null, verified_on date not null,
  superseded_by uuid references statutory_rule(id),
  exclude using gist (key with =, jurisdiction with =,
                      daterange(effective_from, effective_to) with &&)
);

create table ruleset_version (               -- a sealed snapshot of the whole store
  id uuid primary key, sealed_at timestamptz not null,
  digest bytea not null, signed_by text not null, notes text
);
create table ruleset_member (ruleset_id uuid, rule_id uuid, primary key (ruleset_id, rule_id));
```

**Documents stamp the ruleset, not a rule.** One GL row from a GST invoice is produced by a rate rule *plus* a place-of-supply rule *plus* a cess formula *plus* a rounding rule; one uuid cannot stamp four. `ruleset_version_id` on the document and on every ledger row identifies the sealed snapshot; individual rules are then recovered by `(ruleset, key, posting_date)`, which reproduces any historical number exactly.

**Resolution has one signature and no fallback:** `resolve(key, jurisdiction, on: Date, ruleset?) → Rule` — **throws** `MissingStatutoryRule` if nothing is in force. The GiST exclusion constraint makes overlapping validity a *database* error, so "no gaps, no overlaps" is enforced rather than asserted. Historical rows are never mutated; a change is a new row plus `superseded_by`.

**Why this table exists rather than a constants file — the evidence, which until revision 5 was an argument from principle.** On **22 September 2025 the entire four-tier GST rate structure (5 / 12 / 18 / 28) was replaced by a two-rate structure — 5% Merit and 18% Standard — plus a 40% de-merit rate**, in a single GST Council decision. Verbatim from the Council's own press release: *"Rationalisation of the current 4-tiered tax rate structure into a citizen-friendly 'Simple Tax' - a 2 rate structure with a Standard Rate of 18% and a Merit Rate of 5%; a special de-merit rate of 40%."* **[PHASE8 PRIMARY]** — [gstcouncil.gov.in, 56th Council meeting press release]. This is not a threshold moving by one notch; it is the whole rate map changing at once. **Any product holding rates as constants needed a code release and a customer-by-customer upgrade. Any product holding them as effective-dated rows needed a data update.** It is the single strongest justification for D9 and §4.2's effective-dated applicability decision anywhere in the evidence base, and it is why the rule store is a day-one decision rather than a v1.1 refactor.

**Direct consequence for the sourcing line item below: any rate table seeded from pre-September-2025 material is wrong today.** The rate master must be re-sourced against the 22 Sep 2025 mapping **including its transitional rules**, and §5.5's GST-rate question changes shape accordingly — from "what are the rates" to "give us the 22 Sep 2025 mapping and the transition treatment for documents straddling it". A rate row is still `(key, jurisdiction, effective_from)`; what changed is that the seed data everyone has lying around is now historical.

**Sourcing the data is a Stage 0 cost line, not a CA favour — and revision 1 had neither.** L10 makes a missing rule an error, so the system cannot post a single invoice until the store is populated, and revision 1 assigned all 70 questions to "a CA on retainer" while §5.5 says of the GST rate schedule "treat neither as current". A CA is an advisor, not a data vendor. Nobody hands you a machine-readable HSN→rate table with effective dates for ~12,000 codes across the 2017/2022/2025 transitions; that is a licensed dataset, and PHASE6 notes the only HSN master in any reference is inside GPL `india-compliance`, which our licensing position forbids. PT slabs across N states, TDS/TCS section × deductee × threshold matrices and PF/ESI parameters span EPFO, ESIC, state commercial-tax departments and CBDT — **four professional competencies, not one CA.**

> **Stage 0 line item: statutory data sourcing.** Either licence an HSN/rate master with effective dates, or budget the person-months to build and maintain one. Name the **annual rupee cost of the compliance function** — CA retainer + data licence + notification watch + the internal owner — before §8.4 answer 10 is used in a sales call, because that cost *is* the answer to "why pay when ERPNext is free". Until that number exists, **W1 is an unpriced operating liability, not a wedge** (§8.2).

**The first rows that can actually be seeded, and what is still missing.** Revision 4 had zero primary-sourced payroll numbers; §5.5's governing rule ("no number reaches the repository without a cited notification and an effective date") meant the store shipped empty. Phase 8 read four of them off the issuing authority's own page, so these are seed rows the CA **confirms** rather than researches — each still a dated row with `notification_ref`, `source_url` and `verified_by` populated per this schema, and each still CA-signed before it ships:

| Rule key | Value | Effective | Label |
|---|---|---|---|
| `esi.rate.employee` / `esi.rate.employer` | **0.75% / 3.25%** of wages | w.e.f. **01.07.2019** | **[PHASE8 PRIMARY]** esic.gov.in/contribution |
| `esi.exemption.daily_wage` | employee contribution waived at daily average wage **≤ ₹176** (employer still pays) | same | **[PHASE8 PRIMARY]** |
| `esi.ceiling.wage` | **₹21,000/month; ₹25,000 for Persons with Disability** | w.e.f. **01.01.2017** | **[PHASE8 PRIMARY]** esic.gov.in/coverage |
| `epf.ceiling.wage` | **₹15,000/month — unchanged** | Gazette **S.O. 2702(E), 29 May 2026** | **[PHASE8 PRIMARY]** egazette.gov.in. **Settles a live contradiction: multiple 2026 blogs claim a hike to ₹21,000 or ₹25,000. The Gazette says ₹15,000. Do not seed the blog number.** |
| `pt.slab.MH` | men — nil ≤ ₹7,500/mo; ₹175/mo for ₹7,500–10,000; ₹2,500/annum (₹200/mo, **₹300 in February**) above ₹10,000. Women — nil ≤ ₹25,000/mo, same structure above | **1 Apr 2023** regime | **[PHASE8 PRIMARY]** mahagst.gov.in PT rate schedule |

**The Maharashtra PT schedule is also the cleanest proof this table has to exist at all: the same PDF carries six distinct rate regimes since 2006, each with its own effective-date window.** PT is an effective-dated table, demonstrably, not a constant.

**Still open and explicitly not seeded:** EPF/EPS **contribution percentages** (epfindia.gov.in refused connection at DNS/TCP level on every method, both schemes, across a whole research session — a tooling failure, not an absence), EDLI, admin charges, and **every non-Maharashtra PT schedule**, which is per-cluster and therefore gated on §10 Q10. L10 still makes a missing rule an error, so a customer outside Maharashtra cannot run payroll until their state's rows exist — that is the intended behaviour and it is a Stage 0 scoping input, not a bug.

**And a data feed needs a delivery channel, which revision 2 never described.** For hosted tenants a new `ruleset_version` is published by the control plane and picked up on the next resolve. For **on-prem** — which §4.1F promises and §3.11 sizes — a ruleset is a **signed bundle file**: a serialised set of `statutory_rule` rows plus the `ruleset_version` digest and signature, imported by the migrator, which verifies the signature against a pinned public key before writing a single row and refuses a bundle whose digest does not match. Bundles are cumulative and ordered, so a customer who lags three releases applies three bundles in sequence. **Version skew is a stated support window, not a hope:** an on-prem build accepts bundles issued within N releases of its own version and refuses older ones loudly, because a rate row whose payload shape the running code cannot parse is worse than a missing rule — a missing rule throws by L10, a misparsed one computes.

### 3.10 Tax data model and the report contract

**Tax schema.** T1–T15 are fifteen rules of algorithm over a data model revision 1 never drew; §3.2 listed "Tax Template" as a master with no shape. Repartition alone is a real model — ordered invoice-list and refund-list of identical length, factor, account, tag, per tax — and it is the mechanism for both RCM and ineligible ITC, i.e. two N-rated compliance gates. Tables required **before Stage 2**: `tax_category`, `tax_template`, `tax_template_row` (charge type, rate, sequence, `include_base_amount`, `price_include`, account, tax character), `tax_repartition_line` (template_row, kind ∈ {invoice, refund}, factor, account, tag, ordinal), `item_tax_template` + `item_tax_template_row` with `valid_from` for T11's ordering.

**Per-line stored tax result is a day-one decision, not a view.** N11/T6 require the GSTR section stamped at post time, so `document_line_tax(document_line_id, tax_character, rate, taxable_value, tax_amount, gstr_section, gst_treatment, ruleset_version_id)` is a stored table. A computed view would reclassify history on every code change — which is precisely the failure PHASE4 §9.3 calls "the single most common source of filing mismatches".

**Tax character enum, corrected: `{igst, cgst, sgst, utgst, cess, cess_non_advol}`.** UTGST appears nowhere in revision 1, nowhere in PHASE4 and nowhere in the account model — T4 knew only CGST/SGST/IGST and asserted "intra-state → CGST + SGST at exactly half". An intra-UT supply in a Union Territory without a legislature attracts CGST+UTGST, and Daman/Silvassa alone is a dense SME manufacturing belt squarely inside the ICP. The invoice would post, print, file and put the wrong head on the return — the #1 entry in §6.1's own table. **[VERIFY] q79:** the list of UT codes without a legislature becomes a rule-store table; SGST vs UTGST derives from the destination state code against it. Validations 1–3 in T4 and the GSTR-1/3B bucketing extend accordingly. Cheap now; a ledger and return-mapping migration later.

**Report contract.** `execute(filters, ctx) → { columns, rows, totals }`, an on-disk TS module, no stored SQL. `ctx` exposes `ledgerQuery()` / `entityQuery(entity)` — Drizzle builders with the compiled scoping predicate already applied and composable further — plus `accountTree()` (one recursive CTE, cached per request), `period(from, to)` and `fx()`. There is no raw connection on `ctx`. Opening/period/closing and comparative columns are produced by a shared `balanceBands()` helper so every financial statement agrees by construction; `root_type` mapping, cost-centre filtering, drill-down to voucher, pagination and XLSX export are engine features, not per-report code. **Trial Balance and P&L are written in Stage 2 as the two reference implementations**, not in Stage 4 — the CA opens them first and they are how we find out whether the engine is right.

### 3.11 On-prem, personal data, and the module boundary

**On-prem topology, stated because §4.1F promises a 4 GB box.** The stack is Bun + NestJS + Next SSR + Postgres 17 + Redis + a BullMQ worker + Chromium for PDF, and a headless Chromium contends with Postgres for that 4 GB every time someone prints an invoice. The self-host build therefore: renders PDF in a **separate, optional, rate-limited process** with a documented degradation (queue the print, or fall back to a server-side HTML→PDF library at lower fidelity); **proxies all GSP calls through our cloud**, which is also where D7's stable allow-listed egress IP lives, so the factory box needs no fixed IP; and **queues the daily signed digest locally and ships it when connectivity returns**, with the hard-fail alarm from §3.5. Redis and the worker run in-process on the small profile.

**Licensing and entitlement.** A commercial, closed-source product shipped on-premise needs a licence key, an entitlement check and a tamper story, and revision 1 had none anywhere. It is a schema and a boot-path decision, not a feature: signed licence blob (tenant, edition, seat count, expiry, feature flags) validated at boot and daily, a grace window that degrades to read-only rather than dying mid-month-end, and entitlement state in the control plane for hosted tenants. Added to §9.1 as **D15**.

**Personal data and the DPDP Act 2023.** The product ingests biometric check-in data and full payroll PII, and §3.5 copies full before/after row snapshots into a table with a retention lock that "permanently disables any purge feature" and a rule that rows are "never deleted". The DPDP Act creates erasure and purpose-limitation obligations over exactly that data. Reconciliation, as **D14** in §9.1:

- **Classify every entity** as `statutory_record` or `personal_data` at schema-definition time; the classification is a column on the registry and a lint gate on new tables.
- Statutory records keep the immutable trail unchanged.
- Personal data is **crypto-shredded**: per-subject key, PII encrypted at rest under it, audit rows store ciphertext, and erasure = destroying the key. **The hash chain survives erasure**, because the ciphertext bytes it hashed are still there.
- Encryption at rest for the tenant cluster and the cold store; India-region residency stamped per §3.5.
- **[VERIFY] q80:** which payroll and biometric fields are erasable versus retained under labour-law record-keeping, and the retention period for each.

**Identity, impersonation, keys and files — the five things the audit schema references and revision 2 never defined.** `audit_log` records `actor_user_id`, `actor_roles`, `impersonated_by`, `api_key_id` and `session_id`, and none of them had a source anywhere in the document. Fixed as decisions, deliberately boring, because §9.3 R14's own rule is to buy this layer:

- **Authentication is bought, not built** — an off-the-shelf OIDC provider, sessions as short-lived tokens with refresh, and **MFA mandatory for any role that can move a period gate, mark a period filed, acknowledge a series gap or bypass a validation**. Those are exactly the actions a Rule 11(g) reader will ask who performed.
- **Impersonation is a first-class, consented, audited protocol**, not a support back door: a support user assumes a tenant identity only with a named reason, only for a bounded window, with `impersonated_by` set on every resulting audit row and a notification to the tenant's admin. A support tool that writes rows indistinguishable from the customer's own is a tamper finding.
- **API keys** are per tenant, per integration, scoped to the same compiled predicate path as a human session — never a bypass — revocable, and stamped on every audit row they cause.
- **Object storage is per tenant and is part of the export story.** Signed IRP JSON, QR images, GRN and gate-pass photos, weighbridge display captures, generated PDFs and import source files all have to survive for the retention period, and `pg_dump -n t_x` — the per-customer export §3.7 sells as a procurement asset — **does not contain them**. Blobs live under a tenant-scoped prefix with the same schema-boundary discipline, and "export" means the dump *plus* the object prefix, produced by one command. Saying otherwise oversells the asset.
- **Payroll and biometric PII** is the `personal_data` class below: encrypted per subject, read-audited, and never rendered in a report a non-payroll role can open.

**The module boundary.** §9.3 R14 endorses "localisation as a plugin from day one" in a single trailing sentence, and §3 never defined what a module is — which is why `vertical_app` was deleted from the overlay's layer enum in §3.2. Two honest options, and v1 takes the second: define the contract now (registration surface for entities/posting rules/reports/rule-store rows, version pinning, per-tenant install table, migration ownership), or **say plainly that v1 is one monolith for one country** and that a second country is a v2 project with its own design. v1 is a monolith. The only concession we make to a future boundary is free: statutory logic lives in `packages/domain` behind named exports rather than being inlined into controllers, and every rule reads from §3.9 rather than from a constant. Revisit when Q2 says partners matter (§10).

**Sizing the small profile, because §4.1F promises a number.** The on-prem box carries the whole schema plus an `audit_log` that stores full before/after JSONB for every write including child rows. Stage 1 therefore produces a **measured** growth figure — audit bytes per 1,000 documents of each major type — and a sizing table derived from it, not an assurance. The on-prem retention policy is the same rule-store row as the hosted one, but the *actor* is different: the self-host build ships a scheduled `detach + archive` job the customer can run to a local disk or an object store, and the alarm when it has not run. A 4 GB box with an unbounded audit table is a 4 GB box for eleven months.

### 3.12 Correctness oracle, testing, and the clean-room protocol

Revision 2's only test artefact was one red CI acceptance suite, in a document that also predicts its own defect list "will look like PHASE2 §6's did in 2014, minus fifteen years of production traffic to find them in". That is a forecast with no counter-measure. And §2 states the clean-room position in a single sentence while §6 transcribes exact formulas, exact key tuples and exact tolerance constants out of GPL source. For a closed-source commercial product these are the two risks that end the company rather than delay it, and neither had a page.

**The oracle: differential testing against running references.** ERPNext and Odoo are GPL — we may not copy their code, and nothing stops us **observing their outputs**. A scenario corpus (a tax breakup across the four heads, a valuation sequence of receipts/issues/returns/landed cost, an ageing walk over instalments and credit notes, a BOM explosion, an MRP netting run) is replayed against a Dockerised ERPNext and Odoo and against ours, and the numbers are diffed. This is the single highest-leverage correctness lever a clean-room team has, it is legal, and it is cheap. **The boundary is written down and enforced:** black-box inputs and outputs only; implementers do not open the reference repositories; the harness lives in its own package with its own review rule.

Around it: **golden-fixture suites per pure core** (tax, valuation, ageing, interval algebra, MRP netting — all of them dependency-free by §9.3's own habit, therefore all trivially testable); **property tests** for the rounding and distribution invariants, which are stated as identities in T3 and are exactly what property testing is for; and the red CI acceptance suite of §7, which stays.

**The clean-room protocol, operationalised.** Asserting "clean-room only" is not a control. The control is: **spec authors** read the GPL sources and write behaviour specs in our own words; **implementers** never open those repositories and work only from the specs; every rule in §6 carries a spec-document id so provenance is traceable per rule; the MIT-framework file-by-file provenance review (§2) and the `ATTRIBUTIONS.md` are completed and **reviewed by counsel before the first customer**, budgeted like the CA retainer; and there is a stated policy for engineers who have previously worked on ERPNext or Odoo — they are spec authors, not implementers, on any surface they have seen. **[Cost line, unpriced:] the legal review.** It belongs next to the CA retainer and the rate-master licence in Stage 0's financial model.

### 3.13 The UI workstream

Forty-plus hand-built screens by revision 2's count, 70–90 by the second review's, a gate tablet, a weighbridge station, a job card with a running timer and a shop-floor queue — and §8.1's whole structural argument is that **these are the screen families ERPNext's generic engine cannot render**. That makes the UI the place the product wins or loses, and revision 2 gave it no stack decision, no headcount and no schedule. Named here so the estimate in §7.6 can carry it:

- **Its own workstream and its own headcount**, distinct from the engineers in §7.6's placeholder — at least one designer and two front-end engineers, and they are in the hiring plan (Q13/Q16).
- **Stack decisions made once:** one form/state library, one validation round-trip shape driven by the compiled `depends_on`/`fetch_from` DSL, AG Grid for every child table (§3.8), one design system, and **keyboard-first data entry** as an explicit requirement — the buyer is an accountant coming off Tally, where everything is keyboard, and a mouse-first form is the first thing they will reject.
- **Who the simple screens are for, which revision 4 could not say.** The user who needs simplicity is the **accounts operator and the shop-floor hand, not the owner**. The Indian "Tally Operator" is a distinct, commonly-advertised job category hired at a **12th-pass bar** **[PHASE8 SINGLE-SOURCE — job-board category listings; the posting-count figure originally attached to this is withdrawn as unverifiable]**, while owners in this segment are **81–85% graduate-educated** **[PHASE8 VERIFIED — ICRIER Annual Survey of MSMEs 2025, n=2,365 Udyam-registered manufacturers; large caveat, the sample is half e-commerce-integrated by design and therefore skews more digital and more English-comfortable than the Tally-using job-work segment, so this is a direction, not a rate]**. Consequence for the design: owner-facing reports and dashboards may assume English and sophistication; **gate, weighbridge, job-card and voucher-entry screens may not.**
- **If a vernacular target is chosen, it is code-mixed Hinglish, not pure Hindi.** myBillBook ships Hinglish as its own named UI language distinct from both Hindi and English, and the entire India voice-billing category treats it as first-class **[PHASE8 VERIFIED]**. The counterweight, and it is the more informative half: **TallyPrime's own product interface is English-only** outside Arabic and Bangla export builds — vernacular exists only for *data entry* and *printed output* **[PHASE8 VERIFIED — help.tallysolutions.com]**. The market leader vernacularised the customer-facing output and not the operator's screen. That is a deliberate incumbent choice to interrogate in Stage 0, **not to copy blindly and not to invert blindly**; nothing in either research pass shows a manufacturing SME asking for a vernacular UI. v1 ships English with the string layer externalised so Hinglish is a data change, and no vernacular claim is made in a wedge.
- **Connectivity loss at the capture points, which is the one genuinely hard UI problem here.** The gate, the weighbridge and the shop floor lose the network. Those three screens queue locally, stamp the **idempotency key** §3.8 already specifies, and reconcile on reconnect; the mechanism exists and had no client story. Everything else stays online-only, and **polling rather than websockets remains correct** — a job-card timer is local state that posts on stop, not a live subscription, so the second review's objection on that point is noted and refused.

---

## 4. THE MVP CUT LINE

The cut line is meaningless without the target. **In:** discrete and light-process manufacturers, ₹5–50 Cr, 20–150 employees, 1–3 GSTINs, job-work-heavy. **Explicitly out:** continuous/batch chemical process, multi-country, export-first, >250 employees, retail.

**Why this band rather than "every business" — the justification, which revisions 1–3 argued from intuition and can now argue from notified thresholds.** ₹5–50 Cr is not an invented range: it straddles the top of Micro and the middle of Small under the revised MSME classification (Micro ≤ ₹2.5 Cr investment / ≤ ₹10 Cr turnover; Small ≤ ₹25 Cr / ≤ ₹100 Cr). *Provenance corrected in revision 5: a second primary source — [PIB PRID 2098389] — carries the threshold table and the Finance Minister's "2.5 and 2 times" quote **[PHASE8 PRIMARY]**, but **that release states no effective date**. The 1 April 2025 commencement rests on a secondary citation of S.O. 1364(E) dated 21 March 2025 and has **not** been re-verified against the gazette — so the bands are primary and the commencement date is **[VERIFY] q93**, which is exactly the distinction revision 4 flattened.* More usefully, it is the band where **statutory obligation peaks relative to in-house capability**: e-invoicing is mandatory from **₹5 Cr** **[PHASE7 VERIFIED]**, ITC-04 moves from annual to **half-yearly above ₹5 Cr** **[PHASE7 VERIFIED]**, and the **30-day IRP upload window** bites from **₹10 Cr** **[PHASE7 VERIFIED]** — while the finance function is still one part-time accountant plus an external CA. Below ₹5 Cr a billing app suffices; above ~₹100 Cr there is a finance team buying SAP or Oracle. And the compliance surface is **segment-shaped, not universal** — retail needs POS and B2C dynamic QR, services need place-of-supply rules for services, process chemicals need co-product yield allocation, exporters need RoDTEP/drawback/EPCG. "Every business" is not one product with more screens; it is N compliance regimes, and §4.2 cuts every one of them deliberately. *(PHASE7 §4.2; all thresholds remain **[VERIFY]** under §5's standing caveat.)*

**What forecloses widening is not the code — it is the channel.** §4.2's applicability decision makes crossing a turnover threshold a data change, so widening up- and down-market inside manufacturing is nearly free; process manufacturing, serial-level valuation and retail/POS are expensive and knowingly deferred. But if the first ten customers are one sub-vertical in one cluster, the eleventh sale is that sub-vertical, because that is who the reference calls reach — widening then costs a new channel, not a new module. **No evidence addresses this and §10 Q2 is untouched.** So: narrow *further* for the first ten as an explicit go-to-market choice rather than a product limit, and record which cluster, because §10 Q10 (Professional Tax states) depends on it. *(PHASE7 §4.3–4.4)*

**Three clusters with real unit-level survey data behind them — usable for *shape*, never for *size*.** SAMEEEKSHA, a government–industry energy-efficiency network, publishes surveyed cluster profiles no other source in this scout reached **[PHASE8 VERIFIED]**: **Faridabad** — 12,015 MSME units, **two-thirds in auto parts, sheet metal and fabrication** (undated on the page; methodology implies multi-year-old data); **Rajkot** — 160 auto-component units (80 micro / 65 small / 15 medium), **explicitly 2017-18**; **Coimbatore** — 535 foundries, ~600,000 t castings/year, **explicitly 2015-16**. Separately, SIDBI–TransUnion CIBIL's *MSME Pulse* (June 2025) — a credit bureau with no stake in the ERP market — reports the **₹10–50 Cr credit-exposure band** as the second-largest share of a ₹35.2 lakh crore commercial portfolio with the **lowest balance-level delinquency of any segment**, notes that *"as manufacturing continues to dominate this segment…"*, and ranks state portfolio share **Maharashtra, Gujarat, Tamil Nadu** with **Gujarat leading growth at 16% CAGR and 0.8% delinquency** **[PHASE8 VERIFIED — industry-body hard data; the per-state percentages originally reported alongside this could not be tied to the chart and are withdrawn, order only]**. **Two disciplines attached:** credit *exposure* is not turnover and the two must not be conflated; and every cluster figure above is 8–10 years old with no refresh in existence, so it may seed a target list and may not seed a market size. Use it to choose *where* the first ten come from — which is what §10 Q10 needs — and nothing else.

### 4.1 IN

**A. Foundation — unretrofittable, which is the whole reason it is in v1**

| Capability | Why it cannot be cut |
|---|---|
| Docstatus lifecycle; immutability after submit enforced by DB trigger; cancel-as-reversal | PHASE5 §8.2 calls it a day-one core-model decision; GST invoices, e-way bills and SLEs all depend on it. Retrofitting means rewriting every write path. |
| Append-only audit log with field-level before/after deltas **including child rows**, grouped by `event_id`, tenant-stamped, **always on with no enable flag**, UPDATE/DELETE revoked at DB role level, **the migrator writing through the same chokepoint**; per-row chain computed inline (§3.5) | MCA Rule 11(g) (PHASE4 §7). PHASE4 §9.1: "This cannot be added later." A log a tenant can switch off, or that a bulk import bypasses, does not answer the clause's "operated throughout the year for all transactions" limb |
| **Statutory-artifact side tables** (`einvoice_artifact`, `ewb_artifact`) with append-only lifecycle, and the posted-vs-issuable split | §3.3. Without them the immutability trigger and the async government call are mutually exclusive, and nothing stops an unIRN'd invoice leaving the gate |
| **Entity classification** `statutory_record` vs `personal_data`, with crypto-shredding for the latter | §3.11 / D14. Retrofitting erasability into an immutable log is a re-model |
| Statutory numbering keyed (GSTIN, doctype, FY), `FOR UPDATE NOWAIT` allocated last, ≤16 chars, cancelled numbers retained, **continuity auditor blocking IRN and GSTR-1** | PHASE6 §1.1 blocker #1. A gapped series is a filing failure and renumbering live books is impossible. |
| Effective-dated statutory rule store; **a missing rule errors, never computes zero**; rule-version stamp on every computed document | PHASE4 §9.4 + §8 gap #5 ("the references' *biggest* architectural failure"). Simultaneously the correctness requirement and the commercial moat. |
| Typed models on Postgres, real FKs, surrogate PKs, spine columns, DDL only in the migrator | PHASE5 §8.1. Kills the mid-bulk silent-commit and SQL-rewriting bug classes outright. |
| One permission predicate path over NOT NULL scoping columns | PHASE5 §8.2 #5. Two paths = a data leak. |
| Job queue with enqueue-after-commit; async government-API service with query-back idempotency and three-class error triage | PHASE4 §9.5. IRP/NIC downtime is routine; a synchronous IRN call inside a request handler loses invoices. |
| **A minimal notification service** — rules table, email/SMS/WhatsApp channel adapters, dedup, delivery log | §4.2 cuts the *workflow and approval* engine and revision 2's row also cut "notifications", while eight v1 features need one: §143 T-90/T-30/T-7 escalation, EWB expiry, A8 and A9 ageing alerts, the signed daily digest mailed to the CA, GSP retry and quota alarms, §3.4's divergence alarms, and P7's scheduled human task. Three days of work that eight features silently depend on; see §4.3 |
| `ext jsonb` + `field_extension` + generic CRUD | PHASE5 verdict, **narrowed**. A column is what you cannot retrofit. `property_override` + the 91-property allowlist + the inspector move to a Stage 2 slot gated on Q9 (§3.2) — building them in Stage 1 contradicted §10 Q9's own instruction to answer the demand question first. |

**B. Money core**

| Capability | Why it cannot be cut |
|---|---|
| GL with a single posting chokepoint (period gates, cost-centre allocation, merge, round-off, balance) | PHASE2 §1: the one design that stops each voucher type inventing its own posting rules. Free to adopt now, ruinous to retrofit. |
| AR/AP as an append-only signed subledger; outstanding **recomputed**, never a mutated counter | PHASE2 §2. Every "why is this customer's balance wrong" ticket dies here. |
| Sales/purchase invoice, credit/debit note, payment entry with allocation, journal entry | The document set the business runs on. |
| Tax engine: per-line rounding, inclusive back-solve, tax character stored as data, one `is_inter_state()` predicate | PHASE4 §9.3. Deriving GST character from tags (Odoo) means editing a tag silently changes a tax's identity. |
| Cost centre as a NOT NULL dimension on every GL row — **one analytic axis, not N** | PHASE6 §3 gap 5: a manufacturing P&L is read per cost centre. One axis as a real column; no runtime custom-field dimensions. |
| Trial Balance, P&L, Balance Sheet, GL, Aged AR/AP, cost-centre P&L — on the §3.10 report contract, TB and P&L written in Stage 2 | The CA is the gatekeeper and these are what he opens first. Note PHASE3 §5: Odoo Community ships report definitions with **no evaluator**, so we must budget the engine ourselves. |
| **Realised** FX gain/loss on settlement | Export/SEZ is in v1 (§5.3). An invoice raised at ₹83/USD and settled at ₹85 does not balance in INR without a realised-FX line, and §3.4's deferred constraint trigger refuses the COMMIT — the alternative is an unpostable receipt or a silent plug. This is ~1 rule and 1 account, not the revaluation subsystem, which stays out. |
| Period control: soft accounting period + hard frozen-till that Administrator cannot bypass + GSTR-1-filed lock | Without it, back-dating destroys filed periods. |

**C. The compliance gate** — PHASE4 §1.1 G1–G14, summarised; full detail in §5 below.

GSTIN capture and structural validation · Place of Supply as a stored first-class field · intra/inter-state head determination with blocking validation · named GST accounts per company per type · HSN/SAC digit enforcement + UQC with `OTH` fallback · per-line `gst_treatment`, taxable value, per-tax rate **and** amount, **GSTR section stamped at post time** · RCM with balanced mirrored rows and self-invoice numbering · blocking validations V1–V12 · **e-invoice (IRN) and e-way bill including on Delivery Note, Delivery Challan, Stock Entry and subcontracting challan** · GSTR-1 + 3B preparation · **GSTR-2B download on every available refresh, feeding a standing purchase-mismatch worklist with ageing — not a month-end reconciliation screen.** 2B is generated on the **14th** and GSTR-3B is due on the **20th**: a **six-day window** to reconcile every purchase invoice **[PHASE7 VERIFIED, 2 sources]**. **Revision 5 upgrades this from a UX preference to a data-model requirement, because IMS changed what 2B *is*.** Since the Invoice Management System went live (1 Oct 2024, actionable from 14 Oct 2024), GSTN's own FAQ states that the 2B generated on the 14th is explicitly a **draft** that can be **recomputed** until GSTR-3B is filed, and that every inward record carries a recipient action — **Accept / Reject / Pending** — with the default: *"By default all the records will flow into 'No Action' category and records with 'No Action' will be deemed accepted at the time of GSTR-2B generation."* **[PHASE8 PRIMARY — GSTN IMS FAQ, tutorial.gst.gov.in]**. Consequences, and they are structural: the purchase side is a **standing per-invoice action worklist whose state feeds a recomputable 2B**, not a monthly pull plus a mismatch report; `2b_action ∈ {accept, reject, pending, no_action}` with its own audited transition history is a **column on the inward-record table from migration #1**; and **a design that models 2B as an immutable monthly snapshot is wrong at the data-model level, not merely at the UX level** — recomputation would silently invalidate a reconciliation already reviewed and signed off. Inaction is not neutral: "No Action" is *deemed accepted*, so a worklist that lets records age quietly claims ITC on the customer's behalf. **[VERIFY] q95.** *Cadence is gated on §10 Q4:* if 2B download inherits the Returns API's OTP-bound session (§5.4), "continuous" degrades to "refreshed on every attended pull" and the worklist is the same object either way — **Q4 is answered before this is built, not after** · **IRN reporting time limit as a blocking validation (T16):** above the applicable turnover threshold, an invoice whose document date is older than N days cannot be submitted for IRN generation. N = **30 days** and the threshold **₹10 Cr**, effective **1 April 2025** **[PHASE7 VERIFIED, single source]** — both rule-store rows with applicability dates, never constants; **[VERIFY] q91** · multi-GSTIN scoping on every query from migration #1 · ineligible-ITC routing into expense or valuation · export/SEZ regimes + LUT validity tracking · MSME/Udyam classification → **15-day (no written agreement) / 45-day (with agreement)** due date, **micro and small enterprises only** → 43B(h) disallowance report (**[VERIFY]**, A8 — revision 2's one-line summary here flattened both the branch and the scope that A8 itself gets right). **The classification thresholds the whole feature depends on are rule-store rows dated to the 1 April 2025 revision** — S.O. 1364(E), 21 March 2025: Micro ≤ ₹2.5 Cr investment / ≤ ₹10 Cr turnover; Small ≤ ₹25 Cr / ≤ ₹100 Cr; Medium ≤ ₹125 Cr / ≤ ₹500 Cr **[PHASE7 VERIFIED]**, **[VERIFY] q93**. Seeding pre-2025 values makes the 15/45-day due date and the 43B(h) disallowance wrong for every supplier near a boundary — silent when wrong, and the whole feature is a silent-wrong-number feature.

**D. Factory**

| Capability | Why it cannot be cut |
|---|---|
| Item, UoM+UQC, warehouse, **batch** (not serial), append-only stock ledger, COGS as value delta | The spine of everything below (PHASE2 §1). |
| **Moving-average valuation only** at the `(item, location)` key (D16), forward-replay repost inside the open period, under the D13 lock | PHASE2 Q1: back-dating is unavoidable (a GRN dated three days ago is normal). MA makes forward replay tractable. *"And it is Tally's default" is struck here as it was in §3.4 — the claim has no source in the scout; **[VERIFY] q74**.* |
| PO → GRN → PI with three-way match tolerance; SO → DN → SI; landed cost. **Bill of Entry capitalisation: see §4.3 — decided IN, gated on Q7** | Freight apportionment is universal; IGST-on-imports wrong = 3B table 4A(1) wrong. |
| **Bank statement import** (CSV/Excel per bank, per-source mapping memory, dedupe by (account, date, amount, ref)) feeding A10's manual matching | A factory reconciles a bank account in week one. Revision 1 shipped the matching screen with nothing to ingest the statement. |
| **Inter-GSTIN branch transfer** as a typed invoice with a stated valuation basis, posting to inter-branch control accounts, excluded from consolidated revenue and from aggregate turnover | 1–3 GSTINs is the stated ICP, so plant→depot across states is monthly. N16 says it *is* a supply; without a valuation basis the invoice is raised at an invented number, and without elimination the client's turnover is overstated by every transfer. **[VERIFY] q81** — Rule 28 basis (open market value / 90% rule / cost+10% / the full-ITC deemed-value election). |
| **Dispatch paperwork**: packing list, LR / consignment note, and the **Transporter master** EWB Part A requires | Neither the transporter master nor the consignment note existed anywhere in revision 1, and Part A cannot be filed without the former. |
| **Gate entry (vehicle, driver, time in/out) and weighbridge (gross/tare/net, variance vs PR/DN)** — with a specified integration path, see below | PHASE6 §3: zero grep hits across erpnext *and* odoo/addons. The first physical checkpoint of every movement in a real Indian factory. |
| **Delivery Challan as a first-class non-invoice movement document with its own series and e-way bill** | PHASE4 §9.2 + PHASE6 §3 gap 3. ERPNext's Delivery Note is invoice-linked dispatch — not the same document. |
| BOM (nested, on-demand rollup), Work Order, issue/backflush, FG receipt, **scrap and process-loss capture** | PHASE4 M7: scrap is a taxable outward supply with its own TCS code, and yield is the number the owner actually manages. |
| Job Card with operator time log, workstation double-booking guard, **reason-coded downtime linked to the job card** | PHASE6 §3: ERPNext's downtime entry is *not* linked to the job card so it can never feed OEE. Capture the link now — schema, not feature. |
| Quality Inspection (template-driven, readings tied to batch) | PHASE6 §7: ship the doctype in MVP so CAPA layers on later without schema churn. |
| **Job work: subcontracting challan out/in, challan *chains* (job-worker→job-worker), ITC-04 Tables 4 and 5A with hard-blocking linkage, open-challan ageing against the §143 window with alerting, opening-challan import, and the deemed-supply posting mechanism (J5)**. **ITC-04 filing cadence is an applicability row, not a constant** — annual for turnover ≤ ₹5 Cr, **half-yearly above ₹5 Cr** **[PHASE7 VERIFIED, 2 sources]**, a boundary the stated ICP straddles, so a customer crossing it mid-contract changes data (§4.2, D17) | PHASE4 §8 gap #1 of 20 by customer pain. The strongest renewal feature on the plan's own reasoning; demand is unmeasured (§8.6(c), Q1). **The free stack does ship a *GST Job Work Stock Movement* report — what no reference implements is the ageing half: a challan aged against the §143 clock, a deemed-supply posting dated to the original dispatch, and interest from that date** (§8.2 W2). J5 moves into v1 because it is that half a bolt-on app cannot do (§8.2). |

**E. Payroll**

Shift type + assignment with overlap guard, biometric check-in ingestion with duplicate guard and late-sync-safe checkpointing, auto-attendance, LWP/payment-days into the wage sheet (PHASE6 blocker #4). Salary component engine on a **whitelisted-AST evaluator, never `eval`** (PHASE4 Q14). **PF, ESI, PT (slab engine + design-partner states), TDS on salary and gratuity — computation only.** The **PF ECR file is OUT of v1** (§4.3); revision 1 listed it in both the IN and the deferred lists.

***"Wages" is a computed rule, not a field the HR admin fills in.*** A PIB backgrounder on the four Labour Codes states: *"'Wages' now include basic pay, dearness allowance, and retaining allowance; 50% of the total remuneration (or such percentage as may be notified) shall be added back to compute wages, ensuring consistency in calculating gratuity, pension, and social security benefits."* **[PHASE8 PRIMARY — PIB backgrounder PDF]**. If that is operative, a payroll engine **cannot sum whatever a component is labelled "basic"**: the 50% test is a rule that reads the whole remuneration structure and adds back, and it is the base every PF, ESI and gratuity computation then runs on. The design accommodation is cheap and is made now: the wage base is a **named function over the salary structure reading a rule-store row** (`codes.wages.addback_pct`), never a sum of components flagged `is_basic`. **[VERIFY] q96.** ***Stated with the caution the source demands: this document does not say the Codes came into force on 21 November 2025.*** That is the backgrounder's **publication date**; the only commencement text on the page is that the Code on Wages was notified 8 Aug 2019 and the other three on 29 Sep 2020, and a widely-repeated 2025 commencement claim failed audit. Whether the Codes and the state rules are in force is half of q96; the other half is whether the 50% test applies identically to PF, ESI and gratuity.

*Biometric ingestion needs the same Stage 0 research slot as Tally, and revision 1 gave it none.* eSSL, Matrix and ZKTeco push/pull formats are as absent from the references as Tally's XML, and PHASE6 §4's "must not be inferred" warning applies identically. Get two real device exports at Stage 0.

**F. Adoption machinery — this is what they are actually buying**

Import engine with fuzzy header matching and **persisted per-source mapping memory**, per-*value* remapping, resumable per-row log, upsert by external id, tree import with cycle detection (PHASE6 §4: "highest-value single idea here" — the second monthly refresh must auto-map). Opening balances: **trial-balance CSV → one balanced Opening JE**, opening AR/AP per party, opening stock reconciliation, all balancing to a temporary balance-sheet account, never P&L (PHASE6 §1.2 — no bulk trial-balance import exists anywhere in ERPNext). **Tally bridge**: masters + balances + open items, with CoA `root_type`/`account_type` inference, a human review step, and a GST-completion step. GST print formats via Chromium/Playwright. ~25 fixed reports including pending job-work challans by age and Rule 37 180-day ageing.

**Weighbridge, specified rather than waved at.** §8.5 calls it "a serial-port read" and §8.4 answer 7 promises "no app… a browser on a cheap Android tablet" — and those are incompatible: Chrome on Android has no Web Serial, and weighbridge indicators are RS-232 into a Windows PC. The honest answer is **a small signed Windows agent** that reads the indicator and POSTs a reading to the API against a pending ticket, with manual entry plus a photo of the display as the always-available fallback. The tablet story survives for gate entry and the shop floor, where it is true. If the agent is not funded, cut weighbridge from the wedge (§8.2 W3) rather than shipping the claim. Deployment, backup/retention, and a self-host build that runs acceptably on a 4 GB box (PHASE6 §6: Indian SMEs will demand on-prem on a ₹40–80k/yr server). **Includes the zero-infrastructure installer** — the one evidenced simplicity commitment §8.2 makes: no Bench, no Docker, no Linux, no site/app concepts ever visible to a customer. It is named in a wedge, so per the rule at the head of this document it appears here and in Stage 4 as a deliverable, or the claim comes out of §8.2.

**The on-prem requirement is carried on one unverified sentence, and neither research pass could verify it in either direction [PHASE8 UNEVIDENCED].** No market evidence exists for or against on-prem demand in this segment; Phase 8 found ~20 cloud-ERP vendor blogs asserting cloud is now the default and **every one of them sells cloud ERP**, against **zero primary buyer voice on either side**. The observation worth flagging: Tally is on-prem-*native*, and the assumption may have been inherited from the incumbent's **shape** rather than from customer **preference** — Tally now also sells subscription alongside perpetual, and Zoho Books, pure cloud, is a real presence in the same market **[PHASE7 VERIFIED]**. On-prem drives packaging, update distribution, backup/retention, ruleset-bundle delivery (§3.9) and a support surface on hardware we do not control. **§10 Q20 tests it at Stage 0: if 8 of 10 accept cloud, the self-host packaging and its support burden become v1.1 candidates rather than v1 scope.** Ask it in the same conversation as Q8 (dedicated-database isolation) — the two answers move together.

### 4.2 OUT

| Cut | Why it can wait | What the customer does meanwhile |
|---|---|---|
| **GST return FILING** (we prepare only) | PHASE4 Q1: filing needs a filing-grade GSP contract, OTP per session, a static allow-listed egress IP, EVC handling, and *liability for a failed filing*. Not what wins the first deal. | Their CA files from our portal-ready JSON/Excel in 20 minutes instead of two days. The CA is the gatekeeper — keeping him in the loop is a feature. *Labelled in revision 4: **CA-as-gatekeeper is this plan's own reasoning and carries zero external evidence.** Phase 7's only cited source for CA-driven retention does not contain the claim and was struck. It may well be true; §10 Q19 is where it gets tested, and until then it is an untested assumption in a document that labels its assumptions.* |
| Fixed assets and depreciation schedules — **entirely**, including the single book D9 previously described | One annual computation under two books; near-zero in monthly life. D9's "v1 ships one book (SL + WDV)" was an asset master, a schedule table, a posting path and a rebuild routine hiding inside the *deferred* table; it is struck (§6.9). | The CA's existing Excel schedule as one journal entry. We keep the fixed-asset boolean and the `jobwork_exempt_category` flag on the item, because ITC-04 goods type and the §143 carve-out need them. |
| Serial numbers / serial-level valuation | PHASE2 Q7: ERPNext carries a dual legacy representation precisely because it retrofitted serials over batches. Decide once, decide later. | Batch/lot with genealogy; serial as a captured text attribute for warranty lookup, not a valuation unit. |
| FIFO / standard costing / variance accounting | Two valuation methods means two repost engines before we have ten customers. | Moving average. *Revision 2 justified this as "accepted under AS-2 and Tally's own default" — **both citations are struck**: AS-2 appears nowhere in PHASE2, PHASE3 or PHASE4's evidence base, and no report establishes Tally's default (**[VERIFY] q74**). The tractability argument in §3.4 stands on its own and does not need either.* |
| Multi-currency FX: **unrealised** gain/loss and year-end revaluation. **Realised FX on settlement is IN (§4.1B)** | PHASE6 §7. **The four-leg GL schema is still day-one** — deferring the schema means a ledger backfill. Revision 1 deferred *both* while putting export/SEZ in v1, which made a foreign-currency receipt unpostable against its own balance constraint. | Rate at posting plus a realised-FX line on settlement; the CA books year-end revaluation. |
| Promotions, cumulative tiers, supplier scorecard, credit-limit *blocking*, RFQ, drop-ship. **The pricing *cascade* is not cut — see §4.3**: S8's item → group → brand resolution with slab matching and a deterministic tiebreak is IN, because a price list without a resolution order is not a price list. What is cut is everything above it | PHASE2 shows the rest are deep and expensive; none is why a factory switches ERP | Price lists + the S8 cascade + per-customer discount %; credit exposure as a report. |
| Workflow engine, approval inbox, assignment rules. **Notifications are NOT cut — see §4.1A and §4.3** | PHASE5 §8.4 skips the *workflow* engine for MVP. In a 40-person SME the owner approves everything. Revision 2's row also cut notifications, which eight v1 features require | Role-based submit rights plus one hardcoded amount threshold on PO/SI. Two days instead of two months. |
| Finite-capacity scheduling / APS | PHASE6 §7: "a multi-quarter project." | Job cards + workstation capacity guard + a dispatch list ordered by due date. |
| Maintenance / MTBF / preventive, fleet, RMA | Only valuable once production data is trustworthy. | Downtime is already captured against job cards, so the data exists when the module lands. |
| CAPA / non-conformance workflow | PHASE6 §7: layers onto Quality Inspection later without schema churn. | Inspection results + email. |
| BI / query builder | PHASE6 §5: "entirely disproportionate to 'an SME wants six dashboards'." | 25 fixed reports + XLSX export. |
| POS, retail, ecommerce, portals, projects/timesheets | No manufacturing SME buys the ERP for these. | — |
| Turnover-based applicability **engine** (aggregate-turnover computation and automatic switching) | PHASE4 Q11 puts it at v1.5. High value, not a gate. **What is IN is the *switchability*:** every threshold-governed behaviour in §5.2 reads an effective-dated rule-store row plus an operator-set applicability date, so the customer who crosses mid-contract changes data, not code. §5.2's "built as switchable, effective-dated behaviour in v1" and this row are consistent under that reading and revision 1 left them contradictory. **Evidence for the decision, which was previously argued only on principle:** the e-invoice threshold has already moved once — ₹10 Cr → ₹5 Cr from **1 August 2023**, now read off **Notification 10/2023-Central Tax** itself rather than a blog **[PHASE8 PRIMARY — gstcouncil.gov.in]** — and a further cut to **₹2 Cr has been discussed but is not notified** as of mid-2026 **[PHASE8 PRIMARY — negative result: searches of gstcouncil.gov.in and pib.gov.in return nothing; the claim lives on one SEO blog while a separate dated source says ₹5 Cr stands]**. The 30-day IRP reporting limit has the same history: **AATO ≥ ₹100 Cr from 1 Nov 2023, lowered to ≥ ₹10 Cr from 1 Apr 2025**, with GSTN's own caveat that below ₹10 Cr there is no restriction *"as of now"* **[PHASE8 PRIMARY — einvoice1.gst.gov.in advisories 08.09.2023 and 27.03.2025]**. A hardcoded threshold would already have been wrong twice on each. *Vendor blogs treat ₹2 Cr as imminent; this document states no ₹2 Cr figure and no date, because none is notified.* **Two further confirmations added in revision 5, and they are the strongest this decision has:** (i) the **whole GST rate structure collapsed from four tiers to two rates plus a 40% de-merit rate on 22 September 2025** **[PHASE8 PRIMARY]** — not a threshold moving, the entire rate map, in one Council decision (§3.9); and (ii) the **MSMED (Amendment) Bill 2026, passed by both Houses in August 2026, removes the numeric MSME classification thresholds from the Act and empowers the government to set them by notification** **[PHASE8 SINGLE-SOURCE — PRS Legislative Research, an independent legislative-research nonprofit; verify commencement and any subsequent notification with the CA]**. The 2025 numbers remain operative and none has been re-notified. **A threshold Parliament has just made easier to change must never be a constant** — and a rate map that has already been rewritten wholesale must never be one either. | Operator enters the e-invoice-applicable date and HSN digit count, as every reference forces them to. |
| **DRC-01C / Rule 88D response workflow** | Rule 88D generates a system intimation when GSTR-3B ITC exceeds GSTR-2B ITC beyond a prescribed threshold, with a **7-day reply window** **[PHASE7 SINGLE-SOURCE]**. The response is a portal-filing surface and filing is out of v1 anyway. Cutting it is defensible; not knowing it existed was not. **[VERIFY] q92** — the prescribed threshold, the 7-day window, and whether non-response blocks a subsequent GSTR-1 (the source attributes that block to **Rule 59(6)**, not to DRC-01C; the research conflated them and **the two must not be conflated in code**) | **What is IN:** the 2B reconciliation worklist exposes the GSTR-3B-vs-2B ITC delta as an explicit, named figure, so the customer sees the DRC-01C trigger *before* filing 3B rather than after the intimation arrives. |
| Rules 42/43, Rule 37 automated reversal, interest/late fee, GSTR-9/9C, ISD/GSTR-6, composition, RoDTEP/drawback/EPCG | Annual, edge-case, or CA-computed. | CA computes and passes a journal entry, as today. We ship the Rule 37 *ageing report* so the number is visible. |
| Native mobile app | Every screen we need on a device is a form and a scanner. | Responsive web on a ₹12k Android tablet at the gate and on the floor. |
| Realtime/websockets, generic form renderer for core screens, visual print builder, server-script sandbox, multi-DB | PHASE5 §8.3/§8.4, verbatim. | Polling; hand-built React on ~40 screens; Nunjucks templates as DB rows; a bounded expression DSL. |
| Process manufacturing: co-products, yield-based costing, potency UoM | PHASE2 Q8: co-product allocation adds a 100%-sum invariant to every BOM. Out of target segment. | Scrap and process loss are in; true co-product cost allocation is not. |
| **Full Tally voucher history replay** | CoA type inference is already rated **Hard** (PHASE6 §4) on masters alone; replaying five years of vouchers multiplies the risk with no monthly benefit. | Masters + balances + open items migrate; Tally stays a read-only archive. This is what every real migration does anyway. |


### 4.3 The cut-line contradictions, resolved

Revision 1 had three features simultaneously in the IN list and the deferred list, which made §9.3 R14's counter-measure ("take the deferral lists literally") impossible to follow. The second review round found three more that revision 2 introduced or left standing. One table, one column, one owner. **Where this table and any other section disagree, this table wins.**

| Feature | Decision | Owner | The losing sentence, deleted |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Bill of Entry + landed-cost capitalisation** | **IN v1**, conditional release: build the BoE fields and V8's capitalisation path in Stage 3; if §10 Q7 comes back "none of the first ten prospects import", drop it to v1.5 before Stage 3 starts and reclaim ~3 engineer-weeks | Product | §5.3's deferral row is struck; §3.5's audit coverage and §6.6 V8 already assumed it was in |
| **PF ECR file** | **OUT of v1** — computation ships, the return file does not, exactly as §5.3's split says | Payroll | §4.1E's "and the PF ECR file" and §7 Stage 4's "…/gratuity/ECR" are struck |
| **Turnover applicability** | **Switchable effective-dated behaviour IN; the aggregate-turnover computation engine OUT** (§4.2, D17) | Compliance | §5.2's unqualified "in v1" is now qualified in place |
| **Notifications** | **IN v1 as a minimal service** — rules table, email/SMS/WhatsApp adapters, dedup, delivery log. The **workflow and approval engine stays OUT** | Platform | §4.2's "workflow engine, approval inbox, assignment rules, notifications" cut the wrong noun; eight v1 features (§4.1A) need delivery |
| **Pricing cascade (S8)** | **IN v1** — item → group → brand, slab matching, priority, deterministic tiebreak with a warning. Promotions, cumulative tiers and cross-document rules stay OUT | Sales | §4.2's "pricing-rule engine … out" contradicted S8, which is a pricing-rule engine and is in the Wave 4 backlog |
| **Interest computation** | **§143 interest IN v1** (J5 cannot state a rupee exposure without it, and it is the number the wedge is sold on). **3B table 5.1 interest and late fee stay OUT** to v1.1 | Compliance | §6.9 D15's blanket "interest and late fee" deferral, which contradicted §5.3's J5 scope. PHASE4 rates the interest computation Low difficulty |

Further scope changes recorded here so they are not relitigated: **J5 (deemed-supply posting) moves into v1** (§5.3, §8.2); **realised FX moves into v1** (§4.1B); **ITC-04 Tables 5B and 5C move into v1** alongside the challan chains that make them reachable (§5.1 N17, §6.7 M8); and **the invoice print contract** — signatory, copy-type, Rule 46A variant, SKD/CKD chain — is in v1 as document and series structure, not as layout work (§3.3).

**One scope request refused, and why it is refused rather than ignored.** The second review round argued for a minimal fixed-asset register in v1 on the grounds that a statutory register living in a spreadsheet undercuts the Rule 11(g) claim the whole architecture is built for. The argument is correct and the answer is still no: D9 is a multi-week module and §4.3's discipline is that a deferral is deferred. What changes is the **honesty of the sales line** — §8.4 answer 4 now states the consequence plainly instead of presenting it as a convenience, so the customer decides rather than discovers. The same treatment is applied to **cost records under s.148 / CRA-1** (**[VERIFY] q87**), which the review round raised as an obligation this turnover band and these product categories can attract and which v1 cannot produce: it becomes a qualifying question in the first sales call, not a silent gap.

---

## 5. COMPLIANCE GATE

**Standing caveat, inherited from PHASE4 and not softened:** every threshold, rate and date below is **[VERIFY]**. The four references are evidence of *implementation shape*, not of *statute*. PHASE4's own header: "This document is not legal advice and must not be trusted as a statement of current Indian law." A qualified Indian CA/GST practitioner must sign the rule table before v1 ships, and that sign-off is a budgeted line item and a dated release artefact, not a favour. **Sourcing the underlying data is a separate cost from the sign-off — see §3.9.**

**Labelling rule, added in review and applied to this document itself.** Every statutory assertion in §3 and §6 carries **[CODE]** / **[VERIFY]** / **[NOT IN CODE]** and a §5.5 question number. Review rejects any unlabelled statutory claim. The adversarial round found seven places where revision 1 stated flat what PHASE4 marks [VERIFY] or [NOT IN CODE], and the plan's own §9.3 rule — "a rule with no provenance is a defect" — had never been applied to the plan. §5.6 lists them.

### 5.1 Unconditional — no threshold, no exceptions

| # | Capability | Why unconditional |
|---|---|---|
| N1 | GSTIN capture + full structural validation (15 chars, state code, embedded PAN, mod-36, category↔format coherence) | A wrong GSTIN poisons every return and is uncorrectable post-filing |
| N2 | Place of Supply stored first-class with an explicit fallback chain | Single field decides CGST+SGST vs IGST on every document |
| N3 | `is_inter_state()` as one explicit unit-testable predicate + six blocking tax-head validations, over the **four-head** character set `{IGST, CGST, SGST, UTGST}` | Wrong head ⇒ recipient loses ITC ⇒ churn. PHASE4: "the single most important rule in the product". UTGST is absent from both references and from revision 1 — see §3.10 and **[VERIFY] q79** |
| N4 | Named GST accounts per company per type (Input/Output/RCM/Refund) **× four heads including UTGST** | 3B cannot be tied to the ledger without them. Adding the head later is a ledger and return-mapping migration |
| N5 | HSN/SAC 4/6/8 digits, `99…`⇒service, UQC on every UoM with `OTH` fallback | Blocks GSTR-1 and IRN outright |
| N6 | Item-level `gst_treatment` with Nil/Exempt/Non-GST as **three distinct zero-rate tax records**, never "no tax"; per-line taxable value and per-tax rate *and* amount | Without these you cannot emit a GSTR-1 or an e-invoice payload at all |
| N7 | Gapless numbering scoped (GSTIN, doctype, FY), cancelled numbers retained | IRP rejects anything else; gaps are an audit finding |
| N8 | RCM with balanced mirrored rows + self-invoice numbering, keyed on **HSN/SAC or expense category**, not counterparty status alone | GTA freight, legal, security from *registered* vendors is the common manufacturer case, and both references miss it |
| N9 | Immutable hardened audit trail | MCA Rule 11(g); constrains the core data layer |
| N10 | V1–V12 blocking validations preventing an unfileable invoice from being submitted | Cheaper to block at save than unwind after filing |
| N11 | GSTR-1 + 3B preparation with **section classification stored per line at post time**, the **six outward amendment tables** (B2BA, B2CLA, CDNRA, CDNURA, EXPA, B2CSA), **Table 13 Documents Issued** fed from `series_nature`, and **Table 11A/11B advances** driven by the advance-taxability flag (§6.5 A6) | Report-time classification is "the single most common source of filing mismatches" (PHASE4 §9.3). The amendment tables and Table 13 were absent from revision 1; without them the product works for one month and fails in month two |
| N12 | GSTR-2B download + purchase reconciliation | ITC is claimable only to the extent it appears in 2B |
| N13 | Multi-GSTIN scoping on every query from the first migration | Factory in one state + depot in another is the normal case |
| N14 | **e-Way Bill on stock movement**, not just invoices — SI, PI, DN, DC, PR, Stock Entry, Subcontracting Receipt — full Part A/Part B lifecycle, with applicability as a **rule-store predicate carrying named carve-outs**, not a bare threshold comparison | Factory material moves on a challan long before an invoice. Truck detention is the failure mode.
