PHASE4 §3.3's rule is threshold-only. **Confirmed on the rule text in revision 5, and it is no longer an "understood requirement":** CBIC Rule 138, third proviso — *"where goods are sent by a principal located in one State or Union territory to a job worker located in any other State or Union territory, the e-way bill shall be generated either by the principal or the job worker, if registered, **irrespective of the value of the consignment**"* **[PHASE8 PRIMARY — taxinformation.cbic.gov.in, Rule 138]**. **The ₹50,000 general threshold does not apply to this movement, and both the threshold and the carve-out are rule-store rows, not one comparison with an exception buried in a service.** A value-threshold-only implementation silently omits the e-way bill on **every** sub-₹50,000 interstate job-work challan — a ₹18,000 tooling consignment becomes a detention event, silently and statutorily, which is exactly the defect class §5's standing caveat exists to catch. Enforced as a **named blocking check inside N14's applicability predicate** — *an outward job-work delivery challan whose job worker is in a different State/UT requires an e-way bill regardless of `consignment_value`* — rather than as a new V-number, for the same reason revision 4 declined to add one (§11: §5.1 says V1–V12 and §6.4 T14 says V1–V11, and adding to a disputed count deepens a defect instead of fixing it). Job-work challans default to "EWB required" and an override needs a reason and a role. **Rule 138's validity arithmetic is confirmed on the same page** — 1 day per 200 km or part thereof for normal cargo — and is a rule-store row. **q82 is narrowed, not closed: does the carve-out still bind when the job worker is *unregistered*?** The rule text reads "either by the principal or the job worker, **if registered**", which qualifies *the generator*, not obviously the job worker; the safe default until the CA answers is that the principal generates. **And the threshold is tested against the wrong number in PHASE4 §3.3**, which uses the document grand total: `consignment_value` is a **computed, stored field on every EWB-eligible document**, excluding exempt lines, and it — not the grand total — is what the predicate reads |
| N15 | First-class **Delivery Challan** with its own series, carrying a declared taxable value with no invoice | Required for job work, returnable gate pass, samples, and EWB `docType=CHL` |
| N16 | Job-work outward/inward with sub-supply types 4/6, ownership never transferring, **GSTIN-pair keying**; and the inter-GSTIN branch transfer as a typed invoice with a declared valuation basis (§4.1D) | Core to Indian discrete manufacturing. A transfer to another branch under the same GSTIN is internal; to the same entity's *other* GSTIN is understood to be a supply — **[VERIFY] q81**, because PHASE4 §5.2 offers only a **[CODE]** observation about a query filter and revisions 1 and 2 both stated it flat. Revision 1 also said it without giving that invoice a value |
| N17 | ITC-04 **Tables 4, 5A, 5B and 5C** with **mandatory hard-blocking linkage** to the original outward challan | The reference only soft-alerts and silently drops unreferenced rows — do not copy. **5B (goods sent from one job worker to another) and 5C (goods supplied directly from the job worker's premises) were absent from revision 2**, which is inconsistent with M8's own challan chains and J7 modelling: a fabrication SME sending a part for heat treatment and then on to a machining shop is the normal case, not an edge case, and a return that stops at Tables 4/5A is incomplete for exactly the customer this product targets. **[VERIFY] q88** — the endorsement documentation and which party reports which table |
| N18 | Ineligible-ITC routing into GST expense or item valuation | Changes manufacturing cost directly |
| N19 | Item aggregation to payload limits (1000 lines IRN, 250 EWB) with HSN+UOM+rate-wise rollup | BOM-heavy invoices exceed both |
| N20 | Payroll: safe-evaluator component engine + attendance→wage-sheet pipeline | PHASE6 blocker: factory wages are attendance-driven; without it the customer runs the old system alongside |

**Commercial gates that behave identically to statutory ones** (PHASE6 §1): opening balances incl. the trial-balance CSV importer, and a Tally import path. Not compliance, same door.

### 5.2 Threshold-dependent — applicability varies, the code does not

Every item here is built as **switchable, effective-dated behaviour** in v1 — the threshold lives in the rule store and the applicability date is operator-set — even where the first cohort sits below the line, because the customer who crosses mid-contract must not have to migrate. What is deferred is the **aggregate-turnover computation** that would set that date automatically (§4.2, §4.3, D17). Revision 1 stated the first half here and the second half in §4.2 without reconciling them.

| Capability | Threshold governs | Reference status |
|---|---|---|
| e-Invoicing (IRN) | Aggregate annual turnover — **₹5 Cr from 1 August 2023** (Notification 10/2023-CT), reduced from ₹10 Cr; a further cut to ₹2 Cr is discussed and **unnotified** as of mid-2026 **[PHASE8 PRIMARY]** | **No turnover logic exists in any reference.** All make an operator type a date. The threshold's own history is the argument for the rule store (§4.2) |
| e-Invoice **reporting time limit** — an invoice older than N days cannot be reported to the IRP | Turnover **≥ ₹10 Cr**; **N = 30 days** from the document date, effective **1 April 2025** and continuing into FY 2026-27 **[PHASE7 VERIFIED, single source]** | Absent from every reference *and* from revisions 1–3 of this plan. Ships as blocking validation **T16**; **[VERIFY] q91** |
| MSME/Udyam classification → 15/45-day due date → 43B(h) disallowance | Investment and turnover bands, **revised effective 1 April 2025 by S.O. 1364(E)** **[PHASE7 VERIFIED]** | **[NOT IN CODE]** in any reference ("MSME No." is a print-header label). Thresholds are rule-store rows; **[VERIFY] q93** |
| HSN digit count (4/6/8) | Turnover boundary; Odoo labels read "< 5 CR"/"> 5 CR"; india-compliance cites CBIC N-78/2020 in a field description | Setting is [CODE], boundary is [VERIFY], and **no turnover lookup is implemented anywhere** |
| GSTR-1 frequency (monthly vs QRMP) | QRMP eligibility turnover | Modelled as an operator-chosen preference |
| ITC-04 periodicity | Turnover boundary — **annual ≤ ₹5 Cr (from FY 2022-23), half-yearly > ₹5 Cr**, quarterly before Oct 2021 **[PHASE8 PRIMARY — GST portal ITC-04 manual]**, a boundary the stated ICP straddles | Operator picks a date range today; the cadence itself becomes a rule-store row rather than an operator habit |
| TDS on purchase of goods | Deductor's preceding-year turnover | **Not implemented anywhere**; encoded under Income-tax Act **2025** numbering effective 2026-04-01 |
| e-Way Bill intra-state | Per-state row: a state may disapply entirely; otherwise per-state threshold, else the global inter-state figure | [VERIFY] both the global figure and every state row |
| B2CL classification | Inter-state only. **The two references disagree on the effective date** (2024-08-01 vs 2024-11-01) and neither cites a notification | [VERIFY] — see §5.5 q.38 |
| PF / ESI applicability | Headcount trigger | **[NOT IN CODE]** in any reference |
| Professional Tax | State slabs, frequency, February-differs rule | **[NOT IN CODE]** — no Indian state is named anywhere in any reference |
| MCA Rule 11(g) | Entity form under the Companies Act | Applicability varies; **the build does not**, because it cannot be retrofitted |

The pattern is a product decision, not a nice-to-have. PHASE4 ranks the turnover-based applicability engine gap #3 precisely because *no reference computes aggregate turnover at all*. One computation feeds four applicability decisions and would make us materially safer than every competitor including ERPNext. It is v1.5, but only because the rule store (which it depends on) is v1.

### 5.3 Phasing, with the commercial consequence of each deferral

**In v1, beyond §5.1:** full e-invoicing (generation, 24h cancellation, CRN/DBN, signed-JWT storage **with signature verification**, duplicate-IRN query-back and verify-before-adopt, and the posted-vs-issuable gate of §3.3); full e-way-bill lifecycle (Part A at invoicing, Part B when the truck loads, transporter reassignment, validity extension, EWB-via-IRN short path with its entirely different key names); **offline JSON fallback + manual EWB entry** (12-digit numeric validation — not every SME buys GSP credits from day one, and it is what makes a low-connectivity self-host usable); export/SEZ four regimes + LUT validity tracking (implemented **nowhere**) **plus realised FX on settlement**, because the two are the same transaction; **job work J1–J7 including J5**; payroll PF+ESI+PT+salary TDS+gratuity **computation**; TDS/TCS on purchases with **PAN-level aggregation across party records**; the effective-dated rule store of §3.9.

**J5 moves into v1, and this is the most consequential scope change in the revision.** §8.2 W2's defensibility argument is that the deemed-supply payload "touches stock, GL and GST simultaneously: a bolt-on app cannot do that; only whoever owns the ledger can" — and revision 1 then deferred exactly that half, shipping a challan register with an ageing report, which is precisely what a Frappe app *can* do over india-compliance's existing ITC-04 tables and which §8.5 concedes any of hundreds of partners ships in a quarter, free. Leading with the commoditisable half while deferring the defensible half is not a defensible v1. What ships in v1 is the **mechanism**: the `deemed_supply_143` voucher type, the posting path of §3.4, `supply_date` / `gst_report_period`, the interest computation and the ITC reversal, with **every constant read from the rule store**. What is gated is the *data* — q24–33 — and §7 Stage 0 now carries a named date for those answers. If the CA has not answered by the Stage 3 entry gate, J5 ships behind a per-tenant flag that is off, and the register plus manual workflow is the fallback. That is a data risk, not an architecture risk, which is the whole point of §3.9.

*Bill of Entry has moved out of this table — it is provisionally IN v1 per §4.3, subject to Q7 at the Stage 0 gate. Revision 1 called it "the deferral I am least comfortable with" while three other sections already assumed it was in.*

| Deferred to v1.5 | Commercial consequence, plainly |
|---|---|
| GST return **filing** | You lose nobody. **But note the trap:** GSTR-2B download is itself a GSP call, so "no GSP in v1" is not an available option. The deferral is of the filing leg only, not the vendor relationship |
| Turnover-based applicability engine | You lose no customer but inherit the same silent-misconfiguration risk every competitor has, and forfeit the strongest safety claim in the sales conversation |
| Deemed-supply **auto**-invoice on expiry (unattended generation) | J5's posting mechanism is now in v1; what remains deferred is firing it without a human. The register raises the alarm and a supervisor confirms the posting |
| Rule 37 / 180-day ITC reversal | No lost deal, but every customer carries a live assessment exposure. Grep for `180` across india-compliance returns only e-way-bill error text |
| Interest and late-fee computation (3B table 5.1) | Nothing lost; SMEs compute it by hand today. Low effort, high goodwill — pull forward if v1 slips and needs a win |
| Statutory payroll **return files** (ECR, ESI contribution, PT challans, 24Q, Form 16, 12BB) — **including the PF ECR file, which §4.1E previously also claimed was in v1** | You keep the customer but do not displace their payroll consultant. Right split: computation feeds the wage sheet and the ledger; the files are format work with no shared logic |
| MSME / §43B(h) discipline | Absent everywhere ("MSME No." exists only as a print-header label). Strong differentiator at low cost — we ship the ageing report in v1 and the full discipline in v1.5 |
| Dynamic QR on B2C invoices | Only bites customers with material B2C volume |

**v2:** GSTR-9/9C, ISD/GSTR-6, composition scheme, Rules 42/43, cash/credit/liability ledger modelling with IGST→CGST→SGST set-off, consolidated EWB and multi-vehicle transhipment, place-of-supply special rules for services (absent everywhere; a hard wall the moment a customer sells AMC or installation).

**Two deferrals refused, and why.**

*Deferring e-invoicing excludes every manufacturer above the AATO threshold — the entire segment worth selling to.* Their customers police the IRN because their ITC depends on it; a customer who crosses the threshold mid-contract does not file a ticket, they churn — to ERPNext, which is free and already has it. What is left is sub-threshold SMEs: most likely still on Tally, least willing to pay, adequately served by a free GPL incumbent. That is a pilot programme, not a market.

*Deferring e-way bill is not a deferral, it is a decision not to sell to manufacturers.* Goods move by road on essentially every despatch, and nothing about it scales with turnover.

**Payroll — overruling PHASE4's own internal disagreement.** PHASE4 §1.1 lists G14 (PF, ESI, PT, salary TDS) as an unconditional v1 gate. PHASE4 §10 Q5 recommends v1 = engine + income tax + gratuity + PF, with ESI and PT in v2. These contradict. **We take a third position closer to §1.1: ship the *computation* for PF, ESI and PT in v1; defer only the *return files*.** Once rates are sourced, ESI is a threshold, two rates, two contribution periods and one mid-period continuation rule — days, not weeks; PT is a state master plus a slab table, the same shape the income-tax slab engine already needs. The genuinely large workstream §10 Q5 is flinching at is the returns, and that is exactly what v1.5 defers. Factories are exactly the covered establishments, and a wage sheet that cannot compute ESI is not a partial feature but an incorrect one. *Revision 1 also asserted that "factory wage bands sit squarely under the ESI ceiling" — **struck.** PHASE4 §6.3 marks the wage threshold **[NOT IN CODE]** and records that the string "ESI" appears in hrms only in a test fixture and a comment; you cannot know where wages sit relative to a number you have declared you do not have. The argument for shipping ESI computation does not need that claim, and **[VERIFY] q11** settles it.*

**Export/SEZ vs multi-currency — an apparent conflict that was *partly* real.** PHASE4 Q7 wants export/SEZ + LUT in v1; PHASE6 §7 defers multi-currency. Revision 1 resolved this as "regimes and LUT are classification and dates (in); FX is accounting machinery (out)" and the split was drawn one step too far: **realised** FX is not machinery, it is one rule and one account, and without it a foreign-currency receipt against an export invoice does not balance in INR and §3.4's deferred constraint trigger refuses the COMMIT — leaving an unpostable receipt or a silent plug. Corrected split: export regimes, LUT **and realised FX on settlement** are in; **unrealised** FX and year-end revaluation are out (§4.2, D2).

### 5.4 External dependencies — the GSP decision

**Everything goes through a GSP/ASP gateway. Never directly to NIC/GSTN.** Both references do this and no alternative architecture exists. **The GSP is a v1 dependency even under the "defer filing" plan**, because GSTR-2B download is a GSP call. There is no v1 without a signed GSP contract — do not schedule GSP procurement for v1.5.

**Blocking question nobody asked: does 2B download inherit the Returns-API session constraint?** PHASE4 §4.6 attaches OTP-per-GSTIN-session and `ip-usr` IP binding to the **Returns API**, which is where 2B lives, and no report says 2B is exempt. If it is not exempt, then monthly 2B reconciliation — an N12 unconditional gate — is an **attended** operation requiring the customer's portal OTP every month per GSTIN, which is a product, UX, support and credential-liability design revision 1 never wrote. **This is a blocking question for the GSP sandbox in Stage 0 (§10 Q4), answered before Stage 2 begins.** If the answer is yes: design the OTP-capture flow now (a scheduled "2B day" prompt to a named user per GSTIN, a session-validity indicator, a clear failure state when the OTP is not supplied, and an explicit statement that we never store the OTP), and add the support load to the staffing model in §7.6.

**Build against a mock, verify against the sandbox.** Revision 1 defined Stage 2 as delivering e-invoice and EWB "against the GSP sandbox" with no path if procurement slips past month 2 — which for a pre-revenue company negotiating sandbox access is the likely case, not the tail case. Corrected: a **fixture-driven mock IRP/EWB/2B service** derived from the published NIC API shapes is built in Stage 1 and is the default target; D6's versioned error-code table makes replaying the failure surface natural. The sandbox becomes *verification*, not *prerequisite*, so a GSP slip costs a week of re-verification instead of a stage.

| # | Decision | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | One GSP for all four services, or several? | **One vendor covering e-invoice, EWB, returns and 2B**, behind an internal interface so a second can be added without touching callers. *The interface itself, which revision 1 named but never drew:* `generateIrn(payload) → Result<IrnResponse, GspError>`, `cancelIrn`, `getIrnByDocNo`, `generateEwb`, `updatePartB`, `extendValidity`, `updateTransporter`, `download2B(gstin, period)` — every method returning a discriminated `Result` whose error carries the versioned `(code, message, class)` of D6, never a thrown string. The fixture-driven mock and the real client implement the same interface, which is what makes the mock a schedule de-risker rather than throwaway code |
| D2 | Standard NIC crypto (we implement RSA/PKCS1v15 auth, AES `Sek`/`Rek`, HMAC-SHA256) vs the GSP's enriched mode (they see our payloads) | **Start enriched.** Keep standard on the roadmap for data-residency objections — "our GSP sees your invoices" must not be a surprise mid-deal |
| D3 | Sandbox before signing | **Non-negotiable contract precondition.** Stamp `is_generated_in_sandbox_mode` on every artefact; the retry scheduler skips sandbox records |
| D4 | Our key or one per customer? | Decide with D9. **Vault either way** — both references store credentials as plaintext fields restricted to a system group |
| D5 | 429 / credit-exhaustion semantics | Negotiate rate limits, burst behaviour and top-up latency **in the contract**, not a support email. Quota outage is a first-class retryable state |
| D6 | Error-code catalogue ownership and versioning | Ship a **versioned code→(message, class) table** for both channels. Never classify by string-matching government prose — NIC changes wording |
| D7 | Egress IP topology | Filing binds the session to the caller's public IP (`ip-usr`; `AUTH4033` on change). **Plan a stable allow-listed egress IP now** even though filing is v1.5 — retrofitting it after horizontal scaling is an infrastructure migration |
| D8 | OTP-per-session UX | Filing cannot be unattended; `sid` is formatted exactly `<PAN>\|<OTP>`. A product-design constraint, not a bug |
| D9 | Tenancy model | **Answer before the audit-log schema is written** (PHASE4 Q15). Our answer is §3.7 |
| D10 | Who owns the statutory rule table | **Internal compliance function with a CA on retainer, delivered as signed data updates.** An operating cost line, and the answer to "why pay when ERPNext is free" |

**Non-GSP data dependencies, all versioned, none as constants:** port codes, country codes, UQC list, pincode-to-state bands (with known overlaps at Chandigarh/Punjab, Telangana/AP, Puducherry that reject legitimate addresses), state code master including synthetic **96 "Other Countries"** and **97 "Other Territory"** as real records.

### 5.5 The statutory questions a human must answer before these features are buildable

PHASE4 §6's governing statement: "this section does not state PF/ESI/PT numbers, because doing so would mean inventing them." No number reaches the repository without a cited notification and an effective date. This is the brief for the CA; each answer becomes a dated, provenance-stamped row in the rule store.

**Provident Fund (nothing exists in any reference) — q1–10:** employee rate and the wage base; employer rate and its EPS/EPF split; the monthly wage ceiling and whether voluntary contribution above it is permitted; EDLI rate and ceiling; administrative/inspection charges; VPF cap and its base; international-worker and excluded-employee definitions; headcount trigger and how counted; **ECR file format, field order and due date**; whether UAN/PF number has validatable structure.

**ESI (the string appears in hrms only in a test fixture and a comment) — q11–17:** wage threshold and which components count; employee rate; employer rate; the two fixed contribution periods' exact boundaries; **confirmation of the mid-period continuation rule** (a state-machine requirement, not a rate); headcount trigger; monthly file format and due date.

**Professional Tax (no Indian state is named anywhere) — q18–23:** which states are in scope for v1 (answered by where the first customers are, not by completeness); per state the full slab table with effective dates, filing frequency, **the February-differs rule**, challan format and due date; whether PTEC and PTRC must both be tracked.

**Section 143 job work — the highest-value gap — q24–33:** the return window for inputs (one year) and the window for capital goods (three years). **Confidence upgraded again in revision 5, and this time on the statute itself.** Revision 4 rested on five secondary Indian tax sources; the windows, the moulds/dies/jigs/fixtures/tools carve-out and the deeming fiction are now read on **the CBIC statutory repository's own s.143 page** **[PHASE8 PRIMARY]**. The CA's job on these shrinks from research to confirmation, which is the difference between a two-week question and an open-ended one — but the standing caveat is unchanged: they are still rule-store rows and they still ship only after sign-off. Alongside it, the **ITC-04 cadence** (half-yearly above ₹5 Cr; **yearly ≤ ₹5 Cr from FY 2022-23**; quarterly before Oct 2021) is now confirmed on the **GST portal's own ITC-04 manual** **[PHASE8 PRIMARY]**. The **≤ ₹25,000 s.125 penalty** for non- or incorrect filing remains a secondary-sourced figure **[PHASE7 VERIFIED]** and is load-bearing for §8.2 W2's "sell the liability, not the filing" argument, so confirm it explicitly.

> **The new fact revision 4 did not have, and it changes the engine — s.143, second proviso, verbatim:** *"the period of one year and three years may, on sufficient cause being shown, be **extended by the Commissioner** for a further period not exceeding one year and two years respectively."* **[PHASE8 PRIMARY]** **A hard-coded 365-day trigger with no extension path manufactures liability that does not exist.** This is a correctness requirement, not a refinement — see §6.7 M8 for the model and **q94** below for what the CA must still answer about the mechanics.

Also still to confirm: **the extension mechanics — who applies, on what evidence, how the order is recorded, and whether an order is per-challan or blanket for a period** (new in revision 5, and the one place §143 got *harder* rather than easier); **the moulds/dies/jigs/fixtures/tools carve-out** scope; **deemed supply on expiry** — valuation basis, the date the tax invoice must bear (we assume back-dated to original despatch — confirm), interest rate and start date; ITC reversal mechanics and whether reversal and deemed supply are alternatives or cumulative; direct supply from the job worker's premises to a third party and who raises which document; waste and scrap arising at the job worker; registered vs unregistered job worker consequences; **Rule 55 valuation confirmation** for returned unused customer RM (the only rule-level citation present anywhere in the job-work code).

**GST thresholds, dates and formulae — q34–51:** e-invoicing AATO threshold, effective date, and how aggregate turnover is defined for the test; HSN digit requirement by turnover and whether N-78/2020 is still operative; QRMP eligibility turnover; ITC-04 periodicity boundaries; **B2CL effective date — 2024-08-01 or 2024-11-01? The references disagree and neither cites a notification; this misstates the return every month if wrong**; B2CS vs CDNUR for small unregistered notes (the reference's predicate chain puts them in B2CS by *not excluding* notes, unlike every other predicate — get a ruling before writing the classifier); EWB inter-state threshold and the per-state intra-state table; **the notified RCM categories list mapped to HSN/SAC** (this replaces the broken counterparty-only model); the RCM threshold for unregistered purchases (₹5,000 in code is a configurable default, **not law**); CGST s.16(4) ITC deadline; GSTR-2B availability date (the 14th is hardcoded, uncited, and GSTN generation dates have moved); interest and late-fee rates for 3B table 5.1 (no rate, no computation, no field exists anywhere); Rule 37 window and reclaim mechanics; Rules 42/43 D1/D2/C2 formulae (a *label* only in the references); the current GST slab schedule (the two references' permitted-rate lists disagree and 12/28/40 coexisting suggests the data spans a transition — **treat neither as current**); **cess "higher of ad valorem or specific"** — the real formula, since both references encode it only in the tax's *display name* ("CESS 21% or 4.170"), which is not computable; HSN-summary B2B/B2C bifurcation date; e-invoice reporting time limit.

**Income tax and withholding — q52–64:** slab bands per regime with dates; standard deduction, relief and marginal-relief limits (all user-entered in the reference, no rupee value hardcoded); surcharge schedule (the reference's surcharge hook is a **no-op**); cess and its base; **old vs new regime election and lock-in rules** (grep for "regime" returns nothing); HRA constants (10%/50%/40% hardcoded with an in-code `TODO make this configurable` and no section cited) plus the metro city list; **gratuity** — the 15/26 fraction, the 5-year minimum, the statutory maximum cap (absent), the death/disability waiver (absent), and part-year treatment (the reference rounds 4.5 up to 5 and would pay someone who has not completed five years); whether the Income-tax Act 2025 renumbering is operative and how the dated changeover works (**both schedules must be supported**, because back-dated documents use old numbering); TDS/TCS rates and both thresholds per section per deductee type with dates; 194Q vs 206C(1H) mutual exclusion and declaration exchange; 206AB, 15G/15H, DTAA; **all TCS rates including scrap (code 1073)** — directly relevant to a manufacturer and absent; the no-PAN penal rate's shape (per-section lookup vs "higher of 20% or twice").

**Statutory framing — q65–70:** **what does a Rule 11(g) auditor actually accept — a hash chain, or append-only grants plus a signed daily digest?** (PHASE5 prices the difference at ~0.5 engineer-months; we ship both until answered.) Retention period for the audit log and statutory documents. Labour Welfare Fund applicability, rate, states (**no doctype, field or mention anywhere**). Statutory Bonus eligibility ceiling, percentage, set-on/set-off (absent; bonus is only an ad-hoc Additional Salary). Minimum-wage floor validation per state per skill category. Statutory register formats (muster roll, wage register).

**Added in review — q71–q96.** Every one is a statutory assertion a reviewer or a research pass made that this document may not simply adopt, and each has a cheap design accommodation already made above so that a "yes" costs nothing and a "no" costs nothing. q71–q84 came from the first review round, q85–q90 from the second, **q91–q93 from the Phase 7 market-research pass**, and **q94–q96 from the Phase 8 deep pass — which is the one that read the statute directly, so its questions are narrower and harder: three of them exist precisely because a primary source answered the easy half and exposed a mechanic nobody had asked about.**

| # | Question | What it settles |
|---|---|---|
| q71 | The full statutory document set a manufacturer must be able to issue — Bill of Supply (s.31(3)(c)/Rule 49), Receipt Voucher, Payment Voucher (s.31(3)(g)), Refund Voucher, Revised Invoice — and the Rule 46 / Rule 49 / Rule 55 particulars each must **print** | §3.2's document list and the print contract. Neither document specified Rule 46 particulars anywhere, and the DC has Rule 55 particulars and a triplicate marking that appear nowhere |

Getting these wrong means the customer's auditor qualifies the report we built the log to satisfy |

Which applies to a job-shop fabricator taking advances? | §6.5 A6, the Receipt Voucher, and GSTR-1 tables 11A/11B. These are what a CA fails an invoice on at a glance, and three of the four are document and series structure rather than layout |

If yes, that is a second set of books in Excel beside the one carrying the Rule 11(g) claim — a qualifying question in the first sales call (§8.4) and a v1.5 scope item, not a surprise at audit |

The mechanism is the same either way; the answer picks the branch |

Revision 2 migrated money and nothing else, so every statutory cumulative this product is sold on started at zero on go-live day |

Wrong vintage is silently wrong for every supplier near a boundary |

| q95 | **IMS**: which of Accept / Reject / Pending are available through the **GSP API** versus portal-only; whether a **Pending** record carries forward and for how long; what a 2B **recomputation** does to a reconciliation a user has already reviewed and signed off; and whether "No Action → deemed accepted" has any exception | §4.1C's purchase-side data model. A per-invoice action state machine over a recomputable draft is a different object from a monthly snapshot, and the difference is not retrofittable once inward records exist. **[PHASE8 PRIMARY on the mechanics; the API surface is unknown]** |
| q96 | **Labour Codes**: (a) are the four Codes in force and are the **state rules** notified — cite the commencement S.O., not a backgrounder's publication date; (b) does the **50%-of-total-remuneration add-back** apply identically to PF, ESI and gratuity? | §4.1E's wage base. A payroll engine that sums whatever is labelled "basic" is wrong under the Code's definition, and the fix is a rule, not a field. **[PHASE8 PRIMARY on the wage definition; commencement unevidenced]** |

**Blocking relationship, updated.** q24–33 block J5's *constants* (the mechanism now ships regardless — §5.3) and **are on the Stage 0 critical path with a named date**, because §7 Stage 3 builds the register against them. q1–23 block the payroll wage sheet computing anything. q34–51 and q79, q82, q83 block the GST rule table's sign-off, which blocks GA. q65, q75–q78 and q85 determine whether half the audit hardening is necessary weight and whether the Rule 11(g) claim is sayable at all. q71, q72, q81, q86 and q89 block v1 scope decisions already made provisionally above. **q87 and q90 are Stage 0 questions rather than compliance-table questions:** the first decides whether we are selling into a segment whose books we cannot fully keep, and the second decides what the Tally bridge must actually carry — which is Q3's real scope, not its optimistic one. **q91 and q93 belong in the CA's first batch alongside q24–33:** both are seed values for rules that ship in v1 (T16's reporting window; the MSME bands behind 43B(h)), both are cheap to get right and silent when wrong. **q92 blocks nothing in v1** — it settles only how the 2B worklist labels its ITC delta. **q94 joins q24–33 on the Stage 0 critical path** — the extension model is three columns and one branch in M8, but shipping J5 without it means firing a deemed-supply posting at a customer holding a Commissioner's order, so the *mechanics* answer is needed before Stage 3 closes even though the *field* is added regardless. **q95 blocks the purchase-side data model, not a rate** — it is a Stage 0/Stage 2 question aimed at the GSP sandbox alongside Q4, and it is the only new question here whose wrong answer costs a migration rather than a data change. **q96 blocks the payroll wage base**, so it sits with q1–q23 in the CA's payroll batch.

### 5.6 Where the plan was more confident than its evidence

The plan's own §2 discipline — "every load-bearing claim cites its report" — was not applied to the plan. Seven claims from revision 1, plus four the second review round found still standing, now corrected in place:

| Revision 1 said | What the scout actually supports | Where it is fixed |
|---|---|---|
| The §143 invoice "**by statute** bears the original despatch date" | PHASE4 §5.4 J5 is inside a **[NOT IN CODE]** block; §5.5 q24–33 says "we assume — confirm" | §3.4 — current posting date + `supply_date`, claim tagged [VERIFY] |
| "GSTR-1 **requires** cancelled numbers disclosed in-sequence" | PHASE4 §9.2 says gaps "are an audit finding" and describes the Documents Issued *shape*. No statutory citation. Rule 46(b) requires a consecutive series, which is a different claim | §3.3 and §3.6 — design kept, justification downgraded, auditor made acknowledgeable |
| "Factory wage bands sit squarely under the ESI ceiling" | PHASE4 §6.3: the wage threshold is **[NOT IN CODE]**; "ESI" appears in hrms only in a test fixture and a comment | §5.3 — struck; q11 settles it |
| "MSME/Udyam → 45-day due date → 43B(h)" | PHASE4 §8 gap 8 — absent everywhere. Stated without the 15-day/no-agreement branch (which A8 does carry) and without the micro/small-only scope | §6.5 A8 — scope and branch made explicit |
| M8's "unused customer RM returned at the customer's declared value (**Rule 55**)" as settled | PHASE4 §5.2: a docstring citation in reference code; q24–33 lists Rule 55 valuation as an open question | §6.7 M8 — tagged [VERIFY] |
| "**MA is Tally's default** which reduces migration friction" | No source anywhere in the scout. PHASE6 says no Tally format exists in any repo | §3.4 — struck; q74 |
| "Frappe's `Version` fails **six of eight** Rule 11(g) criteria" | PHASE5 §5.2 lists **eight gaps of which six are graded Fatal**. There is no eight-criterion Rule 11(g) checklist anywhere | §3.5 — restated |

**Four more, found in the second round.**

| Revision 2 said | What the scout actually supports | Where it is fixed |
|---|---|---|
| Rounding is "half-away-from-zero (**Indian statutory convention**)", stated flat in §3.1 | No report cites a rounding statute. Both references implement it, which is evidence of practice | §3.1 — **[VERIFY] q85**, and the mode becomes a rule-store row |
| Moving average is "accepted under **AS-2**" (§4.2) and "**is Tally's default**" (§4.1D) | AS-2 appears nowhere in PHASE2, PHASE3 or PHASE4's evidence base; the Tally claim was already struck from §3.4 in revision 2 and left standing in two other sections | §4.1D and §4.2 — both citations struck; **[VERIFY] q74** |
| The MSME summary line in §4.1C: "Udyam → **45-day** due date → 43B(h)" | A8 itself carries the 15-day / 45-day branch and the micro-and-small-only scope; the summary flattened both | §4.1C — restated to match A8 |
| N16: a transfer to the same entity's other GSTIN "**is** a supply" | PHASE4 §5.2 offers a **[CODE]** observation about a query filter, not a statutory statement | §5.1 N16 — **[VERIFY] q81** |

Plus the one non-statutory drift of the same kind: **the SeaRM tenancy citation** in §3.7, which cited a repo no scout report examined, in support of the least-settled decision in the plan. Struck.

**Four more, found by the Phase 8 deep pass — and note that three of them are the plan being *under*-confident or imprecise rather than over-confident, which is a different failure and just as costly in a sales call.**

| Revision 4 said | What the evidence actually supports | Where it is fixed |
|---|---|---|
| The free stack has **nothing** on job work (implied by §8.2 W2, §8.3 and §8.4 answer 10) | india-compliance **ships a GST Job Work Stock Movement report**; a maintainer closed a request for one as `not_planned` because it already existed. What is absent is the **clock, the deemed-supply posting and the interest** | §8.2 W2, §8.3, §8.4 answer 10, §4.1D, §6.1 — narrowed everywhere to *"a stock-movement report is not a liability engine"* |
| **"Zero record of a §143 notice or penalty"** | A **Madras HC judgment of 08.06.2026 upheld an extended-limitation s.74 demand** on this exact failure, and a taxpayer reports a s.125 penalty levied at audit. **The claim was true of the evidence then and is false now** — but the demand side is unchanged and must be stated separately | §8.2 W2, §8.6(c) |
| The §143 window is a **1-year / 3-year clock** | It is **extendable by the Commissioner** by a further 1 / 2 years on sufficient cause (s.143, second proviso). A hard-coded trigger **manufactures liability** | §6.7 M8, §5.5 q94 |
| The e-way-bill carve-out for inter-state job work is an *"understood requirement"* **[VERIFY]** | It is **Rule 138's third proviso, read on the CBIC rule text** — an e-way bill is required *"irrespective of the value of the consignment"* | §5.1 N14, §6.7 M8; q82 narrows to the unregistered-job-worker case |

**And the discipline extended, because labelling statute is not enough.** The second round found four **DDL fragments that would not have run or would not have meant what they said** — an `audit_log` primary key that omitted the partition key, a nullable `cost_center_id` contradicting three sections that call it NOT NULL, a merge key naming a `finance_book` column that does not exist, and a numbering retry loop that would have failed on its first retry. A plan whose credibility rests on being specific has to be specific *correctly*; from here, **every SQL and pseudocode block in this document is expected to be executable as written**, and a reviewer finding one that is not is finding a defect of the same class as an unlabelled statutory claim.
---

## 6. BUSINESS RULES BACKLOG

Rule IDs: **L** ledger · **T** tax · **A** AR/AP · **V** inventory/valuation · **M** manufacturing · **P** procurement · **S** sales · **D** deferred. Sources cited per rule. Every rule below lives in **typed code**, not metadata — none is expressible as configuration.

**Selection principle.** Two filters, in order. (1) *Statutory gate:* PHASE4 §1.1–1.3 lists 14 general + 9 manufacturer-specific capabilities without which the product cannot be sold at all; any rule they depend on is v1 by force. (2) *Silent-wrong-number weight:* among the rest, a rule is v1 if getting it wrong produces a number that looks plausible, posts cleanly, and is discovered months later by a CA. A rule that produces a visible error or a blocked save is cheaper and can wait. Filter (2) is why bank auto-matching and dunning are deferred while outstanding recomputation and horizon-dated reorder are Wave 0/1.

### 6.1 The twelve most expensive rules to get wrong

Ranked by cost of *silent* error, not effort.

| # | Rule | Silent failure mode |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **T4** `is_inter_state()` **and the four-head split** | CGST+SGST on an inter-state invoice, or CGST+SGST where CGST+UTGST is due. It posts, prints, files. Recipient loses ITC and churns; you learn from the customer |
| 2 | **V1** COGS = stock value delta | Gross margin wrong on every return, back-dated receipt and landed cost. Trial balance still balances |
| 3 | **A1** outstanding derived, never incremented | AR ageing drifts on every cancel/amend; collections chase the wrong customers |
| 4 | **L9/L10** audit log + effective-dated rule store | A rate change silently re-computes historical documents on any recompute path. Unfixable retroactively |
| 5 | **T6** GSTR section stamped per line at post time | "The single most common source of filing mismatches" (PHASE4 §9.3) |
| 6 | **P2** reorder evaluated at the lead-time horizon, `quantity_in_progress` including open RFQs | Double-ordering. Real cash, no error message. ERPNext fires on today's `projected_qty` |
| 7 | **T2/T3** inclusive back-solve + per-line rounding | Every inclusive-priced invoice wrong by cents that accumulate into a 3B-vs-books mismatch |
| 8 | **V6/V7** negative-stock prohibition + valuation cascade terminating in a throw | An AVCO average poisoned by a negative-stock window stays wrong forever; a silent fallback to standard cost puts a fictional number into inventory *and* COGS |
| 8b | **V16** the `(item, location)` advisory lock (§3.4) | Two concurrent GRNs both read the same prior average and both write a wrong one. Nothing errors, nothing balances differently, and the valuation is wrong forever. Revision 1 had no serialization design at all for the hardest concurrency problem in the system |
| 9 | **M8** job-work register with quantity-level FIFO matching | Deemed supply under §143 with interest, found at assessment — **now a documented outcome, not a hypothetical: a Madras High Court judgment of 08.06.2026 upheld an extended-limitation s.74 demand on exactly this failure** (§8.6(c)). The free stack ships a stock-movement *report*; **no reference implements the clock, the deemed-supply posting or the interest** |
| 10 | **S2/S4** billable-after-return; returns inheriting the original conversion rate | An unrelated credit note re-opens an order; credit notes manufacture phantom FX gain |
| 11 | **L8** gapless numbering; cancellation never returns a number | An unexplained gap is an audit finding. `SEQUENCE` burns numbers on rollback, invisible until filing |
| 12 | **T7** RCM as ±100% repartition keyed on HSN/SAC | RCM from *registered* suppliers (freight, legal, security) missed entirely by the counterparty-status heuristic both references use |

### 6.2 Where ERPNext and Odoo differ — the twelve picks

| # | ERPNext (PHASE2) | Odoo (PHASE3) | Pick |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Inclusive tax as slope+intercept accumulated across the whole table incl. on-previous-row chains and per-qty charges | Three passes with **batching** of consecutive taxes sharing `amount_type`/`price_include`/`include_base_amount`, four closed forms | **Synthesise (T2).** Odoo's batching answers *which taxes share a base* (why two 10% inclusive taxes divide by 1.20, not 1.10 twice) — not present in ERPNext at all. ERPNext's slope/intercept is the general closed form and the only one putting a per-unit charge inside an inclusive back-solve. India needs both: CGST+SGST share one base; `cess_non_advol` is an intercept term |
| 2 | Rounding by error diffusion; residual dumped on the last row | Delta smoothing: round once, allocate `round(factor·cents)`, leftover to the **largest** factors | **Split (T3).** Error diffusion for line-wise ordered distribution; delta smoothing for weight-wise splits. **Reject last-row dumping entirely** — ERPNext does it in ≥4 places with a literal `# TODO: fix rounding difference issues` |
| 3 | COGS at stock movement (`stock_value_difference` at the SLE) | COGS at invoice time, paired `display_type='cogs'` lines | **ERPNext (V9).** Goods move on a challan weeks before an invoice exists and PHASE4 M1 makes EWB-on-movement a gate, so the invoice-time window is the *normal* case here; invoice-time COGS needs a delivered-not-invoiced interim account no SME will reconcile; the CA expects stock and GL to agree at every date. **Accepted cost:** imperfect revenue/COGS matching across a month end. **Mitigation:** ship a delivered-not-invoiced report in v1 (cheap — S1 already holds both numbers) |
| 4 | Append-only SLE, Bin as rebuildable cache | Recompute-on-read (v19 deleted the valuation layer) | **ERPNext (V1).** PHASE3 agrees against itself: §6.17 calls recompute-on-read O(history) and §7 Q1 recommends the pre-19 append-only layer, calling v19 "a cautionary tale" |
| 5 | Reconciliation re-opens the submitted payment, splits the child row, rebuilds the PLE | Partial reconcile as an **edge**; connected component reaching zero = full reconcile via union-find | **Odoo (A2)** — ERPNext's method mutates submitted documents, which PHASE2 §5 itself lists under Avoid. Edge deletion makes unreconcile trivially correct. **But take ERPNext's optimistic-concurrency check** ("modified after you pulled it") and live re-validation of outstanding at submit, and **compute** the matching number rather than denormalising it (PHASE3 §6.7 records the bug the denormalisation caused) |
| 6 | `status_updater` config table, recompute by re-SUM, `min()`-cap, allowance ladder | Tri-quantity ledger; every status a pure function | **Odoo's model, ERPNext's mechanics (S1).** PHASE2 §5 lists its own approach under Avoid ("SO has `status` + three more + four `per_*` fields… guaranteed drift"). Store three quantities, derive status; keep re-SUM (makes cancel/amend correct for free) and the allowance ladder (real warehouses over-ship) |
| 7 | Reorder fires on today's `projected_qty` | `max(min,max) − virtual_available(at horizon) − quantity_in_progress` | **Odoo (P2).** ERPNext's timing is simply wrong — it compares today's stock against a need materialising after the lead time |
| 8 | RCM as a document flag + mirrored pair | ±100% repartition emitting the mirror | **Odoo (T7)** — PHASE4 §2.5 calls it "cleaner than a flag; consider it". Same mechanism gives ineligible-ITC routing for free. **Fix both:** key applicability on HSN/SAC, not counterparty status |
| 9 | Landed cost: cancel and replay the receipt's SLE+GL | Pro-rate by the move's **`remaining_qty`**; sold goods never retro-valued | **Odoo (V8).** Forward-only is cheaper and legally safer — retro-valuing sold goods restates COGS in a period whose GSTR-1 is filed. ERPNext's replay also flips docstatus to 2 and back |
| 10 | Scheduling: greedy bucket-pack of overlapping time logs | **Interval algebra**; a scheduled work order *is* a calendar leave on the workcenter resource | **Odoo (M7).** One primitive serves shifts, holidays, leave and capacity. PHASE6 makes attendance→wage-sheet an MVP blocker so `Intervals` gets built anyway; reusing it for capacity is free. Retaining the payload means you still know which attendance produced each slice — which is what makes a wage sheet defensible |
| 11 | MRP netting: safety stock, MOQ, **then** purchase-UOM `ROUND_CEILING`, transfer-first/purchase-remainder | Declarative rule engine resolved by walking the location tree | **ERPNext at v1 (P1), Odoo's engine deferred (D3).** PHASE3 admits rule resolution is "effectively undebuggable" and makes an `explain(procurement)` trace a *precondition* of adoption. A single-factory SME does not need route resolution. Take Odoo's horizon (P2) and in-batch guard (P3) regardless |
| 12 | BOM explosion keyed **(item_code, operation)**; phantoms merged inline | Only phantoms flatten — a nested normal BoM is a *procurement* boundary; convert through each child's **batch** quantity; round leaves **up** in the component's own UoM | **Both (M1).** Odoo's semantics with ERPNext's key |

### 6.3 Wave 0 — Foundation. Nothing may post until these exist.

- **L1 Single posting chokepoint.** The fixed pipeline of §3.4. No document type may bypass it or add to it. *Why v1:* it is the only place the GST account validations can be made unbypassable. *Naive failure:* each document type grows its own posting path and one of them (typically the stock entry or the RCM self-invoice) skips the tax-head check. *(PHASE2 §3.1)*
- **L2 Reverse, never delete.** `on_cancel` on a ledger row throws; cancellation writes swapped contras dated in the current open period. No toggle. *(PHASE2 §3.1, rejecting its `enable_immutable_ledger` flag; PHASE4 §7)*
- **L3 Balance invariant, two layers.** Precision-derived tolerance; round-off line; **re-check after insert**; plus the deferred DB constraint trigger. *(PHASE2 §3.1 corrected by §5; PHASE3 Bet 2)*
- **L4 Merge key includes every dimension and the source line reference.** Zero rows dropped after merge; exceptions modelled as a **row type**, never a string match on voucher name. *Naive failure:* merging two lines differing only in cost centre — company P&L right, departmental P&L wrong, nothing errors. *(PHASE2 §3.1, §5)*
- **L5 No negative amounts stored.** *(PHASE2 §3.1)* — otherwise any `SUM(debit)` report silently under-reports.
- **L6 Amounts in both transaction and company currency on every line; never reconverted at report time.** Party currency permanently fixed to that of its first GL entry per company. *(PHASE2 §3.1)*
- **L7 Period gates, four orthogonal** (§3.4 step 2), including the GSTR-1-filed lock. *(PHASE2 §3.1; PHASE4 §2.6 V12)*
- **L8 Statutory numbering** — full spec in §3.6. *(PHASE6 §1.1; PHASE4 G7, §9.2)*
- **L9 Append-only audit log at the persistence layer** — full spec in §3.5. *(PHASE4 §7, §9.1; PHASE5 §5.2)*
- **L10 Effective-dated statutory rule store — schema in §3.9.** Every threshold, rate, slab, code list, digit requirement, UQC, port code, pincode band is data with a validity window. No gaps and no overlaps (enforced by a GiST exclusion constraint, not asserted), **no silent fallback — `resolve()` throws, never computes zero**. Historical rows never mutated. A document computes from the ruleset in force on its posting date and records a **ruleset**-version stamp, because one GL row is produced by four rules and one uuid cannot stamp four. *Naive failure:* constants in code — a slab change silently recomputes every historical document on any recompute path. **Corollary the review forced: this rule means nothing can post until the store is populated, so populating it is a Stage 0 cost line (§3.9), not a downstream task.** *(PHASE4 §9.4, §8 gap 5)*
- **L11 Opening balances — and opening *statutory state*, which is the half revision 2 was missing. [VERIFY] q90.** Every opening entry balances against a **placeholder balance-sheet account, never a P&L account**, swept later by an explicit Opening JE, and every path runs under `is_migration` posting mode: an audited, role-gated mode that may write into a period marked filed-elsewhere, with a mandatory reason, and which is the **only** thing that may. Paths:
  1. **Open AR/AP at invoice level — mandatory, not summarised.** Revision 2 specified "one summarised opening invoice per party" while §4.1F promised "open items", and the summarised version is the one that was specified. It destroys the base that A8 (MSME 15/45-day and the 43B(h) report), A9 (Rule 37 / 180-day), A5 (ageing consumption order) and the s.16(4) claim-period walk all require — every one of them a feature this document sells. Open items come in one row per open invoice with its document date, due date and party, `is_opening`, **each row in its own savepoint**.
  2. **Opening stock reconciliation**, difference account forced to Asset/Liability.
  3. **Trial-balance CSV → one balanced Opening JE**, server-side debit==credit validation.
  4. **Open job-work challans** (challan no, date, GSTIN pair, item, qty, item type, `clock_start_date`, declared value, chain parent). Without it a client migrating in October with 40 challans and material at job workers for seven months sees a **green §143 dashboard over their actual exposure** — we would sell the clock and ship a false all-clear to exactly the customers who need it.
  5. **The statutory cumulatives, all of which reset to zero on go-live without an import path and each of which produces a wrong return in the first year:** the **filed-return register** per GSTIN per period (which is what §3.4 gate (d) and the s.16(4) walk read, and which had nothing to read on day one); **194Q / 206C(1H) YTD purchase value per PAN** (A7 aggregates by PAN and would under-deduct for the rest of the year); **TDS deducted YTD per section per deductee** for 24Q/26Q continuity; **payroll YTD** per employee — taxable earnings, exemptions, TDS, the annualise-and-spread state; **ESI contribution-period state** and the mid-period continuation flag; **gratuity service days**; **aggregate turnover YTD**; and **electronic credit and cash ledger opening balances**.
  CoA import refuses to run once any GL entry exists. Opening series state (`start_ordinal`, `migrated_from/to`) is set here, per §3.6. *(PHASE6 §1.2)*
- **L12 Monotonic ledger sequence as the total order.** *(PHASE2 §3.4, §6)*
- **L13 Back-dating policy** — §3.4. **There is now no back-dating exception at all:** the §143 deemed-supply invoice posts in the current period carrying `supply_date`, so the one case revision 1 carved out no longer needs a carve-out. The single named period-gate exemption is the `deemed_supply_143` voucher type passing gates (b) and (d) with a mandatory reason, which is not the same thing as back-dating. *(PHASE2 §6, §7 Q1)*

### 6.4 Wave 1 — Tax and the sellable invoice

- **T1 Tax computation is a pure function.** `compute(taxes, unit_price, qty, precision, rounding_method, special_mode) → tax_details` — no ORM records, no document, no settings lookup. One implementation, server-side. Explicitly rejecting ERPNext's 1,420-line controller that recomputes the document up to three times per save and mirrors commission math into JS where one copy is apparent dead code. *(PHASE3 §4.1; PHASE2 §5)*
- **T2 Inclusive back-solve — batched slope+intercept.** Flatten groups, sort by `(sequence, id)`, batch consecutive taxes on matching `amount_type`/`price_include`/`include_base_amount`, accumulate slope (Σ ad-valorem) and intercept (Σ per-unit × qty), back-solve `net = (amount − Σintercept)/(1 + Σslope)` once per batch. Passes ordered fixed-desc → included-desc → excluded-asc. *Naive failure:* `amount/(1+rate)` — wrong for chained taxes and per-quantity charges. *(synthesis: PHASE3 §4.1 + PHASE2 §3.2)*
- **T3 Rounding, three rules.** (a) compute raw, keep unrounded for the e-invoice payload, round exactly once; (b) line-wise distribution by error diffusion so the breakup sums exactly; (c) weight-wise splits by delta smoothing, leftover cents to the largest factors. Residual beyond a precision-derived tolerance **throws**. **Never dump a residual on the last row.** For GST, **round per line, not per document.** *Note on an apparent conflict:* PHASE4 §9.3 states a *statutory* rule about the tax amount; PHASE2/PHASE3 describe *numerical* techniques for reconciling parts to a whole. They do not conflict, but a distribution step must never alter a per-line tax amount. And on the GSTR-1 residue: **reject the reference's workaround of forcing the difference onto the largest HSN row** — that deliberately misstates a statutory row. Carry an explicit `rounding_difference` bucket. *(PHASE3 §4.2; PHASE2 §3.2, §6; PHASE4 §2.7, §4.1)*
- **T4 Tax-head determination as one predicate. [GATE]** Source state = company GSTIN[:2] for sales/payment; supplier GSTIN[:2] for purchase/subcontracting (Overseas → 96, unregistered → supplier address state, fallback company); bill-from GSTIN[:2] for stock entries. SEZ counterparty is **always** inter-state. Inter-state → IGST only; intra-state → **CGST + (SGST or UTGST) at exactly half each**, the second head chosen by testing the destination state code against the rule-store table of Union Territories without a legislature (**[VERIFY] q79**). Revision 1 knew only three heads and would have put the wrong head on every intra-UT supply — the #1 silent failure in §6.1, in a manufacturing belt inside the ICP. State is the 2-digit code, 96 and 97 real records. Six blocking validations at submit: no CGST/SGST inter-state; no IGST intra-state; intra-state must have **both**; no GST row on "On Previous Row Amount"; "On Item Quantity" only for `cess_non_advol`; sales use only Output/Sales-RCM/Output-Refund accounts, purchases only Input/Purchase-RCM. **Two reference anti-patterns not to copy:** skipping validations 1–3 when the *company's own* address is SEZ (that disables the rule rather than applying SEZ logic), and returning FALSE when PoS is blank — **fail loudly instead**. *(PHASE4 §2.3, §9.3)*
- **T5 Tax character is authoritative data, not derived from tags.** Report tags derive from it, never the reverse — explicitly rejecting Odoo's direction, where editing a repartition tag silently changes a tax's identity and the HSN summary follows. *(PHASE4 §9.3)*
- **T6 GSTR section stamped per line at post time.** *(PHASE4 §9.3)*
- **T7 Repartition and reverse charge. [GATE]** Ordered invoice and refund repartition lists of identical length and percentages; positive tax factors sum +100, negatives (if any) sum −100 — that is the RCM pattern, emitting a mirror leg with inverted amount, forced price-excluded. Assert `Σ(non-RCM GST) + Σ(RCM booked) == 0` to 2 dp. Same mechanism routes **ineligible ITC** into expense or valuation. **Applicability keyed on HSN/SAC or expense category**, with counterparty status as an additional trigger, not the sole one. *(PHASE3 §4.3; PHASE4 §2.5, M5)*
- **T8 Exemption is not zero. [GATE]** `gst_treatment ∈ {Taxable, Zero-Rated, Nil-Rated, Exempted, Non-GST}`; Nil/Exempt/Non-GST are **three distinct zero-rate tax records**, never blank. Sales to SEZ/Overseas → Zero-Rated; import purchase → Taxable; no GST rows → keep Exempted/Non-GST if set, else Nil-Rated. *(PHASE4 §2.4; corroborated by ERPNext's `NOT_APPLICABLE_TAX` sentinel)*
- **T9 Line coherence and rate consistency, blocking**, with a **proportional** tolerance — the reference's flat ₹1 per line is meaningless on a ₹5 line and generous on a ₹5,00,000 one. *(PHASE4 §2.4)*
- **T10 HSN/SAC. [GATE]** 4/6/8 digits only, never 5 or 7; company floor ∈ {4,6,8}; `99…` ⇒ service; warning at draft, **hard throw at submit** on sales documents; mandatory 4/6/8 for e-invoice and Overseas/SEZ purchase invoices. Independently editable on the line and **not copied on item duplicate**. *(PHASE4 §2.4, §9.6)*
- **T11 Item Tax Template resolution.** Bounded templates preferred, sorted `valid_from` desc, matched on tax category; **an already-valid template on the row is never silently changed**; intra-state rows must have `|rate| = gst_rate/2`. *(PHASE2 §3.2; PHASE4 §2.4)*
- **T12 Fixed charges do not shrink with a discount; discount is two-pass.** `Actual` and `On Item Quantity` charges and any percentage tax chained onto them are excluded from the discountable base; header discount distributed proportional to line net, then the whole calculation re-runs. *Naive failure:* a header discount silently reduces freight, changing the tax base on a document that already printed. *(PHASE2 §3.2)*
- **T13 Cess as a real formula.** `cess` ad valorem + `cess_non_advol` per-unit, with statutory `max(ad_valorem, specific × qty)` implemented as a formula — both references encode "higher of X% or ₹Y" **only in the tax's display name**, so anyone copying that structure silently computes the wrong cess. *(PHASE4 §2.7, §8 gap 15)*
- **T14 Document-level blocking validations V1–V11.** Company GSTIN and PoS mandatory; category consistent with the party GSTIN; **no GST when company GSTIN == party GSTIN**; no input GST from a Registered Composition dealer; no GST on export/SEZ without payment; no GST on a non-RCM purchase from a supplier with no GSTIN; supplier bill number mandatory unless Unregistered; post-submit changes to party address/GSTIN/category/PoS blocked once an IRN or EWB exists. Skipped only when company country ≠ India, company is Unregistered, or the document is an opening entry. *(PHASE4 §2.6)*
- **T15 HSN-wise summary computed server-side once.** Grouped by `(HSN, UoM, combined GST rate)` where combined = the **sum** of distinct igst/cgst/sgst rates, so a 2.5+2.5 intra-state line groups with an IGST-5 line. Cess excluded from the key, reported separately. UQC from the closed list with `OTH-OTHERS` fallback. *(PHASE4 §2.7; note Odoo implements this twice, Python and JS — a flagged correctness hazard)*
- **T16 IRN reporting time limit — blocking. [VERIFY] q91.** For a tenant above the applicable turnover threshold, an invoice whose `document_date` is more than **N** days old **cannot be submitted for IRN generation**: the attempt is blocked at submit with the remediation named, never silently queued for a retry that will never succeed. **N and the turnover threshold are rule-store rows with applicability dates, never constants** — currently N = 30 days at turnover ≥ ₹10 Cr, effective 1 April 2025 **[PHASE7 VERIFIED, single source]**. Note the interaction with D17: this rule reads `document_date`, not `posting_date`, which is exactly why the two are separate columns. *Cost: one rule row and one validation. Cost of omission: a customer above ₹10 Cr discovers on day 31, in production, that an invoice they have already issued can never carry an IRN.* *(PHASE7 §7.1)*
- **T17 2B reconciliation is a standing worklist, not a month-end batch.** 2B is generated on the 14th and 3B is due on the 20th — **six days** **[PHASE7 VERIFIED, 2 sources]**. The schema and workflow therefore model a *persistent* purchase-mismatch list with per-line ageing and status, refreshed on every available 2B pull, not a report regenerated on demand. Cadence is gated on §10 Q4 (§5.4): if 2B download inherits an OTP-bound session, the refresh is attended and scheduled rather than continuous — **the worklist object is identical either way, which is why this can be decided now and the cadence later.** *(PHASE7 §7.2)*

### 6.5 Wave 1b — AR/AP

- **A1 Party subledger append-only; outstanding derived** by summing rows keyed on the voucher and rows keyed against it. Never incremented. *(PHASE2 §3.1, §5)*
- **A2 Reconciliation as an edge graph** — partial reconciliation is an edge with a positive company-currency amount; component reaching zero is full; matching identifier computed by union-find, **not denormalised**; unreconcile is edge deletion. At submit, re-fetch live outstanding for every reference and throw if it changed. *(PHASE3 §4.4; concurrency guards from PHASE2 §3.8)*
- **A3 Payment terms schedule.** Base = `(rounded_total or grand_total) − write_off − advances`; three due-date bases; a due date before the posting date is clamped up; duplicates rejected; total matched within a **precision-derived** tolerance (the reference hard-codes `0.1` regardless of currency). *(PHASE2 §3.8 + its audit correction)*
- **A4 Per-installment overdue.** Overdue when `(total − outstanding) < Σ` of past-due scheduled installments, not merely when the final due date passed. *Naive failure:* ageing understates overdue on every instalment invoice. *(PHASE2 §3.3, §3.7)*
- **A5 AR ageing consumption order.** A payment against a credit note is **re-keyed onto the invoice that note was issued against**; advances allocated at invoicing are deducted before payment-term allocation; payments then credit notes consumed term-by-term in due-date order. *(PHASE2 §3.8)*
- **A6 Advances, with taxability as a rule, not an assumption. [VERIFY] q83.** An advance is an order line flagged as a down payment, re-emitted on the final invoice with `quantity = −1` and reversed tax data (PHASE3 §4.10 — Odoo down-payment *mechanics*). *Revision 1 said "GST is payable on advances" as a bare statement of law, cited to a report about Odoo mechanics, with no [VERIFY] tag, in a document whose §9.3 says a rule with no provenance is a defect.* The understood position is that tax on advance receipts was **withdrawn for goods** (Notification 66/2017-CT) and **persists for services** — and the target segment is goods manufacturers, so revision 1's rule would have created phantom output liability on every advance a fabricator takes. Corrected: **advance taxability is an effective-dated rule-store flag keyed on supply type**, defaulting to not-taxable for goods, and the **Receipt Voucher** (§3.2) is the document that carries it. **GSTR-1 tables 11A/11B populate only when the flag is on** — both the voucher type and the tables were absent from revision 1, and job-shop fabricators take advances constantly.
- **A7 Withholding (TDS/TCS) as its own reconciling subledger.** `remaining = cumulative_threshold − YTD under-withheld`, capped by a single-transaction threshold; under tax-on-excess only the excess is taxed and **the exempt slice is recorded as its own entry with a reason code**; once any settled entry exists in the period the threshold is permanently crossed. **PAN-level aggregation across party records.** 194Q and 206C(1H) mutually exclusive, enforced. Rates, thresholds and **section numbering itself** live in L10. *Do not copy* ERPNext's `_adjust_against_old_entries`, which PHASE2 §6 records as throwing whenever amounts match exactly on the last entry. *(PHASE2 §3.2; PHASE4 §6.8)*
- **A8 MSME / §43B(h). [VERIFY] q34-block.** Udyam classification on the party, **scoped to micro and small enterprises only** — medium enterprises are understood to fall outside 43B(h), and §4.1C's one-line summary omitted both that scope and the no-agreement branch. Due-date derivation: **15 days where there is no written agreement, 45 days where there is**; year-end disallowance report of unpaid MSME invoices. Nearly free once A3 exists. *(PHASE4 §8 gap 8 — absent everywhere; "MSME No." exists only as a print-header label)*
- **A9 Rule 37 / 180-day ITC ageing report** in v1; automated reversal in v1.5. *(PHASE4 §8 gap 4)*
- **A10 Bank statement import + clearance (manual matching at v1).** **Import first** — per-bank CSV/Excel profiles reusing the §4.1F mapping-memory engine, dedupe on `(account, value_date, amount, bank_ref)`, statement rows as an immutable staging table. Then: `allocable = the voucher's bank GL amount − prior allocations`; over-allocation throws; clearance date set only when every bank GL line is fully allocated and equals `max(transaction date, latest prior allocation date)`. Revision 1 shipped the matching half against a filtered candidate list with **nothing to ingest the statement**, and a factory reconciles a bank account in week one. *(PHASE2 §3.8)*

### 6.6 Wave 2 — Inventory and valuation

- **V1 Ledger is truth; caches rebuildable; COGS is a value delta.** GL debits inventory by `stock_value_difference` and credits COGS by its negative. **Never `rate × qty`.** *(PHASE2 §3.4; PHASE3 §7 Q1 concurs)*
- **V2 Inventory as double-entry between locations** with virtual counterparties typed by usage ∈ {supplier, customer, inventory, production, transit, view, internal}. *Why v1:* it makes "goods at the job worker remain the principal's inventory" fall out of the location type. *(PHASE3 Bet 3 — "build it first, before any warehouse feature")*
- **V3 Valued-location predicate.** A location counts toward company valuation iff it has a company **and** usage ∈ {internal, transit}. One predicate defines in/out for every move. *Naive failure:* per-document-type in/out logic; one type gets it backwards and value drifts silently. *(PHASE3 §4.5)*
- **V4 Ownership excludes from valuation.** A quant or move line owned by another party is physically present, financially absent. *Why v1:* inbound job work (being the job worker with customer-supplied material) is a real Indian SME business and PHASE2 §7 Q9 notes most open ERPs omit it. *(PHASE3 §4.5)*
- **V5 FIFO queue mechanics** — spec'd now, shipped v1.1 (v1 is MA-only). `[qty, rate]` bins; removal returns consumed bins so the caller derives value from the delta; **purchase returns consume the rate-matched bin, not the queue head**; negative bins when the queue empties; merge into the last bin at equal rate; `round_off_if_near_zero(precision=7)` on every qty and value. *(PHASE2 §3.4 — 267 lines, zero framework imports, "the most portable file in the repo")*
- **V6 Negative stock forbidden; AVCO reset as a safety net.** `diff = balance + actual_qty − reserved` against `max(10^-float_precision, 0.0001)`, where `balance` is the **locked `stock_balance` row**, not a cache (§3.4) and not a re-scan of the ledger; **reserved stock genuinely reduces what you may consume.** The AVCO rule (previous qty ≤ 0 ⇒ average resets to the incoming price) is implemented as a safety net, not a routine path. *Reasoning on a disagreement:* PHASE3 §6 flags as **[unsettled]** whether the AVCO reset is correct under partial returns spanning a reset. Forbidding negative stock makes the question not arise — the cheapest available resolution of an unverified rule. *(PHASE2 §3.4; PHASE3 §4.5)*
- **V7 Inbound value cascade terminating in a throw.** manual override → invoice/bill → production cost → source order line → original move (returns) → standard cost, **each step consuming remaining quantity and appending a human-readable justification**; then landed costs. Under perpetual inventory, exhausting the cascade **throws with a remediation message naming the options** — never a silent fallback. *(PHASE3 §4.5 for the cascade and justification; PHASE2 §3.4 for the terminal throw)*
- **V8 Landed cost, forward-only.** Apportion by qty/amount/manual with the residue delta-smoothed and re-checked or thrown; non-stock rows valuation 0; all-zero amounts fall back to quantity. **Pro-rate by remaining quantity — sold goods never retro-valued.** This is the mechanism for Bill of Entry capitalisation (assessable value + BCD → IGST base, duty into item cost) — **in v1 per §4.3, conditional on Q7**. *(PHASE2 §3.4 + PHASE3 §4.5; PHASE4 M6)*
- **V9 COGS posts at the stock movement, not the invoice** — with a delivered-not-invoiced report as the mitigation. *(explicit rejection of PHASE3 §4.5; reasoning in §6.2 row 3)*
- **V10 Reconciliation is a barrier.** An absolute balance bounds forward propagation; delta = `qty_after − previous balance` read from the locked `stock_balance` row inside the posting transaction; on cancel it uses the balance captured at submission; an all-zero row emits an adjustment whose value difference is the negated cumulative value, forcing the ledger to exactly zero. *(PHASE2 §3.4)*
- **V16 Serialization of `(item, location)`.** The advisory-lock contract of §3.4, stated as a rule because V1, V6, V10 and V11 all depend on it and revision 1 omitted it entirely. Take the lock for every affected pair in sorted key order at the **start** of any SLE-writing transaction; record the lock set. *(PHASE2 §6, the concurrency half of the finding revision 1 cited only for portability)*
- **V11 Projected quantity is the availability contract.** `projected = actual + ordered + indented + planned − reserved − reserved_for_production − reserved_for_subcontract − reserved_for_production_plan`. One formula for every availability check. Preferred over Odoo's `virtual_available` because it names the manufacturing reservation classes separately. *(PHASE2 §3.4)*
- **V12 Reservation.** The move line **is** the reservation. `_get_reserve_quantity` down-rounds to the move UoM then half-up back so you never over-reserve; negative quants netted per key before allocation; already-picked quantity from other open pick lists subtracted first; removal strategy decides quant ordering (FIFO/LIFO by in-date, FEFO by removal date). *(PHASE3 §4.6; in-flight-pick netting from PHASE2 §3.4)*
- **V13 Received = accepted + rejected** at row precision; auto-filled when blank, else throws. *(PHASE2 §3.4)*
- **V14 Immutable item masters.** `has_batch_no` / `is_stock_item` / valuation method freeze once submitted transactions exist. *(PHASE2 §3.4)*
- **V15 Dry-run ledger preview.** *(PHASE2 §3.4, §5 — "steal this verbatim")*

### 6.7 Wave 3 — Manufacturing and job work

- **M1 BOM explosion.** Only phantoms flatten; a nested normal BOM is a procurement boundary. Convert through each child's **batch quantity**; round leaf quantities **up** in the component's own UoM; rows keyed **(item_code, operation)**; recursion guard by recursive CTE with self-ancestor rejection. *Naive failure:* rounding components down produces chronic small shortages that look like theft. *(PHASE3 §4.10 + PHASE2 §3.5)*
- **M2 BOM cost identity and secondary items.** `total_cost = operating_cost + raw_material_cost − secondary_items_cost`, both currencies; operation cost = `hour_rate × minutes/60`; `cost_per_unit = operating_cost / batch_size`; customer-provided and supplier-sourced items forced to rate 0; FG plus all secondary allocations total **exactly 100%**. *Why v1:* PHASE4 M7 — scrap and secondary output are a **taxable outward supply** with a TCS code, so they must be valued and reportable, not written off. *(PHASE2 §3.5)*
- **M3 Consumed and transferred quantities are derived, never counted** — recomputed from submitted entries grouped by `(item_code, original_item)` so a substitute's transfer credits the original required item; `material_transferred_for_manufacturing = min coverage fraction across all required items × WO qty` (the `min` is the point). *(PHASE2 §3.5)*
- **M4 Backflush = BOM-proportional at v1, with mandatory variance reporting.** Mode is a **per-BOM property**, never a global setting. *(PHASE2 §3.5, §5)*
- **M5 Job card conservation identity.** `total_completed_qty + process_loss_qty + pending_qty == for_quantity`; a later operation cannot exceed the previous operation's manufactured quantity, with a distinct message when the gap is process loss. **Bonus:** ITC-04 defines `lwqty`/`lwuqc` (loss/waste) and the reference **never populates them despite ERPNext tracking `process_loss_qty` on the very same receipt** — wire them together and the ITC-04 loss columns come free. *(PHASE2 §3.5; PHASE4 §5.5)*
- **M6 Operation sequencing.** Same sequence id ⇒ parallel; otherwise start at the previous group's max end + inter-operation minutes; semi-FG warehouse chaining with only the last operation writing to the FG warehouse. *(PHASE2 §3.5)*
- **M7 Interval algebra as the one calendar primitive.** `Intervals` = ordered disjoint `(start, stop, payload)` with union/intersection/difference; **work = attendance − leave**; the payload is retained so you know which attendance or leave produced each slice; two-week alternating schedules use `floor((ordinal−1)/7) % 2`, deliberately **not** ISO week numbers; batch by timezone. An approved absence materialises as a calendar leave row so no other module needs to know the leave module exists; a scheduled work order is the same thing on the workcenter resource. *Naive failure:* two calendars — PHASE2 §6 records exactly this bug in ERPNext, first-response time and the response deadline computed on different calendars. *(PHASE3 §4.9)*
- **M8 Job work: register, §143 clock, deemed supply. [DIFFERENTIATOR]** — five defects found in review; the register as revision 1 specified it would have given every new customer a false all-clear and could not model how a real job shop works.
  - *Register as a **chain**, not a pair.* Every outward challan line records item type, quantity sent, received back, returned as scrap/loss, outstanding — and carries `parent_challan_id` plus an **endorsement leg**, because machining → plating → heat treatment on one challan chain under s.143(1)(a) is the normal fabrication flow. Revision 1 keyed challans company→job worker only, so a job-worker-to-job-worker move had nowhere to go.
  - *Matching:* **quantity-level FIFO** of received against a specific outward challan's outstanding, walking the chain. Document-to-document linkage is not sufficient — partial returns spanning multiple challans go unapportioned, and the deadline feature is then confidently wrong.
  - *Clock:* ageing per challan **line**, by item type, from **`clock_start_date`** — a stored field defaulting to despatch date but settable to the job worker's date of receipt, because where inputs go **directly from the supplier to the job worker** (routine for castings and bar stock) the period is understood to run from receipt, and there is no GRN at a location the company never physically received. **[VERIFY] q84.** Windows from §3.9 — **the 1-year (inputs) / 3-year (capital goods, excluding the moulds-dies-jigs-fixtures-tools carve-out) figures are now read on the CBIC statutory repository's own s.143 page [PHASE8 PRIMARY]**, not inferred from reference code and not resting on secondary tax sites. Still a rule-store row, still CA-signed before it ships (**[VERIFY] q24–33**). **Do not ship a constant, corroborated or not.**
  - ***The window is extendable, and the clock must model that. HIGH PRIORITY — this is a correctness requirement, not a nicety.*** s.143, second proviso: *"the period of one year and three years may, on sufficient cause being shown, be extended by the Commissioner for a further period not exceeding one year and two years respectively"* **[PHASE8 PRIMARY]**. The challan record therefore carries an **optional extension order — `extension_ref`, `extension_order_date`, `extended_to_date`** — and **the deemed-supply trigger evaluates against `extended_to_date` where one exists, and against the statutory window where it does not.** The maximum extension is itself a rule-store row (1 year for inputs, 2 for capital goods), so the register validates that an entered extension does not exceed it. **Cost: three columns and one branch. Cost of omission: a compliance product that tells a customer they owe tax they do not owe, posts a deemed-supply invoice with interest running from a dispatch date months earlier, and is wrong in the customer's favour in exactly the direction that destroys trust in the whole register.** That is worse than silence, and it is worse than the cry-wolf failure the tooling carve-out was added to prevent — this one does not merely alarm, it posts. Mechanics of applying for and recording an extension are **[VERIFY] q94**; the field and the branch ship regardless of the answer, because a customer who *has* an order must be able to enter it on day one.
  - *Item statutory category, in v1 — a three-valued field, never a boolean.* **`jobwork_item_category ∈ {input, capital_goods, tools}`** on the item master, where `tools` is the moulds / dies / jigs / fixtures / tools carve-out (PHASE4 J6, **[VERIFY] q24–33**). Revision 2 named a `jobwork_exempt_category` flag beside a goods type still derived from `is_fixed_asset`, and a boolean cannot carry a three-way statutory classification: ITC-04 needs Input vs Capital Goods (`8b` vs `7b`), the clock needs a **per-category window read from the rule store — including "no window"** for the tools category — and tooling that was expensed rather than capitalised is misclassified by `is_fixed_asset` in both directions. Without this the largest population of open challans in a job-work-heavy manufacturer — the dies and fixtures sitting at a press shop — gets a running clock and T-90/T-30/T-7 alerts it should not have, and **the differentiator becomes the thing that cries wolf.** `is_fixed_asset` remains a separate accounting flag and is never read by the register. **Retrofitting a classification onto issued challans is a data-cleanup project at every installed site**, which is exactly the §9.1 criterion.
  - *Direct supply from the job worker's premises to a third party* (PHASE4 J7, **[NOT IN CODE]**): the challan chain terminates in a supply leg rather than a return leg, and the register must not treat it as outstanding. Modelled in v1 because the alternative is a permanently wrong outstanding figure; who raises which document is **[VERIFY] q24–33**.
  - *Alerts:* escalating at configurable T-90 / T-30 / T-7.
