  - *Expiry — **mechanism in v1**, constants CA-gated (§5.3):* the `deemed_supply_143` voucher of §3.4 — current posting date, `supply_date` = original despatch, interest from `supply_date`, ITC reversal, `gst_report_period` = current. **Stated explicitly because a one-line ambiguity here understates the customer's liability: interest accrues from the *original dispatch date*, not from the expiry of the §143 window.** A worked illustration in a secondary tax source applies 18% p.a. from the original dispatch date **[PHASE7 VERIFIED]**; rate and start-date basis are both rule-store rows and both are **[VERIFY] q24–33**. The invoice already carries the dispatch date as its supply date (§8.2 W2) — the interest computation must read the same field, not the trigger date. Understating a tax liability in a compliance product is the defect class that ends a reference-customer relationship. Supervisor-confirmed rather than unattended. If q24–33 are unanswered at the Stage 3 gate it ships behind an off flag and the manual pre-filled draft is the fallback.
  - *Opening challans:* imported at migration via L11's fourth path. Non-negotiable — see L11.
  - *Challan GST:* declared taxable value with no supply; charge type On-Net-Total or On-Item-Quantity, `Actual` rejected; **no GST when bill-from GSTIN == bill-to GSTIN**; rate resolved **deterministically from the item's HSN/tax template**. **EWB is required on an inter-state job-work challan irrespective of consignment value — Rule 138, third proviso, read on the CBIC rule text [PHASE8 PRIMARY]**, not a default and not a threshold comparison; override needs a reason and a role, and **q82 narrows to the unregistered-job-worker case only**.
  - *Keying:* **GSTIN pairs, not party master records.**
  - *ITC-04:* **Tables 4, 5A, 5B and 5C** — Table 4 (`8b` Inputs / `7b` Capital Goods) on despatch, 5A on return to the principal, **5B on a job-worker→job-worker endorsement** and **5C on a supply made directly from the job worker's premises** — every row carrying the **original** challan number and date, walking the chain to find it; **block submission in both directions** when the reference is missing. 5B and 5C were absent from revision 2 while M8 already modelled the movements that generate them, which would have shipped a return that is silently short by two tables. **[VERIFY] q88.**
  - *Valuation:* unused customer RM returned at the customer's declared value — **[VERIFY] q24–33**. PHASE4 §5.2 records this as a *docstring citation of Rule 55 in reference code*, and §5.5 already lists Rule 55 valuation as an open question; revision 1 stated it as settled.
  *(PHASE4 §1.3 M2–M4, §5.1–5.5, §8 gap 1)*
- **M9 Subcontracting valuation.** FG rate = `rm_cost + service_cost + additional_cost + landed_cost` per unit, scaled by `cost_allocation_per`; available materials keyed `(rm_item, fg_item, order)` accumulating sends minus returns minus consumed receipts; transfer-based consumption `= receipt_qty × transferred_qty / pending_qty_to_receive`, ceiled for whole-number UoMs. *(PHASE2 §3.5)*

### 6.8 Wave 4 — Procurement and sales completion

- **P1 MRP netting.** `required = qty + safety_stock − (projected − consumed pool)`; MOQ; **then** purchase-UoM conversion with `ROUND_CEILING` so a binding MOQ survives conversion; transfer-first / purchase-remainder split; sub-assembly netting tracks a consumed pool; aggregate keeping **max BOM level**. *Naive failure:* converting before applying MOQ silently under-orders. *(PHASE2 §3.5)*
- **P2 Horizon forecasting. [HIGH SILENT-ERROR]** `qty_to_order = max(min,max) − forecast_available(at horizon) − quantity_in_progress`, rounded up to the replenishment multiple; `horizon = today + Σ lead days along the resolved chain + company horizon days`; `quantity_in_progress` **must include open RFQs and unconfirmed POs**. *(PHASE3 §4.7, replacing PHASE2 §3.7)*
- **P3 In-batch stock claim guard** — a shared consumed-from-stock accumulator across lines in one batch run. *(PHASE3 §4.7)*
- **P4 Batch job shape** — batches of ~1000, savepoint per batch, drop only failing records and retry the rest, per-record activity log, resumable with checkpoints and coverage-skip. *(PHASE3 §4.7 and PHASE2 §5 agree exactly)*
- **P5 Last purchase rate** — only the chronologically latest submitted purchase sets it, normalised by conversion factor; internal suppliers and free items excluded. *(PHASE2 §3.7)*
- **P6 Minimum order quantity** validated against the **sum** across all rows of that item, warning (not throw) when the excess is only UoM rounding. *(PHASE2 §3.7)*
- **P7 Asymmetric third-party document updates** — increasing a linked PO quantity is automatic; **decreasing schedules a human task** rather than silently changing a document already sent to a supplier. *(PHASE3 §4.8)*
- **S1 Tri-quantity order ledger.** Ordered / fulfilled / invoiced; status a pure function; updates **recompute by re-summing sibling submitted rows**; percentage = `Σ min(|done|, |ordered|) / Σ |ordered| × 100` (the `min()` cap stops over-delivery exceeding 100%). **Over-delivery allowance ladder:** breach iff `overflow% − allowance > 0.01`; allowance resolves item field → company setting; a designated role downgrades the throw to a warning; the error names both the maximum and the amount to reduce by. *(PHASE3 §4.8 for the model; PHASE2 §3.3 for the mechanics)*
- **S2 Billable after return.** `billable_qty = min(qty, max(qty − returned_qty, delivered_qty))`; refunds subtract **only when linked back to the order**. *(PHASE2 §3.3 — "one line; it is the whole answer")*
- **S3 Delivered quantity from stock — the dropship trap.** Incoming moves subtract only if genuinely returns (origin-returned reference set, or source location outgoing, or picking carries a return reference). *(PHASE3 §4.8)*
- **S4 Returns are sign-flipped same-document.** Guards: party matches; posting datetime ≥ original; **conversion rate equals the original's** (this is what stops credit notes creating phantom FX gain); stock-update consistency; account match; cumulative returned quantity checked **per column** at that field's precision with distinct messages. *(PHASE2 §3.8)*
- **S5 Return valuation from the original layer** — `stock_value_difference / actual_qty` of the original row; a standalone credit note falls back to selling rate; expired batches value at zero. *(PHASE2 §3.8)*
- **S6 Credit limit as a report, not a block** in v1: `outstanding = GL(debit − credit for party) + Σ open order grand totals × (100 − per_billed)/100 + unbilled delivery value`, restricted to deliveries not linked to an order or invoice to avoid double counting; limit resolves customer → group → company. *(PHASE2 §3.7)*
- **S7 Selling price floor** — net rate may not fall below last purchase rate or valuation rate; exempt internal customers, non-stock, free items, returns; warning with a bypass role, not a hard throw. *(PHASE2 §3.7)*
- **S8 Pricing cascade, v1 subset.** Item → Item Group → Brand, stopping at the first level yielding rules unless they opt into stacking; slab matching applies the UoM conversion factor; on conflict prefer document currency, keep max priority, then a **deterministic tiebreak with a warning** — never a save-blocking exception, because that surfaces a *configuration* problem on the transacting user. *(PHASE2 §3.7 + its audit correction)*
- **S9 Duplicate line detection** keyed `(item, description, warehouse, source doc, batch)` for stock items. *(PHASE2 §3.7)*
- **S10 Kits are phantom BOMs** — one explosion mechanism, not two; the explosion snapshot is stored on the document so historic documents reproduce their original explosion. *(consolidating PHASE2 §3.7's versioning requirement into PHASE3 §4.10's kit-as-phantom-BOM)*

### 6.9 Deferred, with the consequence

| ID | Deferred | Consequence |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | Generic Tax Rule resolution engine (blank-as-wildcard, specificity scoring) | No per-party or per-territory overrides beyond GST category. Acceptable for domestic GST. The reference's conflict detection inspects only the *first* overlapping rule, so it needed rewriting anyway |
| D2 | **Unrealised** FX and revaluation; exchange-difference suppression on partial reconcile. **Realised FX on settlement is IN v1** (§4.1B, §4.3) | Revision 1 deferred realised FX while putting export/SEZ in v1, so a settlement at a different rate could not balance against §3.4's own deferred constraint trigger — an unpostable receipt or a silent plug. Realised FX is ~1 rule and 1 account. Year-end revaluation stays with the CA. **Mitigation already in v1:** S4's original-conversion-rate guard. PHASE3's ±half-rounding-rate interval rule is the exact spec for v1.1 |
| D3 | Odoo's declarative procurement rule engine | Multi-step routes are configuration-generated code paths, not data; a third step later is a code change. PHASE3 itself makes an `explain(procurement)` trace a precondition of adoption |
| D4 | Strict chained-move reservation and `_free_reservation` | Multi-step transfers can reserve stock a downstream step assumed was earmarked — manifesting as a pick list that cannot complete, a **visible** error, which is why it is deferrable. `_free_reservation` should be early v1.1: it is shop-floor reality |
| D5 | Serial-level valuation | Serialised items value at batch average. PHASE2 Q7: deciding batch-only avoids ERPNext's dual legacy representation entirely |
| D6 | Bank auto-matching | Manual matching against a filtered candidate list. The reference's flat 0–4 integer score has "no date proximity, no description similarity, no tie-break", so it is not worth copying as-is. Keep two rules when built: subtract existing allocations before display, and **abstain when the top two tie** |
| D7 | Deferred revenue/expense schedules | AMC and service contracts need manual JEs. The ledger-derived restartable design is the right spec when built |
| D8 | Material-transferred backflush mode | Substitutions and over-consumption need manual adjustment. Acceptable **only because** M4 ships mandatory variance reporting, so the discrepancy is visible rather than silent |
| D9 | **Fixed assets and depreciation entirely** — asset master, schedules, both books | §4.2 and §8.4 answer 4 say fixed assets are out; revision 1's D9 then said "v1 ships one book (SL + WDV)", which is an asset master, a schedule table, a posting path and a rebuild routine — a multi-week module sitting inside the *deferred* table. Resolved OUT (§4.3). v1 keeps only the `is_fixed_asset` boolean ITC-04 needs. The four rules that were listed (month-end stickiness, pro-rata on real calendar days never 30 or 365, salvage floor landing exactly, WDV recomputed only on fiscal-year change, rebuild never touching posted rows) are **retained here as the v1.1 spec**, because they are the expensive ones to rediscover. Consequence: the CA's schedule stays outside the system, as §8.4 answer 4 already tells the customer |
| D10 | Time-phased MRP, CRP, capacity load, APS | The plan is right in aggregate and wrong in timing beyond a couple of levels |
| D11 | Cost-centre allocation at posting time, budgets, >2 analytic axes | v1 has a bounded set of typed dimension columns; a fourth axis is a migration, not a user action. The correct trade for a controlled upgrade path |
| D12 | Materialised period-closing balance snapshots | The balance sheet scans GL from the start of time — fine for two years, painful at five. Ship the Period Closing Voucher **concept** in v1; add the snapshot when a ledger crosses a few million rows |
| D13 | Dunning, subscriptions, SLA/support, projects & T&M, supplier scorecard, drop-ship, portals, POS, BI, maintenance, fleet, RMA | None is manufacturing core. Two things to keep when they arrive: T&M's invoice stamp belongs on the individual time-log row, not the timesheet; and downtime capture must be reason-coded and job-card-linked **now** so the data exists when maintenance lands |
| D14 | GSTR-9/9C, ISD/GSTR-6, composition, RoDTEP/drawback/EPCG, PoS special rules for services | Annual or segment-specific |
| D15 | Rules 42/43, cash/credit/liability ledger set-off, **3B table 5.1 interest and late fee**. **The §143 interest computation is NOT deferred — it is in v1** (§4.3, §5.3) | 3B stops at declared figures; set-off computed outside and booked as a JE, exactly as every reference and every SME does today. Revision 2's blanket "interest and late fee" deferral contradicted §5.3, which ships J5's interest — and J5 without an interest number is a deemed-supply invoice that cannot state the exposure the whole wedge is sold on. The engine is the same; only the 3B *return* line is deferred. **The remaining 3B half is the cheapest v1.1 compliance item** |
| D16 | Full EWB lifecycle extras — multi-vehicle/transhipment, consolidated EWB, expiry monitoring | Part-B update, transporter change and validity extension are **in v1**; the rest is v1.1. Odoo has none of it and india-compliance only the first two, so there is no reference for the remainder |
| D17 | Turnover-based applicability engine | Operator-entered settings, as in every reference. Cheap and high-trust once L10 exists — strong v1.1 candidate |
| D18 | Dynamic QR on B2C | Absent everywhere; only bites material B2C volume. **Note: government JWT signature verification is NOT deferred** — both references decode with verification disabled, it is rated Low difficulty, and it is the difference between holding evidence and holding a string |

**Two open items this backlog inherits and cannot close:** the §143 window figures (**[VERIFY]** — no reference code corroborates 1 year or 3 years), and PHASE3's second **[unsettled]** item (the unbuild-with-lot defect), which is out of v1 scope since unbuild is not in the backlog.
---

## 7. BUILD SEQUENCE

Ordering principle: **the unretrofittable first; the externally-dependent started earliest; the novel-with-no-reference in the middle, not last; and the thing whose target schema must already exist, last.**

### Stage 0 — weeks 0–4, **two people, no engineers, and a written go/no-go at the end**

Revision 1 ran Stage 0 as "week 0, parallel" and then scheduled Q1–Q4 to be answered "before Stage 1 ends" — i.e. *during* two months of a full team building the unretrofittable foundation. §8.5 states the case against the whole project plainly ("choosing to build the whole ERP is choosing the most capital-intensive path to test the cheapest hypothesis") and §10 Q1 calls ten field interviews "the cheapest way to be wrong about the biggest decision". Ten interviews take three weeks and no engineers. Answering them after the team is hired is the single most expensive scheduling error available.

**Gate — all of these are written down and signed before Stage 1 begins, and any of them can stop the project.** Revision 4 adds Q19 and Q20 and rewrites Q1, because a market-research pass established that *none* of the demand-side questions has a published answer anywhere: **there is no source that says an Indian manufacturing SME wants to leave Tally, would pay for a job-work clock, values simplicity enough to switch, or can be reached through any channel.** These interviews are the only instrument that exists.

| # | Deliverable | Stops the project if |
|---|---|---|
| Q1 | 10 field interviews, **three questions and a falsification criterion** (§10 Q1): (a) do you send material out for job work and how much is out right now; (b) how do you track it today, and has a challan ever aged past a year; (c) what is it worth per year to be told that ₹X has been out for 11 months and is about to become a taxable supply with interest from the dispatch date | **Falsification:** if fewer than 6 of 10 can name, unprompted, a challan currently outstanding beyond six months, the pain is theoretical. Then sell the module, not the ERP (§8.5) |
| Q19 | **"What do you hate about Tally — and what specifically happened that made you start looking?"** What does it not do; what do you keep in Excel because Tally cannot hold it; what would have to be true for you to move off it; **and what was the trigger.** Same 10 interviews, same conversation as Q1 | Nobody wants to leave. The entire switching argument (§8.4 answers 2 and 10, W4, "no import path = no sale") assumes they do, and **no manufacturer voice anywhere establishes it**. **Second falsification (§10 Q19): if 8 of 10 name a trigger unrelated to manufacturing or compliance capability, §8.3's lead story is rewritten before Stage 1 begins** |
| Q21 | **WhatsApp today: what do you send, to whom, and would you want it automatic?** Same conversation. **A scoping question, not a gate** | Nothing — it cannot stop the project. It sizes §4.1A's notification adapters and keeps a chat-ERP out of scope |
| Q20 | **Cloud or on-premise, and why** — data control, internet reliability, cost, or precedent from Tally? Asked in the same conversation as Q8 | Nothing stops the project; **8 of 10 accepting cloud moves the self-host packaging, backup/retention, bundle delivery and customer-hardware support out of v1** (§4.1F) |
| Q2 | 5 partner conversations on carrying a closed-source ERP against a free one | Distribution has no answer and no engineering fixes it |
| Q3 | Tally spike against two **real** ERP 9 / Prime export files, with a **written day-budget**: if the parse spike exceeds N days, Tally is reclassified as a **services deliverable** and the GA gate is restated as "opening balances from CSV" | It is a two-year build and nobody says so until Stage 4 |
| Q7 | Do the first ten prospects import raw material? | Decides Bill of Entry in/out before Stage 3 (§4.3) |
| Q8 | Does any design partner require a dedicated database as a contractual isolation term? | Reverses D4 **before** any migration is written (§3.7) |
| Q17 | **Do the first ten prospects fall under s.148 cost records / CRA-1?** (**[VERIFY] q87**) | If most do, we are selling "compliant books" into a segment that must also keep a cost-records set v1 cannot produce. That is either a v1.5 scope item or a segment carve-out — and either way it is said in the sales call, not discovered at audit |
| — | **A one-page financial model**: price, ACV, CAC, burn, unit economics, and the **annual cost of the compliance function** (§3.9) — CA retainer + statutory data licence + notification watch + internal owner + **the clean-room legal review (§3.12)** | §8.4 answer 10 is argued entirely on features against a competitor whose price is zero, with our own number left blank. Without it the commercial section is a slogan |

**Also in Stage 0, in parallel:** shortlist GSPs with a **mandatory sandbox before signing** (PHASE4 Q2) and get the 2B-session question answered (§5.4). Put a CA on retainer (Q4) **with a named date for q24–33**, because those constants gate the §143 register in Stage 3. **Source the statutory data** — licence a rate master or budget the build (§3.9). Obtain real Tally export files *and* **two real biometric device exports** (eSSL / Matrix / ZKTeco); PHASE6 §4's "must not be inferred" warning applies identically to both, and revision 1 gave the biometric formats no research slot at all. **Start the MIT-framework provenance review and book the clean-room legal review** (§3.12) — it is a lead-time item like the CA and the GSP, and it is the one risk that ends the company rather than delaying it. Sign 3 design partners in one sub-vertical. **Two cheap research items added in revision 5, both of which two automated passes could not do and a person can:** (i) **get the job-work prevalence number** — a human opening the ASI 2022-23 PDF, or a MoSPI microdata query; it is the single most valuable missing figure in the whole evidence base, it is a tooling gap rather than an absence, and **it should be done before the financial model is written, not after**; (ii) **ask the GSP sandbox the IMS questions (q95) alongside Q4's 2B-session question** — which accept/reject/pending actions are API-reachable versus portal-only, and what a 2B recomputation does to a reconciliation already signed off. **q95's wrong answer costs a migration, not a data change**, which puts it in the same class as Q8.

**Q3's scope, corrected.** The Tally spike is not "can we parse the file". It is "can we parse the file **and produce §6.3 L11's opening statutory state from it**" — open items at invoice level, open job-work challans with their original dates, the filed-return register, YTD withholding per PAN, payroll YTD. Revision 2 asked the easy half of the question and would have got a reassuring answer to it.

*Why first:* the longest lead times in the project are a GSP contract with sandbox credentials, a CA who will put their name on the rule table, and a licensed statutory dataset. None is code and all three block Stage 2. **Hiring is the fourth: 6–8 engineers who do not exist yet take 3–4 months to recruit** (§10 Q13 admits the senior owner of the permission chokepoint is an open hiring question), and revision 1's Stage 1 started at month 0 with a full team.

### Stage 1 — months 0–2: the foundation nobody can retrofit

`packages/money` (`Money`/`Qty`/`Dec`, §3.1) **first**; spine + docstatus + the immutability trigger + the statutory-artifact side tables; hardened audit log with `event_id` and the inline chain; numbering with FY/GSTIN/series scoping, the cut-over ordinal and the acknowledgeable continuity auditor; **the statutory rule store of §3.9 with its resolver**; permission predicates over NOT NULL scoping columns; tenant model **and the migration orchestrator**; job queue with the outbox; `ext jsonb` + `field_extension`; the **fixture-driven GSP mock**; the `(item, location)` lock protocol as a tested primitive.

*Two corrections to revision 1's Stage 1.* The **migration orchestrator** was a decisive argument for the tenancy bet, priced at 3–4 engineer-months in R21, and appeared in no stage — "tenant model" and "tenant provisioning" are not it. It is now a named Stage 1 deliverable with its own estimate, and §3.7 no longer leans on it as evidence. **`property_override` + the 91-property allowlist + the inspector move out**, to a Stage 2 slot gated on Q9 (§3.2).

Also in Stage 1: `payment_ledger_entry` + `reconciliation_edge` (§3.4) with the two canonical CTEs; **the differential-testing harness** of §3.12, which is worth most when the pure cores are first written, not after they are wrong; and the **measured audit-log growth figure** that §3.11's on-prem sizing table depends on.

**Demonstrable:** a stub tax invoice that prints with a correct series and shows a field-level, chained edit log with actor and IP, grouped by event, **and an `audit_log` row proving the migrator wrote through the same chokepoint**. Show it to a CA, not a customer.
**Retires:** R2, R3, R4, R5, R6, R8, R20 — everything PHASE4 §9.1–9.4 calls impossible later.

### Stage 2 — months 2–5: the invoice a CA signs off

GL + AR/AP + period control; **the tax data model of §3.10** then the tax engine (T1–T15, four heads); the full GST field set G1–G8, G10; SI/PI/notes/payments/Bill of Supply/the three vouchers; print formats with the IRN+QR contract; **the report engine with Trial Balance and P&L as its two reference implementations**; GSTR-1/3B preparation **including the six amendment tables and Table 13**; 2B download and reconciliation; **e-invoice and e-way bill against the mock, verified against the GSP sandbox**; **payroll computation cores** (PF/ESI/PT/gratuity as pure functions over a rule-store payload, rate rows stubbed and marked unverified) — they share the slab-engine shape with the tax engine and both are pure functions, and leaving them in Stage 4 put the most externally-dependent workstream last; `property_override` + allowlist + inspector **if Q9 says yes**.

**Demonstrable:** a real invoice, a real sandbox IRN, and a GSTR-1 JSON a CA validates in the government offline tool. **This is the first artefact worth a customer meeting.**
**Retires:** R1 (the register's central risk), R9's first checkpoint, R11, R17, R18 — government API reliability, error-code surface, idempotency and quota behaviour proven in month 4 rather than month 10. Discovering the GSP is unusable at month 10 kills the company.

### Stage 3 — months 5–8: the factory

Items/warehouses/stock ledger with the D13 lock protocol/MA valuation/bounded repost; PO-GRN-PI, SO-DC-SI, landed cost **and Bill of Entry if Q7 said yes**; **gate entry + weighbridge (with the Windows agent)**; **delivery challan + e-way bill + transporter master + dispatch paperwork**; inter-GSTIN branch transfer; BOM/WO/job card/scrap/reason-coded downtime; quality inspection; **job-work challan chains + ITC-04 + §143 ageing register + the J5 deemed-supply posting path**.

**Entry gate:** q24–33 answered, or J5 ships flag-off with the manual fallback (§5.3). The register is built either way; only the constants are gated.

**Demonstrable:** one continuous story — truck at gate → weighbridge → GRN → RM issue → work order → FG → dispatch with an e-way bill; and a job-work challan crossing its §143 window and raising an alarm. This is the demo that closes deals.
**Retires:** R7 (back-dated repost correctness), R13/D13 (stock serialization, proven under a concurrent-posting load test, not assumed), and the §143 quantity-level FIFO matching over challan chains — the one feature with **no reference implementation anywhere**, attempted while there is still time to be wrong about it.

### Stage 4 — months 8–11: the switch

Import engine incl. **bank statement import** and **opening open-challan import**; opening balances incl. trial-balance CSV, invoice-level open items, the series cut-over state **and the opening statutory registers of L11 path 5** (filed-return register, YTD withholding per PAN, payroll and ESI YTD, gratuity days, turnover YTD, ITC ledger openings); **Tally bridge** (or the CSV fallback, if Q3's day-budget was exceeded); the remaining report pack; payroll **pipeline** (attendance → wage sheet), the cores having landed in Stage 2; backup/restore, deployment, tenant provisioning, licensing/entitlement (§3.11); **the zero-infrastructure installer** (§4.1F — no Bench, no Docker, no Linux, no site/app concepts visible to the customer), which is the only simplicity claim §8.2 permits us to make and therefore must ship.

**Demonstrable:** take a design partner's Tally backup on Monday and have their books live by Friday — **including a correct mid-year series cut-over** (which revision 1's numbering schema made impossible) **and a §143 dashboard that shows their real open challans rather than a green screen** (which revision 2's opening import made impossible).
**Retires:** R10 (adoption blockers).
*Why Tally is last despite being the strongest wedge:* the bridge's *target* schema is everything built in Stages 1–3. Building it earlier means building it twice. Its **research** was done at Stage 0, which is where the real unknown lived.

### Continuous, from month 1

A **red CI acceptance suite** that produces a GST-valid invoice, a valid e-invoice payload, a delivery challan, a Table 13 extract and a GSTR-1 extract from a seeded company, **and asserts T16 — that an invoice dated beyond the rule-store reporting window is refused for IRN generation for a tenant above the threshold, and accepted for one below it.** It stays red until Stage 2 passes. Alongside it, from Stage 1: the **differential harness** and the golden-fixture and property suites of §3.12, and the **per-rule provenance log** that makes the clean-room claim auditable rather than asserted. The compliance gate is the definition of done for the foundation, not a downstream module — this is the single counter-measure to R1.

### 7.5 GA gate, restated so it is measurable and reachable

Revision 1's gate — "two paid pilots complete a month-end and a GSTR-1 we generated is filed without manual correction" — could not be met inside the stated timeline and half of it was unobservable. A manufacturer's month-end needs stock close, payroll and Tally-migrated opening balances, all of which land in Stage 4 (months 8–11), so pilots cannot reach their first month-end before month 11 and GA is month 13–14 at the earliest. And "filed without manual correction" is invisible to us when the CA files from our JSON and will always tweak something.

**Pilot go-live: month 9**, on Stage 3 output plus the opening-balance path pulled forward for those two tenants. **Pilot cycle: 2–3 months**, explicitly on the headline timeline rather than assumed away.

**GA criteria, all observable:**
1. Two paid pilots each complete **two consecutive** month-ends in the system (one to learn, one to prove).
2. The GSTR-1 JSON for both passes the **government offline tool with zero errors**, **and an ITC-04 extract covering Tables 4 and 5A validates against the government offline utility for both pilots.** §8.3 leads the entire sales story with the job-work flow; shipping it unproven against the government's own validator while GSTR-1 ships proven is exactly the wrong way round.
3. The CA signs a one-line statement that they made **zero line-level edits** before filing. Signed, not inferred.
4. `audit:verify` runs clean over both tenants' full history, and the daily digest chain is unbroken.
5. Zero open **Sev-1** defects in posting, numbering, tax head or the §143 register.

### 7.6 The estimate, and why this document no longer states one

Revision 1 said: "PHASE5 §8.4 costs a billing-and-inventory MVP on this architecture at 6 months; compliance depth, job work, payroll and migration take it to 9–11 months with 6–8 engineers." Three things are wrong with that sentence and they compound.

1. **The source does not support it.** PHASE5 §8.4's six months is for **~15 core entities**, and §9 Q6 says that plan "assumes **3–4 engineers**". So the prior is ~21 engineer-months and the plan's figure is ~70 — a 3.3× budget presented as a derivation.
2. **The source disclaims itself.** PHASE5's closing line: "All engineer-month figures in this document are experience-based priors, not measurements. Two audits explicitly graded the cost sections `hand-wavy`. **Re-derive anything that enters a funding decision.**" Revision 1 did not re-derive; it multiplied.
3. **The scope broke the estimating basis.** The 3.3× covers the full GST gate (N1–N20 + IRN + the whole EWB lifecycle + GSTR-1/3B + amendment tables + 2B recon + export/SEZ + RCM + TDS/TCS), all of manufacturing and job work, all of payroll computation, the Tally bridge, an import engine, ~25 reports and 79 numbered business rules — **every one clean-room, none copyable**. And "~40 screens over ~18 entities" was itself wrong: the real figures are **~40 entities** (§3.2) and a screen list that excludes at least nine major workbenches.

**What must replace it, before this document is used to raise or spend money:**

- A **bottom-up re-derivation** from the enumerated entity list (§3.2), the enumerated screen list (a Stage 0 deliverable) and the 79 rules — each rule sized, the ~15 marked [GATE] or [HIGH SILENT-ERROR] sized separately because they carry test burden the others do not.
- Stated as a **range with a P50 and a P90**, not a point. A point estimate on a scope with 90 open statutory questions is a fiction.
- **Plus 3–4 months of hiring** before Stage 1 can run at full team, since the team does not exist.
- **Plus the pilot cycle** (§7.5): GA is 2–3 months after engineering-complete.
- **Plus implementation and support headcount.** The GA gate is two pilots through a month-end, and two pilots at an Indian SME consume a full-time implementer for months. That person is nowhere in the 6–8 and is not an engineer.
- **Plus the UI workstream** (§3.13) — a designer and two front-end engineers against 70–90 screens, four of which are screen families no reference has ever rendered. Revision 2 costed the screens and staffed nobody to design them.
- **Plus QA.** Neither revision named a QA or test-engineering headcount against 79 numbered rules, ~15 of them [GATE] or [HIGH SILENT-ERROR], a differential harness and a red acceptance suite. "The engineers write the tests" is a decision, not an omission — but it has to be made on purpose and it changes the per-rule sizing.

Until that exists, the working figure for planning conversations is *9–11 months of engineering at 6–8 engineers, plus hiring, plus pilots* — and it is labelled a **placeholder**, not an estimate.

**A sequencing argument rejected:** "build inventory and manufacturing first, it's the actual product." No. Compliance is a *gate* — PHASE4 §1: "an Indian ERP that cannot produce a compliant tax invoice is not sellable" — and the CA, not the owner, holds the veto. An unsellable perfect factory module is worth nothing.

---

## 8. THE COMMERCIAL CASE

### 8.1 ERPNext, assessed honestly

**What it does well and we will not beat soon.** PHASE2 §1: every money-touching document funnels GL dicts through one function running a fixed pipeline. COGS is the delta in stock value. Outstanding is a two-CTE recomputation over an append-only signed subledger. Cancellation writes contras. **These are not features; they are fifteen years of production defects converted into constraints**, and we will rediscover a fraction of those defects the hard way. PHASE2 §2 rates *Deep* on 22 modules including subcontracting in **both** directions (PHASE2 notes most open ERPs omit inbound entirely). PHASE5 §4.1's customization overlay lets a partner add a field in production at 11pm and still take the next release. PHASE6 §6: four processes on 4 GB with native database-per-site tenancy — already the right architecture for ₹40–80k/yr customer hardware. Plus free, GPLv3, an India partner network already trained, and `frappe-india-compliance`.

**Weaknesses that are fixable, therefore not a moat:** seven-level inheritance with `if self.doctype in [...]` dispatch, full recompute in `validate()`, ~30 Singles toggles that change posting math with no rule version on the ledger row, `frappe.flags` as an argument channel, status written by `db_set` from dozens of call sites, reposting that mutates *submitted* documents.

**Weaknesses that are structural, therefore exploitable:** PHASE5 §7.1 — the generic Desk is ~99,000 LOC and its ceiling is **categorical, not aesthetic**. Grep-verified: zero paste handlers in `grid.js`/`grid_row.js`, no range select, no fill-down; report-view inline edit is one RPC per cell; Quick Entry bails at >7 fields; `Meta.check_if_large_table` exists purely to disable UI features past 100k rows; the list-view record count is a 60-minute-cached estimate — "a lie by design". Four screen families sit outside the model entirely: bulk data entry, POS/counter, mobile/shop floor, and stateful operational screens (job card with timer, machine queue, station QC). **ERPNext's own POS is a hand-written page — that is the tell.** And PHASE4 §8 gap #5: statutory content is hard-coded (a 2099 sentinel means every rate change is a code change). **A free project cannot fund a CA on retainer.**

**8.1a The rest of the shortlist, because a prospect does not compare us to ERPNext alone.** Revision 4 assessed one competitor; Phase 8 read six vendors' own pricing and documentation pages and the picture is now specific enough to be useful in a room **[all PHASE8 VERIFIED unless noted]**. **TallyPrime** — accounting + inventory, not a manufacturing ERP; manufacturing journal records consumption *after the fact* on a single-level accounting BOM; **no ITC-04 export as of TallyPrime 5.0** per a user on a third-party Tally forum **[single-source, and it sits in direct tension with Tally's own marketing page — treat it as a strong lead, not proof]**. **BUSY** — accounting-rooted with a real manufacturing add-on (BOM, MRP, gate entry, QC) that needs a reseller to configure; its ITC-04 offering is **a downloadable Excel template** its own page calls *"a practical starting point"*. **Marg** — pharma/FMCG distribution software; job work is **a value in a Sales Type dropdown**, with no ITC-04, no deadline and no deemed supply anywhere in its own support article. **Zoho Books** — no manufacturing; delivery challans are a generic multi-purpose document. **Odoo** — real MRP, and a paid **$499** third-party add-on exists precisely because *"Odoo out of the box has no idea what a delivery challan under Rule 45 is."* **The finding this supports, verified across seven products' own documentation: not one demonstrably tracks the s.143 deadline or computes deemed-supply liability.** Two products to stop shadow-boxing: **Sage X3** publishes $100K–$400K TCO for 50–1,000-employee companies with **no India/GST localisation content at all — it does not belong on a ₹5–50 Cr Indian shortlist and is dropped from any competitive framing**; **SAP Business One** is positioned by cost advisors at *"₹25–50 Cr+ turnover"* and ₹12–22 L year one **[consultant-blog, single-source, not re-verified]** — a real competitor for the **top decile of our band only**, and saying so is more credible than pretending to compete with it across the range.

### 8.2 The wedges, ranked by defensibility

**W1 — Effective-dated statutory rule store as a maintained, signed data feed. Reclassified: an unpriced operating liability until §3.9's sourcing line item has a rupee number.** The *architecture* is defensible and unretrofittable — the rule-version stamp cannot be added to documents already posted. But revision 1 called this the #1 wedge while specifying no schema, no data-acquisition plan, no cost and no vendor. It was a schema, not an asset, and it is the harder half: nobody hands you a machine-readable HSN→rate table with effective dates across the 2017/2022/2025 transitions, and the only HSN master in the references is inside GPL code our licensing position forbids. **It becomes a wedge on the day we can state what the compliance function costs per year and where the rate master comes from.** Until then it is a cost centre we have not sized. Least demo-able either way: invisible. *(PHASE4 §8 gaps 3, 4, 5, 8; §9.4; §3.9)*

**W2 — Job-work control: §143 deadlines and deemed supply. Restated in revision 4: the strongest *competitor-absence* evidence in the whole scout, which is not the same thing as demand evidence, and this document stops calling it the latter.** **State the claim precisely, because the loose version is now false and a prospect will find that out in ten minutes.** The free stack **does** ship a **GST Job Work Stock Movement** report — a maintainer closed a March 2026 request for a job-work-challan report as `not_planned` with *"This report is already available. Check: GST Job Work Stock Movement"* **[PHASE8 VERIFIED — india-compliance#4086]**. **"ERPNext has nothing here" must stop being said.** What it does not ship — repo-wide grep across india-compliance for `143`, `deemed supply`, `one year`, `three year` returns **only HSN-code digit coincidences**, confirmed in PHASE4 and unrefuted in PHASE8 — is **a clock**: ageing a challan against the §143 window, posting a deemed-supply invoice dated to the original dispatch, and computing interest from that date. **A stock-movement report is not a liability engine.** On the filing side the gap is still real and on the record: Frappe's own team, answering a user asking for an ITC-04 report, *"Currently, No such report is available. We are planning it with e-Waybill generation for Subcontracting."* **[PHASE8 VERIFIED — discuss.frappe.io/t/123323]**. And an implementation partner's blog claiming ERPNext *"handles ITC-04 natively… material approaching the 1-year limit is flagged"* is contradicted by this project's own grep — **trust the grep**, and expect that blog to be quoted back at us. ITC-04's `lwqty`/`lwuqc` are defined and never populated; `nature_of_job` is hardcoded to the literal `"Job Work"` with an in-code TODO; the FG-receipt challan number is free text with no validation, uniqueness or date. Defensible because the payload — a tax invoice carrying the despatch date as its supply date, with interest, plus ITC reversal — touches stock, GL and GST simultaneously: **a bolt-on app cannot do that; only whoever owns the ledger can.** *That claim is only true if we ship it*, which is why J5 moved into v1 (§5.3). Revision 1 deferred exactly this half and led with the challan register plus an ageing report — which is what a Frappe app *can* do over india-compliance's ITC-04 tables and which §8.5 concedes any of hundreds of partners ships in a quarter, free. Leading with the commoditisable half is not a wedge. Highly sale-able — *if* the owner already fears it and it has a rupee number for him, which is Q1. **Revision 5 splits what revision 4 stated as one undifferentiated blank, because the two halves have now moved in opposite directions and conflating them again would be the same error in reverse.**

> **The liability is real and enforced.** *Tvl. Technocast Foundry v. State Tax Officer, Coimbatore*, **2026-VIL-582-MAD**, Madras High Court, judgment **08.06.2026**: on an allegation of *"failure to maintain job-work records, furnish Form GST ITC-04 and establish return of goods within the prescribed period"*, the Court held that *"without proper records showing receipt within statutory timeframes, a deeming fiction applied treating the movement as supply, creating tax liability"*, and **upheld extended limitation under s.74** — remanding only on a revenue-neutrality argument. The job worker was a **sister concern**, i.e. the relationship was informal, which is how SME job work actually runs. Separately, a taxpayer describes a **s.125 penalty actually levied at GST audit for ITC-04 non-filing**. **[PHASE8 PRIMARY / primary-user-voice]**
>
> **The demand is not.** No prevalence figure for job work among Indian discrete manufacturers (the ASI and SIDBI PDFs are image-based and could not be read; nobody has counted). **No first-hand description anywhere of how an SME tracks job work today** — Excel, challan book, or nothing. **No buyer, anywhere, across two research passes and roughly 250 queries, has stated a rupee figure they would pay to prevent this.** **[PHASE8 UNEVIDENCED]**

**What that changes and what it does not.** Revision 4's "zero record of a §143 notice or penalty" is **withdrawn — it was true of the evidence then and is false now.** The exposure is proven, not inferred, and the interview can open with a real case instead of a hypothetical, which turns Q1c from an abstract question into a concrete one. **The axis that decides the project has not moved at all.** A liability being real is not the same as it being budgeted; every piece of evidence found is **reactive** — discovered at audit, not anticipated — which if anything weakly suggests it is *not* currently a line in anyone's budget. **The risk is now proven real; the market for preventing it is still unproven.** Note also the asymmetry the fact-check surfaced and this plan must not paper over: the *exposure* (retroactive deemed supply plus interest from dispatch) sounds severe, while the codified *filing* penalty is capped at ₹25,000 under s.125 **[PHASE7 VERIFIED]** — **so sell the liability, not the ITC-04 filing.** **The Madras HC case settles which half bites: the demand upheld was s.74 extended limitation, not s.125.** That is precisely why J5 is in v1 and why the register-plus-ageing half is the commoditisable one. **One more competitive fact worth carrying into a demo:** the single purpose-built paid product found in either pass — a **$499** Odoo add-on sold specifically for this gap — advertises on its own sales page that it tracks *"the 180-day return window (and the 3-year window for capital goods)"* **[PHASE8 VERIFIED — vendor page]**. **180 days is wrong.** The statutory window for job-work inputs is **one year**; 180 days is the unrelated Rule 37 ITC-reversal-on-unpaid-invoice provision. Whether that is an error or an early-warning buffer mislabelled as the statute, the research could not distinguish — but it is consistent with *"nobody has built this correctly"* and inconsistent with *"the market already solved this."* And the felt pain may be **operational** (knowing what is out, with whom, since when — a daily stores question) rather than **compliance** (a periodic return). Those are different products with different buyers; the plan pitches compliance and nothing in evidence says that is the right choice. Q1b exists to separate them. *(PHASE4 §5.4–5.5, §8 gap 1; PHASE7 §5)*

**W3 — The factory-floor UI the metadata engine cannot render.** PHASE6 §3: zero grep hits across *both* erpnext and odoo/addons for material gate entry, weighbridge, delivery-challan-as-movement, and shift-wise production rollup (HRMS *has* a shift model but nothing wires it to production output). Defensible not because each item is hard — they are not — but because PHASE5 §7.1 proves ERPNext's generic form engine cannot render these as task UIs without leaving its own architecture. **Strengthened, not weakened, by Phase 7:** three named users on Frappe's own forum in November 2025 report that v16's navigation between modules and doctypes is confusing and that they "lose track of where I started" **[PHASE7 VERIFIED]** — a shop-floor or gate operator who loses their place does not use the system, they call the accounts person. **Strengthened again by Phase 8, and this time with a labour-market argument rather than a UX one — which also finally names the user.** *The screens that must be simple are the operator's, not the owner's.* The Indian accounts-operator role is a distinct, commonly-advertised job category hired at a **12th-pass bar** **[PHASE8 SINGLE-SOURCE — job-board category listings]**, while owners in this segment are **81–85% graduate-educated** **[PHASE8 VERIFIED — ICRIER 2025, with the sampling caveat in §3.13]**. So: **owner-facing dashboards may assume English and sophistication; gate, weighbridge, job-card and voucher-entry screens may not.** That is a design constraint with a hiring-bar behind it, not a slogan — and it is what W3 was always arguing from first principles. Two disciplines that come with it (both in §3.13): if a vernacular target is chosen it is **Hinglish, not Hindi**; and the incumbent's own choice — **TallyPrime's UI is English-only**, vernacular only for data entry and printed output — is to be interrogated, not copied and not inverted. **This is the defensible form of the simplicity hypothesis and it stays here rather than being promoted to a headline.** **Caveat added in review:** weighbridge is only in the wedge if the Windows agent of §4.1F is funded; a browser on an Android tablet cannot read RS-232, and "a serial-port read" plus "no app" is a contradiction, not a plan.

**W4 — Migration (Tally + opening balances). A gate, not a wedge.** Real and evidenced, and **stronger in revision 5 because it is now the maintainer's own words rather than our inference**: ERPNext's co-founder **nabinhait** closed the Tally-migration issue tracker on **13 January 2025** with *"We have removed Tally Migration in the latest version, closing the issue."* **[PHASE8 VERIFIED — erpnext#44680]**. The free incumbent does not do this badly; **it has withdrawn from doing it** — while `setup_wizard.js:73` still puts "Tally" at the head of the current-system dropdown and offers nothing. A community member shipped a replacement open-source Tally Migrator in **June 2026** whose own announcement says migration *"has been a challenge for ERPNext implementers for years"* **[PHASE8 VERIFIED]**. ***Do not say "removed after years of unresolved bugs"*** — the audit found that issue was open one month and its headline error was a duplicate-entry `IntegrityError`, not a long-running defect; the **withdrawal** is the hard fact and the "years" framing is stitched from separate complaints. No bulk trial-balance import anywhere in ERPNext. "No import path = no sale." But it is consumed once, at the moment of lowest trust; a partner can deliver the same outcome as a service; and the formats are unresearched. **Fund it as cost of entry; do not price the product on it.**

**W5 — AI-native operation. Reclassified: a multiplier, not a wedge — confirmed by what the market actually ships.** ERPNext core carries only minimal native field-level AI helpers; substantive capability lives in third-party community apps and MCP bridges, and a native-AI-provider request for v16 is **open, not shipped** (`frappe/erpnext` issue #50807, opened 29 Nov 2025) **[PHASE7 VERIFIED]**. Zoho Books' AI is OCR receipt extraction, bank-feed matching and dashboard insights — **automation of existing steps, not a new interaction paradigm**, and no source describes users abandoning the normal UI for a chat interface **[PHASE7 VERIFIED]**. Nobody has captured a defensible position here. **One architectural constraint adopted now because it is free now and a re-model later:** if AI is built, it reads through the **same compiled permission predicate as every other query path** (§3.8) — no raw SQL, no bypass, no service account. This is not a preference; a chat interface that leaks another cost centre's margins is a permission defect with a friendly face. *(A related community pattern — permission-aware restricted query layers rather than raw LLM database access — is **[PHASE7 SINGLE-SOURCE]**, one developer's project across two forum posts, and is not cited here as a norm.)* Where it attaches to evidence, it attaches to W2/W3/W4: CoA type inference for Tally (PHASE6 §4 rates it **Hard** and explicitly requires "heuristics + human review"), fuzzy header mapping (Odoo already does SequenceMatcher at cutoff 0.2 — we would be matching, not leapfrogging), and 2B reconciliation. An API key is not a moat and it is the one capability a free competitor can add in a weekend.

**Not a wedge at all — deployment simplicity.** Frappe is already four processes on 4 GB with native per-tenant databases. Match it or lose on it; treat it as a constraint, not a selling point.

**Not a wedge at all — radical simplicity. Table-stakes hygiene, and the defensible half is already banked as W3.** The owner's hypothesis is that ERP is complex, users hate complexity, therefore a radically simple ERP wins. Four reasons it does not survive contact with the evidence. (i) **The complaint is real but from the wrong population** — ERPNext's setup is genuinely hard and has been for seven years, and its v16 navigation disorients users today **[PHASE7 VERIFIED]**, but every complainant is a self-installer, developer or global reviewer. An Indian SME buys ERPNext through a partner who absorbs exactly that pain, and **no source anywhere records an Indian SME choosing or rejecting an ERP on simplicity** **[PHASE7 UNEVIDENCED]**. (ii) **"Simple" now has a partial definition, and it is narrower than the hypothesis needed.** Revision 4 said none of mobile-first / Hindi UI / WhatsApp / fewer-screens had been researched. Phase 8 researched them and returned three different answers, not one: **WhatsApp is evidenced as a channel** — 3,323 of 4,218 digitally-active respondents in a 7,835-respondent India SME Forum/META survey use it for day-to-day business interaction **[PHASE8 VERIFIED — industry-body hard data]**; **vernacular is evidenced as *Hinglish*, not Hindi**, and the market leader's own UI is English-only (§3.13); and **mobile is evidenced in neither direction for this segment** — the only mobile signal is ERPNext forum users asking for a native app, none of them an Indian manufacturer, so §8.4 answer 7 stands untouched. **Voice is an adjacent market only** — every voice-billing product found is retail/kirana, and there is zero evidence of voice input for job cards or weighbridge readings. None of that promotes simplicity to a wedge; what it does is stop this plan asserting the *wrong* specifics. Scope discipline attached to the one positive finding: **WhatsApp is a notification and document-delivery channel, not a data-entry interface** — §4.1A's notification service already has the adapter, and **nothing in the evidence supports a WhatsApp bot for transactions. Do not let this become a chat-ERP.** *(The forum evidence originally offered for WhatsApp demand inside ERPNext was found fabricated by Phase 8's own audit and is withdrawn; the survey data stands on its own.)* (iii) **The category is occupied, well funded — and its economics are ugly, which is new and matters.** Vyapar: **$36 M raised**, FY25 revenue **₹69 Cr (+53% YoY)** against a **₹63 Cr net loss**, cash down **93% to ₹6 Cr** **[PHASE8 VERIFIED — Entrackr, RoC-filing based; this replaces revision 4's ~$35 M / ~1,400-staff listing-aggregator figures]**. myBillBook's own pricing page shows **Diamond ₹291/mo (annual) · Platinum ₹333 · Enterprise ₹570** and **no ₹199 or Silver tier at all** **[PHASE8 VERIFIED — vendor page; revision 4's ₹199/month aggregator figure is withdrawn]**. Khatabook, the most-cited "simple wins" story, is a **cautionary** data point: it shut its e-commerce product in 2021 to refocus, tripled losses while tripling revenue, laid off ~6% of staff in 2023 having raised $187 M, and **became a lending platform — simple bookkeeping alone did not fund the business** **[PHASE8 VERIFIED — news, secondary]**. **The category owner spends roughly ₹2 to earn ₹1.** A manufacturing ERP will not out-simple them, does not need to — they do not do BOMs, work orders, job-work challans or ITC-04 — and should not envy their unit economics either. (iv) **UI is the cheapest thing on earth to copy** — §8.5's "any of hundreds of Frappe partners ships it in a quarter" applies with *more* force to simplicity than to job work. Where simplicity does pay it pays as a **sales-cycle and support-cost property**: "live in two weeks" converts; "simpler than ERPNext" invites a demo comparison we may lose, because ERPNext's advocates genuinely like its UI once learned. **The one simplicity commitment worth making, because it is the one that is evidenced:** the setup path hides infrastructure entirely — no Bench, no Docker, no Linux, no site/app concepts ever visible to a customer. *(PHASE7 §3)*

**Two disagreements, and the picks.** (i) PHASE5 §4.2 calls the customization overlay "the entire commercial argument". Commercially that is wrong — **ERPNext already ships the overlay and gives it away**, and PHASE5 §4.4 is the honest line (only 91 properties are changeable; real requests routinely fall outside and then need a fork). Build it; never sell on it. (ii) PHASE6 §4 calls Tally "probably the strongest single wedge available"; PHASE4 §8 ranks §143 #1. **Pick PHASE4** — PHASE6 undercuts itself two paragraphs later by conceding the formats are unresearched, and a one-time migration is exactly the value a services partner captures instead of a product. Tally decides whether they *can* switch; §143 decides whether they renew and whether we can charge above ERPNext-partner rates.

### 8.3 What we lead with

**"Material that leaves your factory and has to come back" — gate entry → delivery challan → job worker → ageing clock → ITC-04 → return or deemed supply.** W2 and W3 are the same physical document flow, so they ship as one story rather than two features. W1 is the mechanism underneath; W4 is the toll we pay at the door.

Why this: it is the only wedge whose absence is proven by grep across four codebases — **and the claim is the narrow one, stated the way §8.2 W2 states it. The free stack ships a *GST Job Work Stock Movement* report; what no product on the realistic Indian shortlist ships is a clock, a deemed-supply posting dated to dispatch, or the interest off it.** *"They have nothing"* is not our claim and must not be said in a room; **"a stock-movement report is not a liability engine"** is, and it survives the prospect opening ERPNext on the next screen. It attaches to a liability that is now **documented in a decided High Court judgment** (§8.6(c)) and that the owner *may* already fear — Q1a/Q1b test whether he does, and §8.6(c) records that no evidence says he does — rather than a productivity claim we would have to prove; it **cannot be bought as a service and cannot be bolted on**; and it naturally drags in the differentiators PHASE6 §3 calls "the daily operating reality of the target customer".

### 8.4 What we say in the first sales call

Rehearse these; the honest answer sells better than the hedge.

1. **"File my GSTR-1 directly?"** No. We prepare — portal-ready JSON, the 2B reconciliation, the mismatch list. Your CA files, in seven minutes instead of two days. Direct filing is v2; it needs a filing-grade GSP contract, an OTP per session and a fixed allow-listed IP, and we would rather ship it right than own a failed filing on the 11th.
2. **"Will it import my Tally data?"** Masters, balances and open items as of go-live. Not five years of voucher history. You go live on a clean opening balance sheet and keep Tally as a read-only archive — which is how every ERP migration in this country actually works. **The line that lands, because it is the maintainer's own:** ERPNext's co-founder closed its Tally-migration issue in January 2025 with *"We have removed Tally Migration in the latest version"* — the free option did not do this badly, it stopped doing it, and a community member shipping a replacement in mid-2026 wrote that migration *"has been a challenge for ERPNext implementers for years."* **Say the withdrawal; do not embellish it with "years of unresolved bugs", which the record does not support.** *(Also worth asking rather than assuming: what actually made you start looking? The only documented switch-away found in two research passes was triggered by remote access and giving the CA direct access — not by any feature. See §10 Q19.)*
3. **"Serial numbers on every piece?"** Batch and lot with full genealogy, yes; serial-level *valuation*, no. If you sell serialised machines under warranty, tell us now.
4. **"Fixed assets and depreciation?"** No — none of it, not even a single book. Your CA's schedule comes in as one journal entry, which is what happens today. v1.1. **Say the consequence, don't let them find it:** that means your fixed-asset register lives outside this system for the first year, so if your auditor is testing whether the *books* were maintained in software with an operative audit trail throughout the year, that register is not covered by ours. Same for statutory payroll return files until v1.5. We would rather you knew that in the first call than in the audit.
5. **"Can it schedule my shop floor?"** Job cards, operator time, a hard double-booking guard, reason-coded downtime — yes. Finite-capacity scheduling, no. Nobody in this price band ships real APS; the ones who claim to ship a Gantt chart you maintain by hand.
6. **"Multi-currency, we export."** Export and SEZ invoicing with all four regimes and LUT validity tracking, yes — and the **realised** gain or loss when the payment lands at a different rate, which is the one you see every month. Year-end **revaluation** of open balances, no; your CA books that. If you are export-*led*, we are not your best option this year.
7. **"Mobile app?"** No app. Gate, weighbridge and shop floor run in a browser on a cheap Android tablet, which is what survives a factory floor.
8. **"Can I add my own fields? Will an upgrade break them?"** Yes, and no — additive, versioned, audited, surviving upgrades by construction. What you cannot do is write scripts. Custom logic is a paid service item, because a customer script that breaks your GST posting is our problem forever.
9. **"Only one analytic dimension?"** Cost centre in v1; more in v1.2. We modelled it as a real column rather than a runtime custom field on sixty tables, which is why adding the second will be boring instead of dangerous.
10. **"Why pay when ERPNext is free?"** *(the one that decides the deal — and the one we cannot rehearse until Stage 0's financial model exists, because it is currently argued entirely on features against a competitor whose price is zero with our own number left blank)* ERPNext is free the way a puppy is free — **budget ₹1 lakh to ₹18.5 lakh for a partner to implement it depending on scope, with manufacturing-module packages clustering ₹2–5 lakh** *(three independent partner sources, each read on the partner's own published page **[PHASE8 VERIFIED]**: e.Soft — Core ₹1,50,000 / **Core + Manufacturing ₹2,20,000 for 70 hours** / + HRMS ₹3,10,000; Dvnks — ₹5 L / ₹12 L / ₹18.5 L+. **Every figure is a seller's asking price, not a paid invoice.** Revision 4 narrowed this to ₹75,000–₹5 L on one partner's blog and was too tight; revisions 1–3 said ₹3–8 lakh, which turns out to have sat inside the real range while having no source at all — both are corrected here)*, plus india-compliance, plus you now own every upgrade. **A partner's own words on what those budgets do, and cite it as a partner's claim about its rivals, never as measured data:** *"I've watched Indian businesses budget ₹4 lakhs and end up spending ₹12 lakhs because nobody mapped out where cost leakage actually happens"* — with a specific case going ₹3,36,000 quoted to ₹8,31,000 actual over 18 months **[PHASE8 VERIFIED verbatim, and self-interested: every source making this argument is a competing implementation partner]**. Then check four things: pending job-work challans **aged against the §143 deadline, with the deemed-supply posting and the interest**; gate entry and weighbridge; a Tally import; and PF, ESI and PT actually computing rupees rather than showing a labelled empty field. **Be precise on the first one, because they will check it and a loose claim costs the room: ERPNext ships a *GST Job Work Stock Movement* report. It is a stock-movement report, not a liability engine — it does not age a challan against the statutory clock, does not post a deemed supply dated to the original dispatch, and does not compute interest from that date; and its own team says on the record that ITC-04 filing does not exist and is only planned.** On the other three it has nothing — **we checked the source, not the brochure.** Two specifics we can name, dated and first-party: on ERPNext's own forum in March 2026, a thread titled *"The onboarding & deployment experience of Frappe/ERPNext is unnecessarily confusing for new users"*, answered the same day by a long-time contributor with *"I remember experiencing all of these pain points when I started my ERPNext journey"*; and `frappe/erpnext` issue **#47509** — a GST tax-computation defect on exempted items, reported with a reproduction, **closed as "not planned."** **[PHASE7 VERIFIED — both quotes confirmed on the live pages]**. That second one is §8.1's argument as an instance rather than an assertion: a free project cannot fund a compliance function. We do all four, we migrate you in two weeks, and when a notification changes a rate you get a signed data update from us instead of waiting for a community pull request. If the four don't matter to your business, use ERPNext — genuinely.
11. **"What if you disappear? GPL can't be taken away."** Fair. Source escrow in the contract, and a full data export in open formats with a documented schema available at any time, not on request — **the database dump and the attached files, both**, because an export without the signed IRP payloads and the gate photos is not a record (§3.11).
12. **"Do you keep my cost records?"** *(ask this one; don't wait to be asked)* Do you maintain cost records under s.148 — a CRA-1 set, product-wise material, employee, utilities, overheads and a reconciliation to the audited financials? If you do, v1 does not produce them: we cut co-product costing, standard costing and variance accounting, and we ship one analytic axis. You would keep that set where you keep it today. **[VERIFY] q87** decides whether this is a v1.5 scope item or a segment we are honest about not fully serving.
13. **"Does my stock value stay right?"** *(new in revision 5, and on the evidence it is a better line than any UI complaint)* ERPNext's back-dated revaluation reposting has been observed **stuck in queue for months across three major versions** — one user reverted to manual accounting *"in order to avoid accounting errors and losses"*, another reported **74,614 items queued and twelve months of incorrect item valuation on a 16-core server** **[PHASE8 VERIFIED — dated, first-party, on the vendor's own forum]**. Our repost is **bounded** (inside the open period only; outside it, a forward-dated adjustment), **observable** (queue depth, per-run progress), and carries a **stated completion SLA** with an alarm and a refusal to close a period over an outstanding repost. **Why this is the strongest of the thirteen:** it attacks the exact capability §8.1 concedes ERPNext does *well* — COGS as the delta in stock value — with the vendor's own users, dated, and it is a number the owner feels rather than a screen he has an opinion about. **It also creates an obligation: §3.4's repost must actually be bounded and observable, or this answer is a lie.** That obligation is written into §3.4 and Stage 3 for exactly this reason.

### 8.5 The honest case against the project

Stated as an opponent would state it.

**The parity bill is unpayable and due before the wedge is visible.** PHASE5 §1 measures 18–30 engineer-months for framework parity **before the first invoice screen exists** — and that is only the framework. PHASE2 §2 then lists 22 *Deep* modules. PHASE4 adds a statutory gate that is entirely table stakes and differentiates nothing. Clean-room makes it worse: we may read the rules but never take the code, so we pay full price for every one of ~110 business rules our competitor received free.

**Every named wedge is a feature, and its absence is market evidence against it.** §143 tracking is an ageing clock over a challan register — perhaps three to six engineer-weeks on a working job-work module. Gate entry is a form with a vehicle number. A weighbridge integration is a serial-port read. These do not exist in ERPNext or Odoo after fifteen and seven years, and the honest reading is not "nobody thought of it" but **"nobody paid enough for it to get built."** The moment we prove demand, any of hundreds of Frappe partners ships the same clock in a quarter, free, into a channel already trained. We will have spent three years buying a six-week lead.

**We are fighting a free, community-maintained incumbent on a compliance surface that changes quarterly**, funding a permanent compliance function against a competitor whose equivalent work is done by contributors for nothing — and our closed-source position means we cannot accept that community's help.

**The rational version of this plan does not require owning an ERP.** If the wedge is real, sell the wedge: a commercially licensed job-work compliance module that plugs into the ERP the customer already runs. Same revenue thesis, ~5% of the cost, revenue in months, and if the wedge turns out not to be worth paying for we learn it for a few hundred thousand rupees instead of after the ledger, the tax engine and the audit trail are built. **Choosing to build the whole ERP is choosing the most capital-intensive path to test the cheapest hypothesis.**

**And revision 5 has to record that this argument got *stronger*, because three vendors have now shipped exactly that product.** **Ecosire**, an Odoo-ecosystem vendor, sells a **$499** "Job Work Challan GST India" module built for this gap. **QuickManager** (Rohin Infotech) launched a "Job Work + ITC-04" module in 2026 with outward/inward challan tracking, half-yearly ITC-04 auto-prep and an **ageing alarm at 9 months, ahead of the 1-year deemed-sale threshold** — priced into a **₹1,499/month** tier. **Terra Insight** publishes sector job-work reconciliation content and sells reconciliation infrastructure. **[PHASE8 VERIFIED for Ecosire's price and page; QuickManager's pricing and launch date are vendor claims not re-verified; Terra Insight's worked rupee figures are the seller's own hypothetical with no named client and must never enter a financial model.]** **The market is showing us the cheap version of this bet, and telling us what it costs: $499 one-time, or ₹1,499/month.** Read it honestly in both directions. It is a **market signal PHASE7 did not have** — three independent teams betting resources on this niche is evidence of vendor conviction, and the one that ships states the statutory deadline *wrong* on its own sales page (§8.2 W2), which is consistent with the gap being real and unsolved. But **none of the three publishes a customer count, a named client or a renewal figure**, so it is conviction, not validated demand. **Nothing in either research pass supports building a full ERP to reach this.** *(Softening this paragraph would be the single easiest way for this document to start lying to its author.)*

**And distribution has no engineering answer — and revision 5 can now say *why*, structurally, rather than as an intuition about margins.** **An ERPNext partner's business model has no licence-margin line in it at all.** Frappe pays **no licence margin**; partners commit to monthly Frappe-product revenue tiers — **₹10,000 / ₹30,000 / ₹63,000 / ₹2,50,000 / ₹6,30,000** — which unlock **10% / 10% / 15% / 20% / 25% Frappe Cloud hosting discounts**, and require two to four full-time certified staff **[PHASE8 VERIFIED — frappe.io/partners/plans, vendor page, hard data]**. Their revenue is **services plus a hosting discount, and they keep 100% of the engagement.** So a closed-source product is **not competing for a share of an existing margin pool — it is asking a partner to accept a cost category that does not exist in their business today, one that reduces a pool they currently keep entirely.** Meanwhile the comparison they will actually run is against a number they already know: **Tally-ecosystem add-on resellers advertise 10–33% licence margin** (one advertising commission on renewals as well as first year) and **Marg advertises "upto 25% referral commission" with "zero investment"** **[PHASE8 VERIFIED — vendor pages]**. Our margin must beat that number **while also funding a compliance function ERPNext gets for free**. Indian manufacturing SMEs are reached through implementation partners who have ERPNext skills, an ERPNext-shaped services business, and a zero-cost base letting them keep the whole engagement fee. To move them we must fund a better margin than free — which we cannot do sub-scale — while asking them to stake a client relationship on a v1 whose defect list will look like PHASE2 §6's did in 2014. That catalogue of unbounded `while` loops, division-by-zero in depreciation, a `for/else` that throws on exact match, double-executed GL merges and silently dead code is not a list of their mistakes; it is a forecast of ours, minus fifteen years of production traffic to find them in.

**The three facts that would settle this argument, and which no report established:** what a job-work-heavy SME will pay per year for the §143 clock alone; whether a single implementation partner will carry an unknown closed-source ERP against a free one they already sell; and whether the Tally bridge is a two-month or a two-year build. **All three are answerable with field research and no code.** See §10 Q1–Q3.

**Two market-research passes changed nothing in this section, and the second one strengthened it.** Phase 7 found no demand evidence for the job-work wedge, no distribution answer, no willingness-to-pay data, and no evidence that the target customer wants to leave Tally. Phase 8 re-ran all four with working search and better tooling and returned: **still no buyer-stated price anywhere** (roughly 250 queries across both passes), **still no prevalence figure**, **still no first-hand account of how anyone tracks job work today**, **still zero primary buyer voice on cloud versus on-prem in either direction**, and **still nothing India-specific on SaaS churn or ACV** after ~10 dedicated searches — *a confirmed absence rather than a budget failure*. What it added is a **mechanism** for the partner problem (no licence-margin line at all) and **three vendors already selling the cheaper alternative this section proposes**. **The case against is stronger after two research passes than it was after zero, and Stage 0 must produce a written, signed answer to this section as a whole — not merely to Q1 and Q2 individually.** *(One thing the passes did not do: they never reached a single ₹5–50 Cr manufacturing owner describing what they pay for software or why they stay on Tally. Two independent attempts with different tooling. That is now a finding about the public web, not a search failure — and it is the whole argument for Stage 0 existing.)*

### 8.6 The three verdicts from the market research, stated where they cannot be missed

Read §0 of both reports before weighing any of this. **Phase 7:** nine of twelve passes died on search-budget exhaustion, eight graded partly-inflated, two unreliable, thirteen claims struck; overall confidence **LOW-MEDIUM**. **Phase 8**, which re-ran the same ground with working India-region search: five of eight passes **trustworthy**, three partly-inflated, **none unreliable**, its statutory section graded **trustworthy with high confidence**, and thirteen further claims struck by its own audit. Overall confidence **MEDIUM — HIGH on statute, and still LOW on demand and willingness-to-pay, which remain unevidenced.** With those discounts applied, three findings are load-bearing enough to change what this plan says. **All three verdicts survive the second pass; one of them changes shape.**

**(a) Radical simplicity is table-stakes hygiene, not a defensible wedge. Do not position on it.** The complaint evidence is real and comes from the wrong population; "simple" was never defined by any evidence gathered; the category is already owned by well-funded billing apps; and UI is the cheapest thing a competitor can copy. The defensible half — four screen families ERPNext's form engine architecturally cannot render — is already banked as **W3** and stays there. Full reasoning in §8.2. *What would overturn this: direct evidence from ₹5–50 Cr manufacturing owners that they abandoned or rejected an ERP because staff could not use it. That evidence does not exist and it is a Stage 0 question.* **Confirmed and sharpened by Phase 8, which finally identified *which role* needs simplicity — the 12th-pass accounts operator and the shop floor, not the graduate-educated owner — and *what* it means: Hinglish rather than Hindi, WhatsApp for delivery rather than entry, and no evidence for mobile in either direction. That specificity belongs in W3 and §3.13, and it is still not a headline.**

**(b) The narrow segment is correct, and now better justified than this plan previously argued.** ₹5–50 Cr is not intuition: it is where e-invoicing (≥ ₹5 Cr), half-yearly ITC-04 (> ₹5 Cr) and the 30-day IRP window (≥ ₹10 Cr) all bite while in-house finance capability is thinnest, inside a notified MSME band **[PHASE8 PRIMARY on the bands; the commencement date is [VERIFY] q93]**. **Independently corroborated in Phase 8 from an unrelated direction:** SIDBI–TransUnion CIBIL's *MSME Pulse* (June 2025), a credit bureau with no stake in the ERP market, finds the **₹10–50 Cr credit-exposure band** to be the second-largest share of a ₹35.2 lakh crore portfolio, with the **lowest balance-level delinquency of any segment**, and observes that *"manufacturing continues to dominate this segment"* **[PHASE8 VERIFIED — industry-body hard data; exposure is not turnover and the two must not be conflated]**. Phase 7 argued this band from notified compliance thresholds; a credit bureau arguing it from lending behaviour lands in the same place. Keep the boundary; narrow *further* for the first ten as a go-to-market choice; say "no" out loud to retail, process chemicals and export-led. Full argument in §4's preamble. **The binding constraint on widening later is the sales channel, not the code — and §10 Q2 has not tested it.**

**(c) The *exposure* is now proven. The *demand* is still entirely unevidenced. Those are two different claims and this document states both, precisely, in that order.**

**The exposure is proven, not inferred.** A **Madras High Court judgment of 08.06.2026** — *Tvl. Technocast Foundry v. State Tax Officer, Coimbatore*, **2026-VIL-582-MAD** — **upheld an extended-limitation demand under s.74** against a foundry for exactly this failure mode: no job-work records, no Form GST ITC-04, no proof of return within the prescribed period, so *"a deeming fiction applied treating the movement as supply, creating tax liability."* The matter was remanded only to reconsider revenue-neutrality; the deeming and the extended limitation stood. The job worker was a **sister concern** — an informal arrangement, which is precisely how SME job work runs. Separately, a taxpayer describes an **actual s.125 penalty levied at GST audit for ITC-04 non-filing**. **[PHASE8 PRIMARY / primary-user-voice]** **Revision 4's statement that no notice or penalty had ever been recorded is withdrawn: it was true of the evidence available then and it is false now.** It also settles the severity asymmetry §8.2 W2 flags — the half that bites is **s.74 extended limitation**, not the ₹25,000 s.125 filing penalty. Sell the liability.

**The demand side is still entirely unevidenced, and this is the half that decides the project.** **No prevalence figure** — nobody has counted what share of Indian discrete manufacturers do job work; the ASI and SIDBI sources that would answer it are image-based PDFs that could not be read, which is a tooling gap and the cheapest high-value research still outstanding. **No description of the tracking method** — not one first-hand account anywhere of whether an SME uses Excel, a challan book, or nothing. **No buyer-stated price** — across two research passes and roughly 250 queries, not one person has said what they would pay. **[PHASE8 UNEVIDENCED]** Every piece of pain found is **reactive**: discovered at audit, never anticipated — which weakly suggests it is not currently budgeted by anyone.

**So the verdict moves on one axis and not at all on the other.** *"Not disproven, also not evidenced"* becomes: **the risk is now proven real; the market for preventing it is still unproven.** The *defensibility* argument is untouched — only whoever owns the ledger can post a deemed supply that touches stock, GL and GST at once — and the *absence* argument narrows to its defensible form: **the free stack ships a stock-movement report; no product on the realistic Indian shortlist ships the clock, the deemed-supply posting or the interest** (§8.2 W2). **Absence in competitor code is still evidence of a gap and still not evidence of a market**, and three tiny vendors now building into that gap (§8.5) is vendor conviction, not validated demand. §10 Q1's falsification criterion stands exactly as written, and §8.5's "sell the module instead" stays open until Q1 returns — **better armed, because the interviewer can now open with a Coimbatore foundry that lost a s.74 demand in June 2026 rather than with a hypothetical.**

---

## 9. RISK REGISTER

Ranked by *expected damage if the risk lands*, not probability. **Retrofit** is the real decision driver — the cost of fixing it after first customer go-live.

### 9.1 THE DAY-ONE DECISIONS THAT CANNOT BE RETROFITTED

*Note on identifiers, because three lists in this document use a `D` prefix and they are unrelated: **§9.1's D1–D18 are day-one architectural decisions**, §5.4's D1–D10 are GSP procurement decisions, and §6.9's D1–D18 are deferred backlog items. Every in-text reference names its section.
