Renumbering them is a revision-4 tidy-up, not a correctness fix, and is recorded here so nobody assumes §6.9's D9 and §9.1's D9 are the same thing — they are fixed assets and the rule store respectively.*

**Eighteen** things are fatal and unretrofittable — revision 1 listed twelve under a heading that said six, the first review round added three, and the second added three more. Together they are roughly three engineer-months of design and schema work in month one, and a re-platform in month twelve.

| # | Decision | The call | Why it cannot wait |
|---|---|---|---|
| **D1** | **Audit trail** | Append-only, hash-chained, actor- and tenant-stamped, insert-snapshotted, one row per *event*, field-level deltas including child rows, captured by the persistence layer — **including the migrator and the import engine**. DB-level UPDATE/DELETE revoked. Mandatory, **no enable flag at all**; the configuration and retention locks stay. No cascade on delete. Partitioned from day one, retention read from the rule store | Retrofitting field-level deltas means rewriting every write path (PHASE4 §9.1). Rule 11(g) is a **sales blocker, not a feature** |
| **D2** | **Numbering** | Counter + `FOR UPDATE NOWAIT` + backoff, scoped (GSTIN, doctype, Indian FY), **allocated last before commit**, cancelled numbers kept forever, format enforced at series-design time, continuity auditor blocking IRN and GSTR-1, never reused for non-statutory documents | Retrofitting means renumbering live books — not a thing you can do |
| **D3** | **Back-dating policy** | Movement-only SLE + separate append-only valuation layer + bounded back-dating inside the open period only; outside it, a forward-dated adjustment document. Period close is a hard barrier. Reposts are governed, capped, permissioned, resumable documents | Forces or avoids an entire subsystem. PHASE2: "the single highest-leverage decision in the whole report" |
| **D4** | **Tenancy model** | Schema-per-tenant, ≤100 per shard, headers stripped at the edge as a security control, migration orchestrator as a first-class service, `tenant_id` on the spine anyway. **[BET] — see §3.7 for the dissent and the reversal condition** | Gates the audit-log schema (PHASE4 Q15), the GSP credential model, the egress-IP topology and gross margin permanently |
| **D5** | **Money representation** | Decimal/integer minor units end to end; a `Money` type carrying currency; **every tolerance derived from the precision system**; round per line; compute raw, round once, redistribute the delta; four amount legs on every GL line; party currency locked at first posting | Changing it after posted rows exist is a full ledger migration, and every wrong number already sits in a filed return |
| **D6** | **Document immutability** | Draft/submitted/cancelled/amended as a real state machine with declared transitions and per-field allow-on-submit, enforced by a DB trigger. Submitted documents never mutated. Reverse, never delete. Balances never stored | Audit, workflow, e-invoice, EWB, GST cancellation disclosure and the whole cancel-correctness story assume it |
| **D7** | **PostgreSQL only, real FKs, surrogate PKs, no polymorphic links, DDL only in the migrator** | — | Deletes a class of concurrency, ordering and SQL-rewriting bugs; the engine-divergence tax is permanent once paid |
| **D8** | **Ledger ordering by monotonic sequence**, `posting_date` a business attribute only | — | Deletes a permanent bug class for the cost of one column |
| **D9** | **Statutory rule store**, effective-dated, no silent fallback, rule-version stamp on every computed document | — | Otherwise every notification is a code release, back-dated documents compute with today's rules, and you cannot prove which rule produced a historical number |
| **D10** | **NOT NULL scoping columns, one predicate path** | — | Per-GSTIN scoping is required "from the first schema migration" (PHASE4 §9.3); blank-means-allow footguns cannot be removed once nullable columns exist |
| **D11** | **Overlay keyed with a `layer` dimension; permissions merge, never replace; inspector on day one; no runtime schema mutation for business actions** | — | PHASE5 §4.3's three flaws are explicitly "impossible to fix later"; the permission-overlay flaw is a live statutory exposure |
| **D12** | **No customer code execution** — bounded whitelisted-AST DSL only | — | Retrofitting a sandbox *out* of a product is impossible once customers depend on it; PHASE5 grades it "unbounded (permanent CVE liability)" |
| **D13** | **Stock serialization.** `pg_advisory_xact_lock` per `(item, location)`, acquired in sorted key order across the whole document, at the start of any SLE-writing transaction; `stock_balance` as a locked posting-path authority, not a cache (§3.4) | Moving average is a read-modify-write against the ledger tail. Two concurrent GRNs silently write a wrong average that never self-corrects, and V6's negative-stock check has the same race. Nothing errors. **Revision 1 had no concurrency design for this at all** — R13's only listed answer was "Postgres only", which does not touch it. As unretrofittable as numbering and more damaging when wrong |
| **D14** | **Personal-data classification and crypto-shredding** (§3.11): every entity classified `statutory_record` or `personal_data`; PII encrypted per subject; erasure = key destruction so the hash chain survives it; encryption at rest; India residency stamped | The product ingests biometric and payroll PII into an immutable, retention-locked, "never deleted" audit table. DPDP Act 2023 erasure and purpose-limitation obligations run the other way. Adding erasability to an immutable log after the fact is a re-model of the log — the exact §9.1 criterion. Absent from revision 1 entirely |
| **D15** | **Licensing and entitlement** — signed licence blob validated at boot and daily, read-only degradation on grace expiry, entitlement state in the control plane | A commercial closed-source product shipped on-prem with no licence key, entitlement check or tamper story has no enforcement mechanism, and adding one after customers are live is a boot-path change plus a renegotiation. Absent from revision 1 entirely |
| **D16** | **Valuation key** (§3.4): the moving average is per `(item, location)`; **batch is a tracking dimension, not a valuation dimension**; S5's expired-batch rule is a real write-down move rather than a valuation-time special case | Revision 2 declared `batch_id` on the SLE and never said whether value was keyed by it, while S5 implied yes and the lock and repost design implied no. Changing the key after valuation layers exist means recomputing every layer in the ledger — the same class of cost as changing the money scale, and it was in neither revision's day-one list |
| **D17** | **`document_date` separate from `posting_date`** on the document spine and on `gl_entry`, with `gst_report_period` stamped at post time; plus one **timezone** policy — UTC storage, business dates derived in the tenant's timezone | Revision 2 had one date and three features that need two: the IRN payload, EWB validity, the GST return period and the §143 supply date. Retrofitting means a ledger backfill plus a re-run of every filed return. The timezone half is the same shape — a 06:00 IST punch-in booked to the previous day is a wage error, and fixing it later means restating attendance |
| **D18** | **Object storage per tenant**, tenant-scoped prefix, included in the export command; **personal-data reads audited**; every artefact retained for the rule-store retention period | §3.7 sells `pg_dump -n t_x` as a procurement asset and it does not contain the signed IRP payloads, the QR images, the gate photos or the generated PDFs. A per-tenant export story that omits half the record is a claim we would have to withdraw in front of a customer, and adding a blob boundary after tenants share one bucket is a data migration |

### 9.2 Master ranking

| # | Risk | Damage | Retrofit | Category |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | Beautiful core that cannot produce a compliant invoice | **Fatal** — unsellable at any quality level | n/a (it *is* the product) | PROJECT |
| R2 | Audit trail not in the storage layer on day one | **Fatal** — qualified audit report at the customer | Rewrite persistence layer | TECH/COMPLIANCE |
| R3 | Numbering wrong (duplicate, gap, wrong scope) | **Fatal** — GST filing failure | Impossible cleanly | TECH/COMPLIANCE |
| R4 | Float money / no explicit precision model | **Fatal-silent** — wrong numbers in filed returns | Full data + code migration | TECHNICAL |
| R5 | Immutability + docstatus not day-one | **Fatal-structural** | Re-model every entity | TECHNICAL |
| R6 | Tenancy decided late | **Fatal-structural** — gates R2, GSP credentials, egress IP, margin | Re-platform | TECH/PROJECT |
| R7 | Back-dating without a valuation architecture that tolerates it | **Severe** — the repost subsystem, forever | Multi-quarter | TECHNICAL |
| R8 | Statutory rates as constants | **Severe** — every notification is a release | Re-model + backfill | TECH/COMPLIANCE |
| R9 | Compliance drift — the [VERIFY] problem | **Severe, recurring** — silently wrong returns, liability | Process, not code | COMPLIANCE |
| R10 | Adoption blockers unbuilt (opening balances, Tally, attendance→wages) | **Severe** — no first customer regardless of quality | Feature-scope | PROJECT |
| R11 | GSTIN/company scoping not on every query from migration #1 | **Severe** | Every query + index | TECH/COMPLIANCE |
| R12 | Overlay without layer/provenance → schema drift | **Severe commercial** — one branch per customer | Re-model overlay | TECH/PROJECT |
| R13 | Concurrency + multi-DB portability | **Severe** — corrupt valuation, engine-divergent results. **Answered by D13, not by D7** — revision 1 listed "Postgres only" as the mitigation for a race that the choice of database does not address | Rewrite locking paths | TECHNICAL |
| R24 | Estimate unsound: a 3.3× multiple of a self-disclaimed prior, over an entity count that was itself ~2× understated | **Severe** — a funding decision on a fiction; the visible symptom is Stage 4 slipping and GA landing at month 14 | Re-derive (§7.6) | PROJECT |
| R25 | Personal data vs immutable audit trail (DPDP Act) | **Severe if realised** — a statutory obligation the architecture actively contradicts | Re-model the log | COMPLIANCE |
| R26 | Statutory data has no source: the rule store errors on a missing rule and nothing populates it | **Fatal** — the system cannot post an invoice on day one | Buy or build the dataset (§3.9) | COMPLIANCE/PROJECT |
| R27 | Post-filing correction has no lawful route; cancel silently rewrites a filed period | **Severe** — books diverge from filed returns from month two | Amendment tables + filed-period lock (§3.3a) | COMPLIANCE |
| R14 | Scope: 15 years of accumulation vs our team | **Severe** — 18 months of engine, zero invoices | Sequencing discipline | PROJECT |
| R15 | Datetime as the ledger ordering key | **Major** — a permanent bug class | Add column + reprocess | TECHNICAL |
| R16 | Recompute storms / O(history) validation | **Major** — degrades as the customer succeeds | Rewrite hot paths | TECHNICAL |
| R17 | Multi-company / multi-currency posting correctness | **Major** — phantom FX gain, unsegregatable books | Ledger backfill | TECHNICAL |
| R18 | Government-API calls inline in request handlers | **Major** — lost IRNs, duplicate submissions | Extract service | TECH/COMPLIANCE |
| R19 | Permission surface breached | **Severe if realised** (cross-tenant leak) | External review | TECHNICAL |
| R20 | Settings that change posting math, no rule stamp | **Major** — cannot reproduce or defend a number | Ledger schema change | TECHNICAL |
| R21 | Migration orchestrator missing at tenant scale | **Major** — you stop upgrading | 3–4 eng-months | PROJECT |
| R22 | Customer-authored code sandbox | **Severe if shipped** — permanent CVE liability | Don't ship it | TECHNICAL |
| R28 | **Clean-room posture asserted, not operationalised** — no two-team split, no provenance log per rule, no legal review budgeted, no policy for engineers who have worked on ERPNext, while §6 transcribes exact formulas and key tuples out of GPL source | **Fatal if realised** — it ends the company rather than delaying it, and it is the one risk with no engineering remedy after the fact | Protocol + counsel, before first customer (§3.12) | LEGAL/PROJECT |
| R29 | **No correctness oracle** — one acceptance suite against 79 clean-room rules, while the plan's own §8.5 forecasts a defect list like PHASE2 §6's "minus fifteen years of production traffic to find them in" | **Severe** — silent wrong numbers found by customers and CAs, which is the churn mechanism §6.1 is entirely about | Differential harness + golden fixtures + property tests (§3.12) | TECHNICAL |
| R30 | **No UI workstream** — 70–90 screens, four screen families that are the structural wedge (§8.1), zero design headcount and no stack decision | **Severe** — the thing the product wins on is the thing nobody is staffed to build | Name it and staff it (§3.13) | PROJECT |
| R23 | The ADAPT bet: extension-field demand unvalidated | **Moderate** — 2–3 eng-months built for nobody | Cheap to abandon | PROJECT |

### 9.3 The three that need more than a row

**R1 — a beautiful core that cannot produce a compliant invoice. This is the register's central risk.** The failure mode, concretely: months 0–8 go into the spine, the overlay, the permission engine and the inspector — all genuinely correct work, all listed in PHASE5 §8.2. Month 9 begins GST, and the team discovers that HSN must be independently editable on the line but *not copied on duplicate*; that PoS needs a stored fallback chain; that the number format constraint belongs at series-design time; that GSTR section must be stamped at post time; that the audit trail needs child-row deltas. Each is a foundation change. The engine is beautiful and the invoice does not exist. **Counter-measures, in force order:** (1) the compliance acceptance suite runs red in CI from month 1 (§7); (2) treat Stage 2 as immovable and take PHASE5 §8.4's explicit permission to skip the workflow engine, notifications, dashboards, report builder and the entire generic form renderer to get there — its own verdict is "if in month 4 the metadata layer is not carrying its weight, we still have a shippable product; if we build the engine first, we have neither"; (3) CA in the loop in month 1, not month 9; (4) schedule the four PHASE4 **[NOT IN CODE]** items explicitly, because no estimate derived from reading references includes them.

**R9 — the [VERIFY] problem is a process risk, not a code risk.** PHASE4's adversarial audit forced three corrections that define its shape: removal of every statutory citation that was a scout inference, demotion of every threshold to [VERIFY], and explicit [NOT IN CODE] marking of PF/ESI/PT rates, §143 deadlines and turnover applicability. **Mitigation has four parts, none of which is code:** a named compliance owner with a CA on retainer, delivered as signed data updates; **CA sign-off of the rule table as a dated release artefact**; a standing monthly notification-watch against CBIC/CBDT with each change landing as a dated rule row (success metric: *a rate change ships without a deploy*); and provenance discipline — every rule carries its notification, verification date, verifier and validity window. A rule with no provenance is a defect. The code half of the answer is R8, and it is what makes the process affordable.

**R14 — scope vs team.** Take the deferral lists **literally** and do not relitigate them one at a time in sprint planning; each is individually defensible, which is exactly how scope dies. *This was impossible to follow in revision 1*, because three features sat in the IN list and the deferred list simultaneously; **§4.3 is now the single authority and it wins over any other section.** Take the "do not build" list literally too (AG Grid not our own grid, Nunjucks not a visual builder, drizzle-kit not a hand-rolled differ, off-the-shelf OAuth, OpenTelemetry not an APM, polling not realtime). **Exploit the MIT licence deliberately** — PHASE5 §9 Q8 calls the copyable framework files "a genuine multi-month head start and the most actionable fact in this report", conditional on the provenance review — and note that the same sentence is why **R28** exists: a licence boundary you exploit is a licence boundary you have to be able to prove you respected, which is §3.12's protocol and not a paragraph of intent. And make **small dependency-free calculation cores the norm**: PHASE2 §5 notes `stock/valuation.py` is 267 lines with zero framework imports and is "the most portable file in the repo because it escaped the framework." Valuation, tax, depreciation, scheduling, MRP netting and the calendar are all pure functions with no I/O. For a small team this is the single highest-leverage structural habit. *Revision 1 closed with "localisation as a plugin from day one, not a folder" — a day-one architectural commitment asserted once, in a risk paragraph, with the plugin boundary defined nowhere and the overlay already carrying a `vertical_app` layer that depended on it.* **Struck.** §3.11 states the position: v1 is one monolith for one country; the only free concession is that statutory logic lives behind named exports in `packages/domain` and reads from the rule store. Revisit if Q2 says partners are the go-to-market.

### 9.4 Where the reports disagreed on risk, and the picks

| Disagreement | Positions | Pick |
|---|---|---|
| Metadata engine | PHASE2: don't. PHASE3: adopt with guardrails. PHASE5: ADAPT, thin overlay | **PHASE5** — they answered different questions (engine vs overlay), and Frappe's source shows they are separable. **PHASE2's warning survives as the boundary rule**, and PHASE5 §1.8 concedes its core point: statutory logic must be greppable. Residual risk logged as R23 |
| Valuation architecture | ERPNext denormalises valuation into the SLE; Odoo 19 removed the layer for recompute-on-read; PHASE3 Q1 recommends the pre-19 layer | **Append-only valuation layer.** Both shipped alternatives fail in opposite directions — one makes history un-editable without replay, the other makes reads unbounded |
| Numbering lock cost | PHASE6 §1.1 vs PHASE5 §2.6 | **Both** — keep the pessimistic counter, allocate last (§3.6) |
| `tenant_id` under isolated tenancy | PHASE5 §6 (no tenant column anywhere) vs §8.1 (tenant_id on every spine) | **Keep both.** The connection is the isolation boundary; the column is required by the audit log and fleet reconciliation and costs nothing |
| Multi-currency depth at v1 | PHASE2 §3.1 core invariants vs PHASE6 §7 deferral | **Schema now, features later.** Four legs, party currency lock and the return-rate guard are ledger shape; FX treatment is a feature |
| PHASE3's two **[unsettled]** items (AVCO reset under partial returns spanning a reset; the `mrp.unbuild` TODO) | Explicitly unresolved | **Copy neither.** Design the negative-stock case explicitly (V6) and write the tests before the implementation |
| Payroll scope | PHASE4 §1.1 (G14 unconditional) vs PHASE4 §10 Q5 (ESI/PT to v2) | **Computation in v1, return files in v1.5** (§5.3) |
| Tally vs §143 as the lead wedge | PHASE6 §4 vs PHASE4 §8 | **§143 leads; Tally is the toll at the door** (§8.2) |

## 10. OPEN QUESTIONS FOR THE OWNER

Consolidated across all six reports, deduplicated, ranked by how much work each unblocks.

**Scheduling, corrected.** Revision 1 said "Q1–Q4 should be answered before **Stage 1 ends**" — i.e. during two months of a full team building the foundation, which is the most expensive possible time to learn the project should not exist. **Q1, Q2, Q3, Q7, Q8, Q15, Q17, Q19 and Q20 are now a hard gate at the end of Stage 0** (§7), answered by two people in three to four weeks with no engineers, with a written go/no-go. Q19 and Q20 are new in revision 4 and are asked in the same ten conversations as Q1 — they cost nothing extra and Q19 may be the more fundamental of the two. **Q21 (WhatsApp) is new in revision 5 and rides in the same conversation; it is a scoping question, not a gate — it cannot stop the project and it is listed here so it is not asked separately later.** **Q19 now carries its own falsification criterion alongside Q1's**, so the ten interviews return two written go/no-go answers rather than one. Q4, Q5, Q6 and Q18 are answered during Stage 0 in parallel; Q4 and Q6 block Stage 2 and Q18 blocks the first customer. Q9 gates the overlay, which has moved to Stage 2 for that reason.

**What "stops the project" means for Q1, stated as a rule rather than a hope.** The Stage 0 table says the number must "support a product company", which is not a decision rule. It becomes one the moment the financial model exists: **the go/no-go compares the interviewed willingness-to-pay against the ACV the model requires, and if the interviews do not clear it, the decision is §8.5's — build the standalone job-work module against the ERP they already run.** The threshold is written into the model at Stage 0 and not revised afterwards to fit the answer.

| # | Question | What it blocks | How to answer |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Q1** | **Rewritten in revision 4 — three questions and a failure condition, because the old form priced the wrong half.** **Q1a:** do you send material out for job work, and how much is out right now? *(prevalence — completely unmeasured)* **Q1b:** how do you track it today, and has a challan ever aged past a year? *(pain — and whether the felt pain is the periodic filing or the continuous visibility; these are different products with different buyers)* **Q1c:** if a system told you today that ₹X of material has been out for 11 months and is about to become a taxable supply with interest running from the dispatch date, what is that worth per year? *(price the liability, not the clock)* **Opener added in revision 5, because Q1c is no longer hypothetical:** *"In June 2026 the Madras High Court upheld a s.74 extended-limitation demand against a Coimbatore foundry — no job-work records, no ITC-04, no proof the goods came back in time, so the department treated the dispatch as a supply. The job worker was a sister concern."* **[PHASE8 PRIMARY — 2026-VIL-582-MAD, 08.06.2026]** Lead with the case, then ask the question. **Also ask to *see* how they track it today** — no first-hand description of the method exists anywhere in either research pass, and a photograph of a challan book is worth more than an answer. | The entire commercial thesis, and the choice between "build the ERP" and "sell the module" (§8.5). *Why the old question was wrong: "what will you pay for the §143 clock alone" priced the **commoditisable** half — the ageing report §8.2 itself concedes any Frappe partner ships in a quarter — asked about a **periodic filing** whose codified penalty is capped at ₹25,000, and had **no failure condition**, which makes it un-answerable as a gate* | 10 field interviews. No code. **Falsification, written down before the interviews and not revised afterwards to fit the answer: if fewer than 6 of 10 can name, unprompted, a challan currently outstanding beyond six months, the pain is theoretical and the decision escalates to §8.5 — sell the module, not the ERP.** This is still the cheapest way to be wrong about the biggest decision |

Silver subscription ₹750/mo · ₹8,100/12mo (list ₹9,000), Gold ₹2,250/mo · ₹24,300/12mo (list ₹27,000); **TSS renewal Silver ₹4,500/yr, Gold ₹13,500/yr — upgraded from [AGGREGATOR] to verified on tallysolutions.com/renew-tss** (*the separately-quoted TallyPrime Server TSS figure remains struck and must not be conflated: different SKU, unverified*). **BUSY Magic** per 360 days: Start ₹5,000 · Smart ₹8,000 · Power ₹12,000 · **Power+ ₹50,000**; legacy perpetual ₹11,000–₹22,000. **Marg** one-time ₹5,550–₹26,000 plus **ARC at 34.4%–38.7% of licence**. **Zoho Books India** ₹749–₹7,999 per org/month annual (₹899–₹9,599 monthly), free below ₹25 L turnover. **Odoo India** ₹580–₹1,140 per user/month. **Frappe Cloud from ₹410/mo** (shared) and **₹3,600/mo** (dedicated). **ERPNext implementation ₹1 L–₹18.5 L**, clustering ₹2–5 L for a manufacturing rollout (three partner sources — §8.4 answer 10). *Withdrawn from revision 4: myBillBook "from ₹199/month" — the vendor's page has no such tier (Diamond ₹291/mo annual is the entry point).* **Note that Tally now sells subscription alongside perpetual: "Indian SMEs won't buy recurring" is not a valid premise — the incumbent has already trained the market on monthly, quarterly and annual payment.** These are **competitors' prices, not evidence of willingness to pay for this product**; It is the only risk in this register with no engineering remedy after it lands | Named owner + a booked review before first customer (§3.12) |

A prospect content with Tally + Excel does not buy a manufacturing ERP no matter how good the §143 clock is. **Amended in revision 5 — ask about the *trigger*, not the grievance:** *"and what specifically happened that made you start looking?"* Phase 8 finally found Tally complaints (LAN-only multi-user, remote access costing extra, UI bloat versus the old DOS product, no automation, manual bank and GSTR-2B reconciliation, no ITC-04 export) — **but almost all of it is unreproducible search-cache from accountants and CAs, none of it from a manufacturer, and it is grievance, not trigger.** The one documented switch-away found in two passes was triggered by **remote/multi-device access, giving the CA direct limited access instead of emailing data files, and direct bank-account linkage** — *none of them a manufacturing feature, and none of them a compliance feature.* **If the trigger is access and collaboration rather than capability, §8.3's entire lead story is aimed at the wrong event** | 10 interviews, same conversation as Q1. **This may outrank Q1: it is cheaper and more fundamental, and published sources contain marketing, not grievance.** **Second falsification criterion, written down before the interviews alongside Q1's: if 8 of 10 name a trigger unrelated to manufacturing or compliance capability, §8.3's lead story is rewritten before Stage 1 begins.** |
| **Q20** | **Is on-premise a requirement or an inherited habit — and why: data control, internet reliability, cost, or precedent from Tally?** | §4.1F's self-host build, its packaging, backup/retention, signed-bundle delivery (§3.9) and a support surface on hardware we do not control — all carried on one unverified sentence from PHASE6 §6. **The reason matters more than the answer:** "data control" is negotiable, "our internet drops daily" is not. **Phase 8 confirms this is unanswerable from research: ~20 cloud-ERP vendor blogs assert cloud is the default and every one of them sells cloud ERP; zero primary buyer voice exists in either direction** | Stage 0 gate, asked in the same conversation as Q8 (dedicated-database isolation) — the two answers move together |
| **Q21** | **New in revision 5. Do you send documents, reminders and updates to customers, suppliers and job workers on WhatsApp today? What would you want the system to send, to whom, and should it send automatically or after you press a button?** | The one "simple" mechanism with hard data behind it: **3,323 of 4,218 digitally-active respondents in a 7,835-respondent India SME Forum/META survey use WhatsApp for day-to-day business interaction** **[PHASE8 VERIFIED]**, and the same survey finds **47.7% of enterprises find digital-platform setup and daily use challenging** with training criticised as *"generic, English-centric."* It scopes §4.1A's notification adapters and the §143 T-90/T-30/T-7 escalation channel — nothing more. **Scope discipline, stated in the question so the answer cannot inflate it: this is a notification and document-delivery channel, not a data-entry interface. Nothing in the evidence supports a WhatsApp bot for transactions. Do not let this become a chat-ERP** | 10 interviews, same conversation as Q1 and Q19. Costs nothing extra |

**The seven things this plan is least confident about, stated plainly:** the tenancy model (§3.7, decided against two of six decision agents and against PHASE5 §6.4, and now resting on one argument instead of three); the §143 statutory windows — **effectively closed as a worry in revision 5, and replaced by a narrower one.** The 1-year / 3-year windows, the moulds-dies-jigs-fixtures-tools carve-out and the deeming fiction are now read on **the CBIC statutory repository's own s.143 page [PHASE8 PRIMARY]**, not inferred from GPL competitor code — which was never the right place to look for Indian statute — and the exposure is confirmed by a decided Madras High Court judgment (§8.6(c)). Still **[VERIFY]** pending CA sign-off under q24–33. **What replaces it as the live worry is the Commissioner's extension proviso (q94): the window is extendable, so a hard-coded clock manufactures liability that does not exist, and the mechanics of applying for and recording an extension are the one part of §143 that got *harder* this revision;** every rate, threshold and date in §5 until a CA signs them; **where the statutory dataset comes from and what it costs** (§3.9); **the duration and the team size**, which §7.6 now refuses to state; **whether a clean-room build of ~110 transcribed business rules survives contact with counsel** (§3.12, R28) — the newest of the seven and the only one that is binary; and whether the wedge is worth what it costs to build (§8.5, Q1, Q15).

Everything else in this document is either derived from grep-verified source evidence or is a design choice we can defend on its own terms — with the qualification two review rounds earned: revision 1 said the same sentence while carrying seven unlabelled statutory claims, one fabricated citation and three features simultaneously in and out of scope; revision 2 said it while carrying four more unlabelled claims, four DDL fragments that would not have run, and three more in-and-out features. **The rate is falling, which is the only honest thing to claim.** The discipline is worth the sentence only if it is applied to the plan and not merely to the references, and only if the plan expects a third round to find something too.

## 11. WHAT CHANGED IN REVIEW

Two adversarial rounds, the same three reviewers each time — a skeptical CTO, an Indian CA, and the engineer who would build it. Round 1 attacked revision 1 and produced revision 2; round 2 attacked revision 2 and produced this one. Material corrections, grouped by what they would have cost.

### Round 1 — revision 1 → revision 2

**Things that would not have built at all**

| # | Defect | Fix | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The immutability trigger rejected every write of an IRN, ack date, signed QR, EWB number and Part-B update — so the async government call and the compliance argument were mutually exclusive and no e-invoice could be stored | `einvoice_artifact` / `ewb_artifact` side tables with append-only lifecycle; `no_copy` framing deleted | §3.3 |
| 2 | The audit trail's property 3 (trigger raises unconditionally on UPDATE) and property 5 (sealer *sets* `prev_hash`) could not both hold; `row_hash` could not cover a later `prev_hash`; `seq` gaps on rollback would alarm `audit:verify` | `prev_hash` computed inline at insert under a per-tenant chain-head advisory lock; `chain_seq` gapless and separate from `seq`; a real drop-the-chain fallback if Q5 says the digest suffices | §3.5 |
| 3 | No schema, no key structure, no resolver and no provenance columns for the statutory rule store — the #1 wedge, a day-one decision, and a Stage 1 deliverable, specified only as policy, with a cross-reference pointing at a section that does not exist | Full DDL, GiST exclusion on validity ranges, `resolve()` that throws, ruleset-snapshot stamping instead of a single rule id | §3.9 |
| 4 | No DDL and no concurrency design for the stock ledger, while four imported rules assumed ERPNext's one-sided per-location shape the two-sided design did not have | `stock_ledger_entry` + `stock_balance` DDL; one-sided rows paired by `move_id`; `pg_advisory_xact_lock` per `(item, location)` in sorted order — added as **D13** | §3.4, §9.1 |
| 5 | `Money` at fixed 4 dp could not hold the unrounded value T3 mandates, and `mul`/`div` semantics were undefined | `Dec` arbitrary-precision intermediate; one `commit()` rounding boundary; `packages/money` written first | §3.1 |
| 6 | No tax data model at all behind fifteen tax rules; repartition, the mechanism for both RCM and ineligible ITC, had no shape | `tax_template` / `tax_repartition_line` / `item_tax_template` / per-line stored tax result, before Stage 2 | §3.10 |

**Things that would have shipped wrong and been found by a customer or an auditor**

| # | Defect | Fix | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| 7 | Mid-year migration was impossible: no opening ordinal, so the blocking continuity auditor failed forever on externally-issued numbers — and Stage 4's own demo could not pass Stage 1's gate. Only one series per GSTIN per year was permitted | `start_ordinal`, declared `migrated_from/to`, `series_code` as a first-class field, auditor scoped forward | §3.6 |

**Overconfidence, corrected in place.** Seven statutory claims stated flat on points the scout marks [VERIFY] or [NOT IN CODE], plus one fabricated cross-reference ("six of eight Rule 11(g) criteria") and one citation to a repo no report examined (SeaRM). All are tabled in §5.6. Fourteen new statutory questions (q71–q84) were added to §5.5 — every one a reviewer's assertion about Indian law that this document accommodates in design but does not adopt as fact.

**Nothing was rejected outright in round 1.** Two fixes were adopted in a different form than proposed: the statement-level balance trigger (unavailable in Postgres — §3.4), and "pull J5 into v1 *or* rewrite the wedge" (both done — §5.3).

### Round 2 — revision 2 → revision 3

Round 1 fixed what the plan *said*. Round 2 mostly fixed what the plan *would have done*: four SQL fragments that would not have run, three subsystems named in a wedge and absent from every stage, and a migration that moved money and left every statutory cumulative at zero.

**Things that would not have built or run as written**

| # | Defect | Fix | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| 39 | `audit_log` declared `seq … primary key` on a table `partition by range (occurred_at)` — invalid Postgres; the migration would not apply. `txid_current()` wraps and forces XID assignment | `primary key (occurred_at, seq)`; `pg_current_xact_id()` returning `xid8`; unique index on `chain_seq`, which is what `audit:verify` walks | §3.5 |
| 40 | The numbering retry loop retried `FOR UPDATE NOWAIT` inside a transaction the first `lock_not_available` had already aborted — every retry would have failed with `25P02`. And "the lock is held for microseconds" is wrong about Postgres: a row lock is held to COMMIT | `SAVEPOINT` / `ROLLBACK TO` per attempt; the hold restated honestly as commit-latency-bounded, with a stated throughput budget and a separate bulk path for imports | §3.6, §3.5 |
| 41 | **No unique index anywhere on `(registration_id, doc_type, doc_no)`** — gaplessness was enforced by a nightly auditor and uniqueness by nothing | Partial unique index on every document table carrying a statutory series | §3.6 |
| 42 | The deferred balance trigger does not fire at savepoint release, so §3.8's savepoint-per-batch import would surface an imbalanced batch only at COMMIT and roll back every earlier batch | Importers `SET CONSTRAINTS … IMMEDIATE` at each batch boundary so the assertion and the savepoint coincide | §3.4 |

| 45 | The overlay's merged descriptor — build-time registry + tenant extension rows + layered overrides — was read by five subsystems and composed by none | Resolution order, cache key, and the invalidation event (an `overlay_version` bump on the existing Redis channel; no wall-clock TTL) | §3.2 |

**Refused, and why — because a rejected critique that is silently dropped is indistinguishable from one that was missed.**

| Reviewer's fix | Disposition | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Statement-level balance trigger with a transition table | Adopted differently | Postgres `CREATE CONSTRAINT TRIGGER` supports only `FOR EACH ROW`. Deduped by `voucher_id` in a transaction-local set instead (§3.4) |
| A minimal fixed-asset register in v1 | Refused | The argument — a statutory register outside the audited system weakens the Rule 11(g) claim — is correct, and D9 is still a multi-week module. §4.3's discipline is that a deferral is deferred; what changes is that §8.4 answer 4 now says the consequence out loud |
| Websockets / realtime for the job-card timer | Refused | A timer is local state that posts on stop, not a live subscription. Polling stands (§3.13) |
| Bring cost records (CRA-1) into v1 scope | Refused for v1 | A real obligation and an unpriced one. It becomes a Stage 0 qualifying question (Q17) and a sales-call disclosure rather than silent scope |
| "Use 55–70 as the entity count" | Refused | Neither 40 nor 70 is defensible without the enumerated register, and §7.6's whole point is that a re-derivation replaces asserted counts. Both figures are recorded; the register decides (§3.2) |
| Move payroll computation to Stage 3 | Refused | Revision 2 already moved it further forward, to Stage 2, for the same reason the reviewer gave — it is blocked on CA data, not on the factory |
| Drop the per-row hash chain now | Not yet | Still gated on Q5, as revision 2 left it. The difference is that the fallback is now a real one-line change rather than a claim (§3.5) |

### What changed in Revision 4 — the Phase 7 market-research pass

Not an adversarial review round. A market-research pass (`PHASE7-MARKET-RESEARCH.md`) whose §8 carries seventeen numbered edits against this document. **All seventeen are dispositioned below; none was silently skipped.**

**Read this first, because it governs how much any of it is worth.** Nine of that report's twelve research passes terminated on search-budget exhaustion; its own fact-check graded eight *partly-inflated*, two *unreliable*, and struck thirteen claims as fabricated or untraceable. Its self-assessed confidence is **LOW-MEDIUM**. Nothing from it was imported at higher confidence than it holds there, nothing struck by its §0.2 appears anywhere in this document, and no statutory figure it contributed was promoted past **[VERIFY]** — the labelling convention is stated at the head of this plan. **The report confirmed nothing about demand**, and revision 4 records that rather than absorbing a market report as implied validation.

(The paraphrase "remove any assumption that Indian SMEs reject recurring pricing" was the merge instruction's wording, **not** E14's — recorded here because this document does not put words in a source's mouth) | Added as a **guard**, not a correction | No such assumption existed anywhere in revisions 1–3 — a grep for perpetual/subscription/recurring pricing language returns nothing in a pricing context. The note is recorded in Q15 so the premise is not introduced later during Stage 0 |

**E17 — recorded as standing, so they are not relitigated on the strength of a thin report.** §8.2 W2 ranked above W4 (nothing found on either side; §9.4's reasoning stands on its own terms) · §8.2 W3, the factory-floor UI (strengthened, not weakened) · J5 in v1 (confirmed; the interest-from-dispatch mechanic makes it more clearly the half a bolt-on cannot do) · §4.2's switchable-applicability decision with no computation engine (confirmed by the threshold history) · §7.6's refusal to state a timeline (confirmed — the research adds nothing that would let anyone state one, and the revenue side is as blank as the cost side) · GST filing OUT of v1, prepare only (untouched by evidence; and Zoho's own "Push to GSTN" is itself gated on plan tier and configured GSTN credentials, so the incumbent cloud player treats direct filing as conditional too).

**What revision 4 deliberately did not do.** It did not add a market-sizing section, a TAM, a competitor matrix beyond what §8.1 already argues, or any ACV/CAC/churn figure — because the research produced none, and a plan that invents them is worse than one that says so. It did not touch §3 architecture at all. Six question areas returned **zero** results and remain open with a fresh search budget: Tally's dealer channel and primary-source pricing; **BUSY and Marg**, which are what an Indian manufacturer actually shortlists and about which we know nothing; enterprise counts by turnover band and cluster geography; IMS, GST rate rationalisation and e-way-bill operational friction — all three of which touch v1 scope directly (§5 leaves them open); documented OCR/AI accuracy in Indian accounting tools; and vernacular/mobile/WhatsApp behaviour, whose absence is why this document defines "simple" only as W3's screen families and nothing more. **Re-run those before Stage 0's financial model is written.**


**Revision 4a — consistency pass.** A post-merge check found seven residual defects; all seven are fixed above. (1) §8.3 asserted the owner "already fears" the §143 liability, contradicting W2, §8.6(c) and Q1's own falsification criterion — restated as *may* fear, with the test named. (2) §1's Stage 0 gate list omitted Q19 and Q20, which §7 and §10 both treat as hard gates; the summary paragraph is what a reader acts on, so it now matches. (3) §4.1D called job work "the strongest renewal feature in the product" flatly — now qualified with the same unmeasured-demand caveat W2 carries. (4) The zero-infrastructure installer — the single evidenced simplicity commitment — was named in §8.2 but in no stage or scope list, violating this document's own rule that *every capability named in a wedge must appear in a stage*; it is now a §4.1F deliverable and a Stage 4 line item. (5) §11's reshaped-fix table quoted E14 with wording E14 does not contain; the actual text is restored, because a document whose whole Phase 7 discipline is about quotations matching their sources cannot paraphrase inside quote marks. (6) The Q15 price shelf was silently widened from PHASE7's ₹10 K–₹50 K to ₹27 K–₹115 K; the correction stands (it matches the anchors both documents list) but is now recorded as a deliberate correction rather than an unmarked import. (7) Two labelling slips: `[PHASE7 UNEVIDENCED]` was in use but undeclared — now defined at the head as the strongest warning label here, meaning *searched for and found nothing in either direction* — and the Vyapar/myBillBook figures were tagged VERIFIED when PHASE7 sources them to listing aggregators; both are downgraded to what their sources actually support.

---

### What changed in Revision 5 — the Phase 8 deep market-research pass

Not an adversarial review round either. A second, deeper market-research pass (`PHASE8-MARKET-RESEARCH-DEEP.md`) whose §10 carries **twenty numbered edits** against this document. **All twenty are dispositioned below. Two were declined and both refusals are recorded with reasons; nothing was silently skipped, and nothing was silently reshaped.**

**Read this first, because it changes how much of it is worth.** Unlike Phase 7, this report is a **materially better evidence base**: five of eight passes graded *trustworthy*, three partly-inflated, **none unreliable**, and its statutory section graded **trustworthy with high confidence — 13 of 14 cited URLs fetched directly, every verbatim quote confirmed on its `.gov.in` page**. Its own audit still struck thirteen further claims, and **none of those appears anywhere in this document** (§0.2 of that report; the labelling convention and the exclusions are stated at the head of this plan). Every Reddit quote it gathered is unreproducible search-engine cache and **none of it is cited here.** Its overall confidence is **MEDIUM; HIGH on statute; and still LOW on demand and willingness-to-pay** — which is the same place Phase 7 left the question that decides the project.

**Statutory corrections — the four that change code, not wording**

| # | Change | Where | Source edit |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The §143 window is Commissioner-extendable** — a further 1 year (inputs) / 2 years (capital goods) on sufficient cause, s.143 second proviso, read on the CBIC statute page. The challan carries `extension_ref` / `extension_order_date` / `extended_to_date`, and the deemed-supply trigger evaluates against the extended date where one exists. **A correctness requirement, not a nicety: a hard-coded 365-day clock manufactures liability the customer does not owe, and posts an invoice for it** | §6.7 M8, §5.5 q24–33 + new **q94**, §10 | **3** |
| 2 | **Interstate job work requires an e-way bill at ANY consignment value** — Rule 138 third proviso, *"irrespective of the value of the consignment"*, read on the CBIC rule text. Upgraded from "understood requirement" to primary; the ₹50,000 threshold and this carve-out are both rule-store rows; **q82 narrows to the unregistered-job-worker case only** | §5.1 N14, §6.7 M8, §5.5 | **4** |
| 3 | **IMS turned GSTR-2B into a recomputable draft fronted by a per-invoice state machine** — Accept / Reject / Pending, with **"No Action" deemed accepted** at 2B generation. The purchase side is a standing per-invoice action worklist feeding a recomputable 2B, **not a monthly snapshot — a snapshot model is wrong at the data-model level, not the UX level**, and `2b_action` is a column from migration #1. New **q95** on the GSP API surface and on what recomputation does to a signed-off reconciliation | §4.1C, §5.5 | **5** |
| 4 | **The GST rate structure collapsed from 4 tiers to 2 rates plus a 40% de-merit rate on 22 September 2025.** Cited in §3.9 as **the single strongest justification for effective-dated rule storage in the entire evidence base** — not a threshold moving, the whole rate map, in one Council decision. Direct consequence recorded: **any rate table seeded from pre-September-2025 material is wrong today**, and the CA question changes from "what are the rates" to "give us the 22 Sep 2025 mapping and its transitional rules" | §3.9, §4.2, §5.5 | **6** |

**What revision 5 deliberately did not do.** It did not add a TAM, a market-sizing section or any ACV/CAC/churn number — Phase 8 searched specifically for India-manufacturing SaaS churn and ACV benchmarks and found **nothing India-specific**, which is a confirmed absence and not a licence to estimate. **It did not touch §3's architecture** except where a statutory fact forced a data-model change (IMS's per-invoice action state, the §143 extension columns, the rate-master re-sourcing note, and the repost-observability obligation). It did not readmit a single struck claim from either report's §0.2. And it did not soften §8.5. **Research gaps carried forward with a named instrument rather than a hope:** job-work **prevalence** among Indian manufacturers is still the single most valuable missing number and is answerable cheaply by a human opening the ASI PDF or querying MoSPI microdata — **do that before Stage 0's financial model is written**; the ~11 cached Reddit quotes need a logged-in browser to verify before any of them is repeated; G2, MouthShut and Capterra reviews remain unfetchable; and cluster associations, not 2015–18 survey PDFs, are the way to refresh §4's geography.
